# Backlog

The queue that answers **"what's next?"** — and the **only** status authority in
the repo. The sprint plans ([neural_terrain_renderer.md](roadmap/neural_terrain_renderer.md)
`ntr`, [architecture_cleanup.md](roadmap/architecture_cleanup.md) `clean`,
[graphics_fidelity.md](roadmap/graphics_fidelity.md) `gfx`) hold strategy and
rationale; they carry no parallel checkboxes to keep in sync. Driven by the
`steer` skill (`.claude/skills/steer/SKILL.md`).

**What gets a row:** work that **outlives the current session** — multi-step
items, anything handed back to the user, anything deliberately deferred. Work you
find and finish in the same change doesn't need one; the commit is the record.
Don't let a row become a second place to write the design.

**Status:** `next` (ready) · `wip` · `verify` · `blocked` (blocker in **Deps**) ·
`done` (date in Notes) · `later` (not yet pickable).

`verify` is the load-bearing one: **landed compile-clean but not runtime-verified.**
Agents can't run the game, so the row names what closes it — a headless probe
(`just screenshot` / `just preview` / `just ui-preview`, agent-servable) or a user
play session. Never `done` on "it compiles".

**IDs:** reuse a plan doc's own ID (`NTR-M*`, `CL-A`…`CL-G`, `F1`–`F9`, `W*`,
`TM*`, `C1`) when the item has one; otherwise mint `BL-YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ-slug`
from `date -u '+%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ'`. **Never allocate the next `BL-n`** — parallel
branches collide, and these IDs are cited from Rust source
(ADR-20260722T170714Z). `BL-1`–`BL-40` are frozen legacy IDs. Mark `done`, don't
delete; never renumber.

**Refs:** `ntr §N` · `clean §N` · `gfx §N` = the roadmap docs ·
`ADR-YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ-slug` = adr/ · `INC-YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ-slug` = incidents/
(plus frozen legacy `INC-NNNN`) · other docs by filename.

---

## Track 0 — Neural terrain × standard-path renderer (keystone · primary sprint · `ntr`)

Goal (`ntr §1`, ADR-20260723T142945Z): make Thalos look good — paired neural
terrain (terrain-diffusion fine-tune, Thalos/earth-like first) + a renderer on
Bevy's standard path, developed as one vertical slice in a **standalone probe
repo** (Earth-scale, `big_space`, clean-room), extracted back into Thalos at M5.
Probe milestone IDs `NTR-M0`–`NTR-M5` and fine-tune IDs `NTR-FT-*` are owned by
`ntr §3`/`§4`; the probe repo holds the detailed checklist, these rows track the
gates.

| NTR-X1 | **In-game Mira slice via the new path:** port the probe's tiles contract/mesher/streaming into `thalos_body_render::tiles` (per-body radius, `SurfaceQuery::sample_d` provider with real albedo/roughness, eye fed from `ViewAnchor` by a game driver); gate udlod's `try_spawn` per body behind `THALOS_TILE_RENDERER=1`; verify with the existing `mira-orbit`/`mira-surface`/`mira-eva` presets vs udlod baselines (BL-34/35's 1.7 m-eye signature is the recurrence test). Thalos body follows as NTR-X2 (needs ocean/veg/flatten re-coupling). **Slice 1 landed + capture-verified 2026-07-24** (e777281 + a9f1883): mira-orbit/surface/eva render through the standard path; **BL-34 recurrence test PASSED** (eva's 1.7 m grazing eye renders clean — the udlod defect class does not reproduce). Live findings fixed same-day: udlod gate narrowed to per-body (Thalos keeps udlod + BodySky + ocean while Mira runs tiles; boot race cleaned via TerrainRebuildRequest), and cold-stream 2m11s → **21.4 s** by adopting udlod's pool discipline (dedicated synthesis pool — the port had reintroduced the Avian-starvation hitch — + bounded rayon rows; telemetry: 30.6 ms/tile mean). **Slice-2 diagnosis 2026-07-24 (e45b7e4 + INC-20260723T232652Z):** the user's "flat/painted/strange shading" was the **impostor billboard painting over the tiles** — the terrain↔impostor swap keyed on udlod residency only; fixed via `TileTerrainRoot::coverage_ready` latch feeding `sync_body_render_lod` (capture-verified: handoff fires, red-tint discriminator proved the tiles render). Second find, pre-existing: `MostLegible` landmark ranking max-selected an analytic relief estimate → `mira-rim` hovered over a plain; landmarks now rank by relief **measured from the surface** at registry load. **Resolved 2026-07-24 (INC-20260724T001023Z):** the "no relief anywhere" was NOT package data — the codebase had **two body-fixed frames** (surface/tidal-lock vs raw ephemeris), coinciding on planets, 132° apart on the tidally-locked Mira; `ViewAnchor` + the capture framings used the ephemeris frame, so the refined tile shell rendered rotated and the camera hovered over coarse far-side tiles. Fixed with one frame authority (`transforms::surface_orientation_authored`; ViewAnchor/sun_shadow/screenshot framings converted). **`mira-rim` now frames a real 17 km/5.9 km crater through the tile path** (capture-verified). **User fly-through 2026-07-24 ("looks good, but super low-res base + swirly fine detail")** — both were the package's true content, previously veiled by udlod's masking noise: (a) craters ≥ 1.5 km were rasterized into the ~2.7 km/texel cubemap → square texel bumps + staircase rims; **fixed by raising `cubemap_bake_threshold_m` above the max crater radius** (mira.ron + rebake): every crater now analytic/round at any LOD, cubemap keeps only macro shape, cost +10 % (34.5 ms/tile), capture-verified (A/B in `artifacts/visual/runs/ntr-x1-threshold-ab/`) — analytic features now read CAD-clean, which is the known ceiling of the EOL producer that diffusion content (NTR-FT-1) replaces; (b) the swirl = the regolith fBM + coarse albedo blobs under flat ambient — largely a shading/ambient read, improved by the **Hapke material, now WIRED** (`tiles::material::TileTerrainMaterial` = ExtendedMaterial + `assets/shaders/tile_terrain.wgsl`: Hapke branch for airless bodies off Bevy's own lights — sum ALL directional lights, moonlight owns slot 0 — + `thalos::shadow` receive fanned by `apply_craft_shadow`; capture-verified at sane brightness). **User finding 2026-07-24 ("larger tiles remain too long"):** the split-case despawn waited for the FULL desired leaf set (up to 4^n descendants) before releasing a coarse parent — refinement read as a late swap instead of gradual sharpening; fixed with progressive replacement (`covered_by_resident`: a parent releases the moment its footprint is covered by resident descendants at any level; merge case unchanged). **Fly-through rounds 2–3: black tiles during motion persisted through a 0.15 s despawn grace** (which falsifies pure upload-latency ~2-3 frames as the cause). Round-3 architecture per user direction — decouple despawn timing from visual correctness: (a) **per-level depth-biased materials** (`LEVEL_DEPTH_BIAS_STEP`, real `DepthBiasState.constant` via `StandardMaterial::depth_bias`) so a refined tile always wins the depth test where it overlaps a lingering coarser one; (b) **lax retirement** (0.5 s AND 20 frames of continuous removability); (c) **coverage-invariant audit** every 2 s (warn "LOGICALLY uncovered"). **Round 4: audit fired in the user's flight → holes were LOGICAL. Root cause found via a descent-simulation test** (production landing rate + low sweep + re-dive, invariant asserted per tick): the despawn split-case called `covered_by_resident` on the tile itself — still in the resident map — so every stale parent **self-certified coverage**, retired, and despawned while its children were still pending (INC-20260724T013637Z). Fixed (descend from children, never self); the simulation is a permanent regression gate. **Round 5 (user: gaps gone, but multi-level poke-through returned):** only desired tiles were ever generated, so a fast approach (selection skipping levels) left a stale parent waiting for its full 4^gap leaf set while its coarse geometry poked through refined cliffs. Fixed with **bridge tiles** (`bridge_requests`: children of any stale resident ancestor whose gap >1 level are requested even though undesired) — replacement cascades level-by-level, visible overlap ≤1 level (within the depth bias' reach), pyramid overhead ≤ ~1/3; sim extended with a 13 s settle phase asserting residents converge exactly onto the desired set. Landed to verify by fly-through. **Remaining slice-2 items:** relief-aware refinement + LOD normal seams (blocky facets on steep walls — ntr §7 screen-space-error row), view-direction admission priority (deprioritize behind-camera tiles; TileEye would need the camera forward), Hapke↔impostor brightness calibration at the swap, conditioned close-detail band (Rclient-class, package-roughness-conditioned), tile-native package provider (per-point decode tax), GPU height mirror | wip | L | **user:** `THALOS_TILE_RENDERER=1 just game mira` — expect ~20 s surface load, Mira on tiles, Thalos normal (atmosphere/ocean back) | ntr §6 steps 1–3 |

| NTR-X2c | **Conditioning probe bench** (user 2026-07-29, discussing planet-wide neural terrain: "the goal is creating a good MVP, with what we have mostly" + biome/landform diversity — mesas, Grand-Canyon-esque). Offline Python bench in the terrain-diffusion checkout (`bench_conditioning.py`, notes in `BENCH_NOTES.md`), no game build: a **province atlas** (plateau/shield/basin/escarpment/range/coastal/… , 18 recipes) driven through `set_custom_conditioning_import`, one 184 km 90 m window per case, hillshade + 1:1 crop + contact sheet + metrics, ~4.5 min/matrix on the 4070 Ti. Answers "which conditioning produces which landform family" empirically instead of by argument, and gates NTR-X3 — conditioning is upstream of every band, so an overnight planet bake on today's chart bakes in whatever the chart gets wrong. Built-in duplicate-case bleed control and a `cond_corr`/`elev_bias_m` instrument that tells *produced* from *correct*. **Landed 2026-07-29 with first results.** Findings: (1) **`cond_snr` is a conditioning NOISE level — lower is stronger** (`cos(atan(s))·cond + sin(atan(s))·noise`); the shipped Thalos `0.35,0.15,…` was already near-optimal and the "our climate is being ignored" hypothesis is **falsified** — measured monotone curve, corr +0.54→+0.99 as s goes 24→0.03; 0.1 buys near-exact adherence. (2) Authored provinces **do** yield distinct landform families, incl. rain-shadow asymmetry from a precipitation gradient alone — the "it's all mountains" impression is our uniform chart, not the model. (3) **Canyon country is reachable today** via humid plateau conditioning (deeply incised branching network, steep walls, flat uplands). (4) **Arid dissected plateau — mesas/buttes/badlands — is NOT reachable by conditioning** at any strength/seasonality tested; the model learned Earth's precipitation↔dissection correlation and an authored trunk trench gets no tributaries. Workaround: wet-conditioned geometry + canonical arid landcover (colouring is already a separate authority). Bench traps recorded: province blocks must exceed the 64-cell coarse tile (32 gave 4× relief scatter), and `finalize_synthetic_map` is skipped on the import path so imports must carry post-finalize values. **30 m check done 2026-07-29** (`--model 30m`, tag `mesa-30m`): the synthetic conditioning prior is identical across both models so recipes carry over unchanged; adherence equally good (r +0.97/+0.98). The 30 m model is **materially better for arid structural terrain** — `canyon_incised` flat 80 %→44 % and slope 1.2°→3.7°, `escarpment` flat 96 %→70 %, gaining a real cliff line with erosional fluting and lateral spurs — but **does not overturn the mesa verdict** (no detached buttes; `plateau_arid` still a smooth platform). Verdict: 30 m is a **showcase-window** tool (2048² = 61 km; planet-wide at 30 m would be 667k×333k px), the planet band stays on the 90 m latent stage. Follow-up: NTR-X2d (conditioning generator rewrite) | done | M | NTR-X2a | ntr §4.3 · §4.4 |
| NTR-X2d | **Conditioning generator rewrite** (follows NTR-X2c). `export_thalos_macro.rs` today emits 3 of 5 channels from one fractal relief field + a latitude temperature ramp + one moisture noise, and applies only one of `finalize_synthetic_map`'s four transforms. Rewrite to: all five channels (BIO4 temp seasonality + BIO15 precip CV are currently Perlin fallback); a **landform-province field** (plain / range / plateau / basin / shield) shaping relief character rather than one amplitude everywhere — this is where landform diversity is actually authored; climate with continentality + orographic rain shadow so aridity correlates with lee-of-range; and the missing finalize transforms (20 °C contrast stretch, temp-sd baseline, precip-CV damping). Bench-verified per province before it feeds a bake. Also feeds ntr §4.4 (learned climate → landcover). Gates NTR-X3. **Landed 2026-07-29:** new `thalos_terrain::macro_conditioning` (`ConditioningChart`) owns the derivation — the example is now I/O + a verification report, and `ProceduralSurface::macro_signals` is the one seam it reads (height stays canonical; the chart never invents continents, so the runway site cannot move). All five channels emitted (ch2 BIO4 + ch4 BIO15 were Perlin fallback before); all four `finalize` transforms reproduced, since the import path bypasses them; province classification (ocean/shelf/plain/shield/plateau/range/basin) from **measured quantiles** of this body's own orogeny/relief, not constants; continentality via wrapped chamfer coast-distance; orographic rain shadow marched upwind along the three-cell circulation. `thalos_export.py` consumes all five and now errors on a missing channel instead of silently falling back to Perlin. 7 unit tests, clippy-clean. **Two defects found by measuring, both regression-pinned in `conditioning_channels_are_not_saturated_or_dead`:** (1) NTR-X2a's `27−52·sin²lat` put land p1..p25 **all at −17.5 °C = `finalize`'s clamp floor** — a quarter of the planet's temperature conditioning was saturated and steering nothing; new curve gives land p25 −3.4 / p50 10.6 (<1 % pinned); (2) the first rain-shadow term used a guessed `smoothstep(300, 2200)` and fired **nowhere** (land p75 exactly 0.0) — now self-calibrated to the measured barrier distribution, 13.0 % of land shadowed, the physically plausible band. **Open finding handed to the user:** the canonical macro terrain is **0.57 % plateau / 0.59 % basin** against 19.9 % plain + 6.6 % range, so the elevation side still has almost no landform diversity to condition on — fixing that means changing `ProceduralSurface` itself (world-visible: `GENERATOR_VERSION` bump, site re-verification), which is NTR-X2f and a scope call, not a silent edit | done | M | NTR-X2c | ntr §4.3 · §4.4 |

| NTR-X2m | **The 5-channel chart collapsed MID-RANGE relief, and that is what "looks worse" means** (user 2026-07-29, after flying it: "you made the terrain flatter... features far less pronounced on a surface scale, few mountains"). Binned by the OLD chart's own 69 km relief, area-weighted, the new/old ratio is **not uniform** — it is a collapse of the middle: 0–150 m 0.75 · 150–300 m 0.64 · **300–600 m 0.60 (30 % of land)** · 600–1000 m 0.65 · 1000–1600 m 0.80 · 1600–2600 m **0.89** · 2600 m+ **0.90**. So **genuine mountains kept ~90 % of their relief while the rolling terrain that covers 48 % of the land kept 60–65 %.** That reads as "few mountains" precisely because the peaks survived and everything between them flattened — variety collapsed, not scale. Matches AGENT-A's bench (gentle provinces lose ~1.9–2.4×, high-orogeny ones barely). **Ruled out as the surface-scale mechanism:** the two height-coupled sub-band gains barely moved — `chart_rough_scale` (fine + erosion amplitude outside the detail window) mean 0.626 → 0.608, `relief_w` (sub-chart octaves) 1.268 → 1.111. | NTR-X2o | **Horizontal "smears" on the world map are the sub-chart noise lattice aligning with latitude** (user 2026-07-29: "what are those strange smears?"). **Mechanism:** `DiffusionSurface::octave_sum` feeds `grad_noise(dir * (R/lambda))`, a **cubic-lattice** 3-D gradient noise. On a sphere `dir.y == sin(lat)`, so a line of constant latitude is a line of **constant noise-lattice y** — the lattice planes and the equirect rows are the same lines. That is invisible while many octaves overlap, but `footprint_gate` cuts the band hard: at the global map's 4 km footprint **only 2 of 5 sub-chart octaves survive** (34 km @ 251 m and 15 km @ 144 m; the 6.5 km octave gates to 0 and the loop breaks), and two octaves of lattice noise do not mask their own lattice. At the 430 m zoom footprint 5 octaves are live and the artifact is **gone** — which is the A/B. **Ruled out by experiment, so nobody re-derives them:** (1) not the chart — a chart-only bicubic render of the same crop is clean; (2) not the 90 m detail window — removing it leaves the artifact pixel-identical; (3) not map aliasing — it survives at `WORLD_W=8192` where pixel ≈ lod; (4) not the producer's coarse tile stride — peak rows sit 11.9 px from a multiple of 48 against 12.0 for random; (5) not the wrap seam, which is a separate and milder edge effect (row-gradient 161–220 m vs 249 m interior). The map's hypso mode **amplifies** it (`hypso_color(z + climate_cold_lift_m(lat))`, `world_map/src/main.rs:429`) because ~250 m of banding straddles the upland/scree ramp stops, but `cold_lift` varies only ~4 m per row so it is not the source — biome mode shows the same fingers, weaker. **Consequence beyond the map: the game samples coarse footprints from orbit too**, so the same latitude banding is expected in orbital framings; it is masked at ground level where 5 octaves are live. **Suggested fix (cheapest first):** feed the noise a fixed irrational rotation so lattice planes can never align with constant latitude — a per-octave basis rotation costs nothing and removes the class, rather than widening the gate and paying for octaves the footprint cannot show **FIX ATTEMPTED AND FALSIFIED 2026-07-29 — do not retry it.** Rotating every band's lattice into its own basis (per-octave quaternion, tilt 32–47° / spin 33–57° derived so no lattice axis lands within 0.73 of Y, with tests pinning the property) was implemented, verified correct by its own tests, and **changed nothing in the frame** — the fingers are pixel-comparable before and after. So the lattice-latitude alignment is a real property of `grad_noise` on a sphere but is **not the cause**. Reverted rather than shipped: it perturbs every diffusion height and forces a `GENERATOR_VERSION` break for no measured benefit, on terrain the user had just approved. (`GENERATOR_VERSION` stays 26.) **Seven candidates now falsified** — chart raster, detail window, map aliasing, producer tile stride, wrap seam, hypso `cold_lift` (it is a smooth power of abs(sin lat) and ~0 at the 17 deg latitude where the artifact is strongest), and the noise lattice basis. What remains: at the map's 4 km footprint the height is **only** `chart + 2 sub-chart octaves` (34 km @ 251 m, 15 km @ 144 m) — everything finer is gated off — and a chart-only render is clean, so the structure is created by those two octaves or by `relief_w` (`chart_h/700 + 0.25`, clamped 0.25–2.0) modulating their amplitude. **Next probe should render `octave_sum` alone**, not the composed height, which is the one isolation nobody has run | next | S | NTR-X2n | INC candidate |
| NTR-X2p | **Earth-like macro geography: inland seas, embayments, island systems** (user 2026-07-29 with a Blue Marble reference: "we want our terrain to be more interesting, like earth. with inland seas, interesting islands"). **Architectural finding — inland seas at sea level are already free on the Thalos path, they are simply never generated.** The analytic ocean is a sphere at r=R and decides water from the *sign* of the field, so **connectivity is irrelevant to it**: any enclosed basin the macro field puts below 0 renders as water today. `combine_macro_and_relief`'s "no sea on land" rule only floors **relief-carved** basins on macro land (protecting the LOD-invariant waterline, INC-0003) — it does not stop the **macro field itself** from having an enclosed sea. And `enforce_single_connected_ocean` lives in the **baked** `feature_compiler`/`aging_oceanic_field` path, not in `ProceduralSurface`, so it does not apply to Thalos at runtime (**but would delete inland seas if Thalos ever bakes through that path — check before it does**). So the gap is not the renderer or the composition, it is that `continentalness` is a smooth plate+fBm blob field that essentially never closes a basin. **Sub-sea-level inland seas (Caspian-like, −28 m) are the genuinely hard case** — one analytic sphere can draw exactly one water level, so those need renderer work and are a separate decision. Scope: enclosed/semi-enclosed basins (Mediterranean, Hudson, Gulf), deep embayments and peninsulas, and continents of *distinct* size and character rather than similar blobs. Shares its lever with NTR-X2f (provinces authored into the macro field) and NTR-X2g (process-typed island chains) — all three are the same upstream fix: the canonical macro field is too smooth to be interesting, and every downstream band faithfully details whatever it is given **FIRST SLICE LANDED 2026-07-29** (user: "make the macro field more interesting, earth-like continents"): a **marine-incursion** term in `continentalness_base` — a sparse thresholded field subtracted from continentalness, weighted to peak just inland of the margin and fade into the deep interior, so it opens **bays** near the coast and closes into **inland seas** behind it, as one continuum. **Tuned against a measured distribution, not by feel** — a throwaway in-module probe showed the incursion fBm runs p50 −0.00 / p90 0.21 / p99 0.35 / max 0.46, and the first bias tried (0.34) sat at p99: 1.1 % of the sphere qualified and the largest possible subtraction was 0.12 against a median inland of 0.21, so it could not flood anything and the land fraction did not move. Retuned to bias 0.15 (~p85) / gain 1.4. Land fell 35.3 % → 33.0 %, so **`CONTINENT_C0` 0.143 → 0.125** restores the lore target: **34.9 % on both backings**, runway site LAND (612 m canonical, 856 m diffusion). `GENERATOR_VERSION` 26 → 27. **The diffusion chart is conditioned on this field, so it was re-exported and re-generated in the same change** (chart `sha 32a44efbb6589cca`, detail window re-run) — leaving it would have put neural relief on the old continents inside the new coastline. Province mix barely moved (plateau 0.57 → 0.66 % of surface), so this does **not** substitute for NTR-X2f. **SECOND SLICE 2026-07-29 — incursions elongate along plate boundaries** (user: "elongate the basins along the tectonic boundaries"). `plate_boundary_normal` reuses `plate_value`'s **exact cell lattice and jitter** and returns the vector between the two nearest feature points — a Worley boundary *is* the perpendicular bisector of that pair, so the normal is exact in one pass, with no finite differences and no second field; it is only evaluated inside the reach window. The incursion sample is then **stretched along** that boundary by `INCURSION_ELONGATION` 3.2. **Direction is load-bearing: stretch, never compress.** Compressing across the boundary would give the same visual aspect ratio but push the octave past its own band limit and alias into hatching — the cloud-cell-field lesson. Stretching only ever makes features coarser, which cannot alias. Because coarser features cross the threshold less often, land rose 34.9 % → 36.1 % and `CONTINENT_C0` 0.125 → **0.136** restores it (34.9 % both backings, runway LAND 609 m canonical / 851 m diffusion). Chart re-generated again (`sha 19b87bbf5e0a2282`). **Trade, honestly:** there are now **fewer but larger and genuinely elongated** seas rather than many round ones — arguably closer to Earth, which has a handful of major inland seas, but it is a trade and `INCURSION_BIAS` is the knob if more are wanted. **THIRD SLICE 2026-07-29 — size hierarchy and character** (user: "give the continents different sizes and character"). Two low-frequency fields, neither touching the plate lattice: **clustering** (`SUPERCONTINENT_WL_M` 11 000 km, well above the 2 800 km cell, added to the plate value so *neighbouring* plates agree and runs of them merge — the size spread emerges from correlation rather than being authored) and **fragmentation** (`FRAGMENT_WL_M` 6 500 km modulating the detail-overlay weight 0.45×–1.75×, so one mass stays a solid craton while another shatters into peninsulas and offshore fragments — character, independent of size). First try at `SUPERCONTINENT_W` 0.42 **merged everything into one supercontinent**, which is a size hierarchy of one; 0.24 gives huge / medium / polar / small-fragment with visibly different margin character. `CONTINENT_C0` 0.136 → **0.150** holds the target (34.8 % both backings, runway LAND 584 m canonical / 828 m diffusion). Chart regenerated (`sha 0173af63b1eab572`). Remaining from the reference: sub-sea-level seas still need renderer work, and there are still **no rivers** — see NTR-X2q | wip | M–L | — | NTR-X2f · NTR-X2g · ADR-20260720T185954Z |
| NTR-X2q | **Rivers — what it would take** (user question 2026-07-29: "what about rivers btw? will we get those?"). **Today: valleys yes, water no.** The diffusion band carries real valley networks at 200 m–2 km and the erosion band adds slope-steered branched gullies below 90 m, so the *shape* of river country is already there — but nothing computes flow and nothing renders water above sea level. Two independent blockers, and the second is shared with sub-sea-level inland seas: (1) **no hydrology** — flow accumulation needs a global raster and a downhill traversal, which is a bake-time operation, while Thalos's surface is runtime-analytic and `SurfaceQuery` is a per-point black box with no neighbourhood; (2) **the ocean is one analytic sphere at r=R** (ADR-20260720T185954Z), so it draws exactly one water level and cannot render a river at 300 m or a lake at −28 m. **Ladder, cheapest first:** *(a)* rivers as a **landcover channel** — flow accumulation baked on the NTR-X3 720 m band (very tractable offline; the band is 27.8k×13.9k), surfaced as a wetness/albedo/vegetation term with no geometry and no water surface. Reads as rivers from the air, costs no renderer work, and rides the landcover authority we already have. *(b)* **carve** the accumulated channels back into the height band, so valleys actually converge downhill instead of merely looking like they do. *(c)* **rendered water at arbitrary altitude** — the real feature, and the one that also unlocks lakes and Caspian-like seas. Needs a water surface that is not the sea sphere; that is a renderer decision, not a terrain one. **(a) is the honest MVP** and it sequences naturally after NTR-X3, because it wants that band to exist **(a) BAKE LANDED AND VERIFIED 2026-07-29; LANDCOVER HOOKUP LANDED BUT ITS VISIBLE EFFECT IS NOT YET DEMONSTRATED.** `examples/bake_rivers.rs` samples the active backing on an equirect grid, fills depressions, routes steepest descent and accumulates catchment; 50 Mpx at 2 km/px in **26 s**. Result is a real drainage hierarchy — max catchment **4 238 038 km²** (Amazon 7 M), p99.9 288 879 (Mississippi-scale) — and the standalone render shows proper dendritic networks with trunk stems reaching the coast. Ships as **u8 log-catchment: 50 MB raw, 7.2 MB lzma** for the whole planet. **Three findings worth keeping.** (1) *Equirect is not a grid*: steepest descent must compare gradients (rise over real run, `Δlon·R·cos lat` wide vs `Δlat·R` tall) and catchment must be `cos(lat)`-area-weighted, or rivers run east-west above the tropics. (2) *Depression filling is mandatory* — without it every pit truncates its catchment and the largest river drained 20 505 km², smaller than the Rhine. (3) *Fill with a **randomised** epsilon, not a constant* — filling to the exact spill height creates flats where no neighbour is strictly downhill, which made drainage **worse** (10 928 km²); a constant epsilon then fixed the catchments but printed **long straight streaks** because every flat drained whichever way the flood swept, and randomising it removed them. That streak/no-streak A/B also **exonerated the terrain**: the NTR-X2o smears are not drainage-visible. **Landcover hookup:** `RiverField` + `ProceduralSurface::with_rivers` + `DiffusionSurface::with_rivers` (forwards to the inner landcover authority), attaching in `world_map`; the raster is backing-stamped and `load` **refuses a mismatch** rather than putting rivers on terrain they did not drain. Applied to landcover moisture via `landcover_moisture_at`, deliberately **not** inside `macro_moisture`, which also feeds `macro_signals` and therefore the diffusion conditioning — the raster is baked *from* conditioned terrain, so feeding it back would close a loop that ADR-20260725T004758Z part 4 forbids. `ProceduralSurface` lost `Copy` to hold the `Arc` (zero call-site fallout, measured). **What is NOT done:** the ribbon is invisible in both places checked, for two separate and now-diagnosed reasons — at planet-map scale a 2 km channel is **sub-pixel** (a 3072-wide map pixel is 6.5 km), and `RIVER_MOISTURE_MAX` 0.72 makes the term a **no-op wherever moisture already exceeds it**, i.e. all humid terrain. The honest fix is a *riparian band* — rivers as their own landcover class with gallery vegetation and a darker channel — rather than a moisture nudge, plus a finer bake for the aerial read. Next agent: start there, not at the bake. **STRUCTURE MEASURED 2026-07-29** after the user reported the network "doesn't have that sense of hierarchy" and looks "a bit too frequent". Horton-Strahler over **stream segments**: orders 1-4 give Rb **4.17 / 4.21 / 3.99 / 4.97**, mean **4.87** — Earth is **3-5**. **The network is correctly hierarchical; the perception was the rendering.** Two things produced it, both fixed by display choices rather than by the bake: (1) *too frequent* is a threshold — a 10^3 km2 channel head puts **3.44 %** of land in channel, where 3x10^4 puts **0.49 %**, which is the density an atlas draws; (2) *no hierarchy* is because brightness alone conveys none — re-rendered with a real threshold and **width by catchment decade** the trunk systems separate immediately and it reads like a river map. **Methodological catch worth keeping:** the first pass counted *cells* per order and reported Rb 2.53, which looks like a genuine structural defect and would have sent the next agent tuning the bake. Horton's ratio is defined over stream **segments** (a maximal run of one order); counting cells weights by channel length and flattens the ratio. Count segment *ends* — where order changes or the sea is reached. A second direction bug in the same code (Strahler swept downstream-first) reported *every* stream as order 1. **Consequence for the riparian band:** scale width and strength by catchment decade and start it near 3x10^4 km2, not 10^3. Residual: polar speckle where the equirect grid oversamples longitude. **RIPARIAN BAND LANDED 2026-07-29 and is visible.** Two **nested** strengths, which is what supplies the width hierarchy: a broad **gallery corridor** (3x10^4 -> 10^6 km2, the measured atlas-density head) and a narrow **channel core** (3x10^5 -> 3x10^6, trunk rivers only). Because catchment falls off away from a channel, a higher threshold is automatically a *narrower line* — so a trunk gets a wide corridor and a dark bed while a creek gets neither, and hierarchy shows as extent rather than as brightness, which was the whole of the original complaint. Applied to **albedo directly** (`riparian_albedo`, at all three albedo sites) rather than only through moisture, because the moisture route is a no-op wherever climate moisture already exceeds `RIVER_MOISTURE_MAX` — i.e. everywhere humid, which is where half the rivers are, and which is why the first hookup was invisible. **Verification needed a tool change:** `render_zoom` coloured by `hypso_color(z)`, a function of height alone, so it is **structurally blind** to any landcover-only channel — the band was correct and unviewable for two renders. `WORLD_MODE=biome` now works in zoom and shows the trunk as a dark meandering line. Cosmetic follow-up: zoom-biome paints ocean with land albedo (it never had a water branch). Still open: rivers as their own `MacroBiome` variant (deliberately skipped — it ripples through the biome table and labels), a finer bake for the aerial read, and NTR-X2q(b)/(c) | wip | (a) M · (b) M · (c) L | NTR-X3 | ADR-20260720T185954Z · NTR-X2p |

Per-filter survivor counts (`coast_site_search`: candidates / none_returned / passed_straddle / passed_strand / passed_sun) are instrumented and compile-clean, but **the run is blocked**: `local_physics/gear.rs:427` fails on another agent's in-flight `GearTuning` rename (`damping_ratio` → `damping_ratio_compress`/`_rebound`). Next agent: run the capture, read the counters, and note the same latent failure may affect `find_ocean_site` — its reject rule `height_m > -250.0` also passes on an all-zero source, and an open-ocean fallback image is indistinguishable from a correct one | verify | S | NTR-X2i | ADR-20260729T054550Z · BL-20 |
| NTR-X2e | **Conditioning lever — superseded, kept as the pointer.** Scoped by me 2026-07-29; executed in parallel as **NTR-X2c** (bench) + **NTR-X2d** (generator rewrite), which went further: 5 channels, landform provinces, all four missing post-`finalize` transforms, and measured verdicts. Two of my scoping claims were corrected by measurement — (1) per-channel `cond_snr` is a **noise level, lower = stronger**, and Thalos's shipped values were already near-optimal, so "conditioning set too weak" is falsified; (2) I said mesa fields need the 30 m model — 30 m is materially better for arid *structural* terrain (escarpment flat-fraction 96 %→70 %, canyon slope 1.2°→3.7°) but still yields **no mesas**. | NTR-X2f | **Provinces must be authored *into* the height field, not only derived from it** (measured 2026-07-29, after NTR-X2d landed). `ConditioningChart` classifies correctly, but it can only classify what the canonical field contains, and the first full run says Thalos is: plain **63 % of land**, range 23 %, shield 9 %, plateau **2.2 %**, basin 2.0 % (area-weighted; the cell-count figures first recorded here — plain 66 %, plateau 1.9 % — carried the same unweighted-equirect defect as this row's plateau multiplier, found by AGENT-A in `macro_conditioning.rs` itself and fixed there with a guard test). Plateau is precisely the province the bench found *does* yield branching canyon networks — so canyon country will stay nearly absent no matter how good the conditioning is. Continentality is 0 at the median (Thalos's continents are narrow). (An earlier version of this row claimed the rain-shadow term fires nowhere; that was measured against a pre-calibration build — NTR-X2d self-calibrated it to **14.7 % of land shadowed** (area-weighted; 13.0 % by cell count), which supersedes it.) The fix is upstream in `ProceduralSurface`: give the macro field high-standing flat regions, wider interiors, and orographic barriers — respecting the one-height-authority and runway-site invariants `macro_conditioning` correctly refuses to break. Also folds in the coast-character field (6,000 km, depositional↔erosional), which is the same object at the margin. **Confirmed end-to-end 2026-07-29** by re-running the pipeline on the new 5-channel chart (seed 1337, same window; old chart backed up): land mean 911 → 739 m, p50 686 → 521, max 8298 → 6124, **69 km relief p50 451 → 262 m**, land above 800 m 39.7 % → 24.0 %, corr 0.92 against the old chart — the planet got calmer and lower, and and plateau-capable terrain appears to halve on an **absolute** cut — high-and-flat land 2.28 % → 1.12 % of all land. **That reading is an artifact and the sign reverses on a level-matched cut** (AGENT-A's methodological catch): the whole elevation distribution moved down ~172 m, so a fixed 800 m threshold conflates *less high ground* with *less plateau character*. Re-cut at the top quartile of **each chart's own** land elevation, flatness cut held absolute at <250 m/69 km: **old 0.26 % of land high-and-flat (1.1 % of its high ground), new 1.45 % (5.8 %)** — the 5-channel chart has **~5.5× more** plateau-character terrain, not less. *Method (reproduce with this or the number is not comparable):* cos(lat) area weighting, 3 px = 69 km max-minus-min with longitude wrap, weighted percentiles. AGENT-B first reported ~9.5× from an **unweighted** equirect count, which over-represents polar rows; AGENT-A reimplemented independently and got 5.5×, and AGENT-B's corrected implementation then reproduced AGENT-A's figures to every digit. Two implementations, one number. So the loss is a level shift, not a flatness collapse, which means the `temp_sd` lever below plausibly recovers the relief **while keeping** the improved plateau character. Caveat that outranks the metric: BENCH_NOTES already records a high-and-flat metric being refuted by the 1:1 crop (smooth ramp with rough knots, no mesas), so this says the province classifier has more to work with, *not* that morphology arrived. Earlier framings of this row (4.6 % → 4.3 %, then 1.89 % → 0.95 %, then ~9.5×) are all superseded: the first two by the level-matched cut, the third by area weighting. Climate steering demonstrably works (dry land >0.72 rose 15.9 % → 22.1 %, snow halved 4.2 % → 1.9 % of land); landform *families* did not appear, and canyon-capable ground got **scarcer**. **Mechanism (AGENT-A, `bench_out/authored5` vs `authored3-control`, channel-set the only axis):** this is the 3→5-channel change, not a pipeline fault — synthetic median 3ch/5ch relief ratio **1.62** (n=16) against **1.83** on the real chart, and it is province-dependent, not blanket (gentle provinces lose ~1.9×, `range_maritime` 0.86 and `highland_cold` 0.65 *gain*). Thalos being 66 % plain is why the aggregate follows the plain-like ratio. It is a **lever, not a fact**: `plateau_arid_lowseason` recovers 442 → 858 m from softening authored `temp_sd` alone — but BENCH_NOTES records that same case as a smooth ramp with rough knots, so the lever buys back *relief*, never *morphology*. **The lever was then run on the real planet (user ruling 2026-07-29 "keep the new chart and try the temp_sd lever") and it is measured, weak, and NOT worth applying.** Two clean single-axis sweeps, chart-only exports, `sd' = 176 + (sd−176)·k` toward `prior::TEMP_SD_MIN` (k=1.00 reproduced the installed chart's hash `02849edc47d1694f` exactly — control passed): 69 km relief p50 **262 (k=1.00) → 296 (k=0.50) → 317 (k=0.30) m**, against 451 m on the 3-channel chart. So the maximum setting recovers only **~29 % of the gap**, and k=0.30 *costs* plateau character (level-matched cut 1.45 % → 1.24 %). k=0.50 holds it (1.43 %) for +13 % relief. The second seasonality channel is not a lever at all: `precip_cv` k=0.30 moves relief 262 → 274 m (+4 %). **Three-panel hillshade of 3ch / k=1.00 / k=0.50 at matched sun and ramp: k=1.00 and k=0.50 are not visually distinguishable.** The bench's ×2 was one synthetic arid province; Thalos is 63 % plain and AGENT-A established the channel-set effect is province-dependent, which damps it. **Verdict: do not bake a `k` into `ConditioningChart`** — it is a crate change, a regen and a cache break for an invisible result. The relief gap is not reachable from climate conditioning; this row's own fix (provinces authored *into* the height field) is the route. Recorded so nobody re-runs the sweep. Coastline and runway site unaffected (35.2 % land both, site LAND at 725 m) — ADR-20260729T054550Z holding across a full content regen. Open question for the user: the relief halving may be a fidelity regression rather than a correction | next | M–L | NTR-X2d | ntr §4.4 |

| BL-20260730T223451Z-shadow-flicker | **Shadows flickered in and out, foliage worst** (user 2026-07-30: "the shadows (especially foliage) are flickering in and out. very annoying"). Diagnosed from the existing shadow lane, not from a capture — flicker is temporal and no still shows it. **Primary cause (INC-20260730T223451Z):** `rendering::sun_shadow`'s `PostUpdate` chain had **no ordering edge** to big_space's `CellCoord::recenter_large_transforms`, which `BigSpaceCorePlugin` registers plain in `PostUpdate` *outside* `TransformSystems::Propagate` — so `.before(TransformSystems::Propagate)` did not constrain it and the executor could slot it between `update_real_space_origin` (publishes `RealSpaceOrigin`) and `update_sun_shadow_camera` (consumes it). On those frames the whole cascade rig sat one 1 km grid cell from the world it covers; cascade 0's half-extent is ~450 m, so near-field receivers fell out of the crisp cascade into cascade 1, whose ~6× coarser texel and saturating foliage bias (`NO_NORMAL_BIAS_SCALE`) erase tree/shrub casters — hence foliage worst. Non-deterministic frame to frame ⇒ flicker. **The instrument already existed and was already firing:** `origin_frame_error_m` read exactly 1000.0 / 1414.2 m (one cell, and its diagonal) on ~1 % of all gauges and ~half of them during sustained camera motion; `just diag` reports it as `shadow_frame_desync`. Fixed by `.after(CellCoord::recenter_large_transforms)` — against the *system*, never the enclosing `BigSpaceSystems::RecenterLargeTransforms` set, which also contains `find_floating_origin` (itself inside `Propagate`) and would cycle. The false doc comment on `update_real_space_origin` that asserted this ordering was already guaranteed is corrected. **Secondary, the slower artifact (same complaint, different mechanism):** the cascade footprint breathed. (a) `craft_local` was a bare `altitude > 50 km` test gating a mode that parks cascades 1–2 and kills every ground shadow in one frame — a camera loitering at the threshold strobed the whole ground shadow world (`active_cascades` alternating 3→1→3 between 1 Hz samples); now latched across a 40–50 km band (`SHADOW_CRAFT_LOCAL_EXIT_M`). (b) The footprint smoother's snap bypass sat at 2×, which every doubling of camera AGL trips, stepping each cascade's texel — and the texel-proportional foliage bias with it — by 2× in one frame; that is the exact scale pop the smoother exists to remove, left reachable by too low a threshold. Now 8× (`SHADOW_FOOTPRINT_SNAP_RATIO`). (c) A missed terrain-height sample fell back to the datum (0 m) — a step equal to the whole site elevation, which feeds both the cascade centre and (via `cam_agl`) a footprint that moves up to 6× it, so an intermittently-cold height source shoved every extent and bias between neighbouring frames; now holds the last resolved sample per body. The system's six `Local`s became one `SunShadowMemory` (it was at Bevy's `SystemParam` ceiling). **New diag check `shadow_mode_strobe`** counts cascade-mode *reversals* — an honest climb reads 3→1 and stays, contributing none — with both-ways tests. Compile-clean; `thalos_diag` 20/20 green; the app boots (no schedule cycle) and `spaceport-aerial` captured. **Not runtime-verified:** the symptom is temporal, and a concurrent `CASCADE_COUNT` 3→4 migration by another agent broke the workspace build before a post-fix capture could be pinned to a source floor containing these edits | verify | S | **user:** fly low over forest with the camera moving and confirm foliage shadows hold steady; then `just diag` — `shadow_frame_desync` must be absent (it is the standing recurrence tell, non-zero only ever as an exact 1 km cell multiple), and `shadow_mode_strobe` absent after a climb to orbit and back | INC-20260730T223451Z-shadow-rig-raced-bigspace-recentring · sun_shadow.rs · real_space.rs · tools/diag |

Regressions checked: `just map` healthy (land 35.3 %, snow 1.6 %, ice polar-only), `mira-rim` clean on the shared tile path. **Now blocked on NTR-X5** — the tile ground renders 2× brighter and 0.6× as saturated as the same terrain through udlod, which flattens all texturing; texgen atlases deliberately not started until the lighting response was settled. **Unblocked 2026-07-24** — NTR-X5's exposure fix landed; the massif frames now carry dark rock faces, real snowfields, and green forest below (`artifacts/visual/runs/ntr-x4-p1/r5-exposure/`). **Ruggedness-aware LOD landed 2026-07-25** (user: "retain more of this mountainous/detailed terrain from higher altitudes" — the fine tiles lingering through the despawn grace during a fast zoom-out looked better than the coarse selection that replaced them). `SPLIT_FACTOR` is now the *floor* of a relief-scaled rule (ntr §7's screen-space-error fork, interim proxy): a tile's split distance ramps 6× → 18× arc with its measured **ruggedness** = relief ÷ arc, a scale-invariant mean-slope proxy so an unmeasured tile can inherit its nearest measured ancestor's value unscaled (a *relief* figure could not — the Hurst factor would compound per level and ratchet the selection deeper each descent). The memo is measured from the `h_range` generation already computes, never pruned on despawn (a key's split factor must not change when its tile leaves residency), so a cold start is exactly the old distance rule and sharpens as tiles land. A detail floor (`RUGGED_SPACING_FLOOR_M` 45 m ≈ Nyquist on the diffusion window's 90 m band) switches the boost off where the source has no more to give — without it the 22 km god view paid 2.7× the tiles for a frame indistinguishable from baseline. **Capture-verified** (`artifacts/visual/runs/altitude-detail/`, `massif-aerial` at 22/60/160 km): at 160 km the surrounding uplands and distant ranges regain ridge and drainage structure where the baseline was low-contrast mush; cost 2,233 → 4,419 tiles (1.98×), cold stream 11.2 s. Plains and ocean unchanged by construction. En route the descent-coverage gate was found **red at HEAD** and mis-calibrated, not broken: its 13 s settle could not drain a 6,450-tile working set at the simulated 120 tiles/s, so the convergence assert fired on a state the harness never reached (the per-tick coverage invariant was live and silent throughout — no hole hid behind it). Settle window now sized from the measured drain, plus a `rugged_descent_…` twin that drives the relief feedback loop through a rugged belt (proves no ratchet: `desired` stabilises and residents converge onto it). | BL-20260729T041005Z-motion-aware-streaming | **Fidelity follows the eye: motion-aware terrain + tree streaming** (user 2026-07-29: trees must load around the view much faster; freecam terrain should feel real, not pop in — sacrifice detail at speed, settle to high fidelity where the camera needs it). Three coordinated changes, all keyed off one new signal — `ViewAnchor.speed_m_s`, the EMA-smoothed body-fixed eye speed (co-rotation counts; teleports reseed to 0): (1) **tile terrain motion brake** — `select_leaves_scaled` refuses splits whose children the eye would cross in under `MOTION_CROSS_MIN_S` (1 s), so a fast view streams coarse-but-stable ground instead of fine tiles that land mid-flight as pop-in; walking never trips it, a 100 m/s freecam stops ~1-2 levels short, authored pad floors outrank it (test: `motion_brake_coarsens_but_never_beats_a_site_floor`); (2) **tree dispatch is coverage-first + coarse-first** — missing tiles outrank re-LOD upgrades (`TREE_UPGRADE_PENALTY` 3x distance), a cold fill (>24 missing) builds mesh-band tiles as cheap impostors first and upgrades nearest-first afterwards (innermost LOD0 band exempt), and the mesh-LOD pick gets a motion look-ahead (`TREE_MOTION_LEAD_S` 3 s: at speed, LOD keys off where the eye is about to be); (3) `TREE_MAX_IN_FLIGHT` 12 -> 24 (queue depth for the async pool). Smootherstep-weighted bilinear was tried and **rejected** — zeroing the gradient at cell edges made the band 4.5× worse. | BL-20260729T011500Z-erosion-lattice | **The erosion band rendered as a tyre-tread lattice** (user 2026-07-29, viewpoint `thalos-8-km`: "the neural terrain is still super lumpy… it's not the erosion filter" — it was; band-off at that viewpoint removes the pattern entirely). Same-size, same-orientation teardrop cells tiled across the massif. **Same object as BL-20260727T012304Z's "shark fins"**: the fold rounding landed there turned each cell's C0 fold into a smooth lobe, which fixed the knife and made the lattice *more* legible. Root cause: **`strength` was being used as a depth knob.** Each octave turns the filter's gully direction by `strength · gully_weight / cell_scale` (the `scale` cancels) — that advection is the only mechanism that makes gullies branch. Rounds 1–3 read cone fields as an amplitude problem and pulled `strength` 0.03 → 0.012, i.e. **1.7 % turn per octave against the filter's designed 22 %**; `gully_weight`, `onset` and `assumed_slope` were softened for the same reason and damp the same advection. Under-regime, no branching develops and the bare phacelle cells (a jittered integer lattice, reused by every octave at 2× frequency — nested and axis-aligned) stack in phase. **Landed 2026-07-29:** shape params back to the filter's own defaults, depth moved to a separate `EROSION_DEPTH_GAIN` multiplier, and `EROSION_SCALE_M` 2400 → 700 m so the cells are a *sharpening* detail on the diffusion terrain rather than a competing macro pattern (user: "proportional and to scale… use it to sharpen up and fine tune our base diffusion terrain"). Carve 41 → 11.8 m mean. `GENERATOR_VERSION` 24 → 25. **Falsified en route and reverted, do not retry:** a cell-scale domain-warp octave — a warp is a smooth displacement, so it bends the lattice without disturbing its local periodicity; vp_scale700 and vp_warp2 are indistinguishable  **Round 2 (2026-07-29, after the user pointed at the filter's own blog):** the **fade target was pinned**. The author defines it as `inverse_lerp(valleyAlt, peakAlt, h)·2−1` — signed, −1 in valleys to +1 on peaks, sweeping the range the local terrain spans. Ours was `clamp(h_c/3000)`: never negative anywhere on land, and saturated at +1 across the whole 3.0–5.8 km massif, so an input the filter varies its pattern by was a constant exactly where the pattern looked most uniform. Now measured against the **planetary band** (deviation from the regional trend, not altitude) over `EROSION_FADE_RANGE_M`. **Correct per spec but it did not move this framing** — kept because the input was genuinely wrong, not because it fixed the symptom. What did move it: restoring round 2's `onset × 0.6`. That finding stands on its own and is a *different* failure from the strength one — `onset` sets how gentle a slope still carves, and the default ramp lets the base octave through on near-flat ground where nothing breaks the cell periodicity. The blog also confirms the compensation rule rounds 1–3 missed: lowering `gully_weight` must be paid for by raising `strength`; they lowered both. | verify | M | **user:** re-fly `thalos-8-km` and judge. Capture trail in `artifacts/visual/runs/sharpen-probe/`: vp_regime (regime only) → vp_scale700 (+700 m cells) → vp_fade (+fade fix) → **vp_onset**. **`EROSION_SCALE_M` is now 1500 m (user request 2026-07-29) and is UNCAPTURED** — the capture host started failing every `shot` mid-stream (host exits after `surface_settle`, before first full coverage) while the concurrent session has `tools/capture` + `crates/capture/protocol` uncommitted and an open incident on idle-host behaviour. **Control run proves it is environmental, not this change:** reverting to the previously-captured 700 m fails identically. **Resolved 2026-07-29: 1500 m captured and REJECTED.** Matched 4K pair on the viewpoint (`cmp_700.png` vs `cmp_1500.png`): at 1.5 km the cells are large, high-contrast, regularly spaced and the serrated cell edges return; 700 m keeps the drainage read with the lattice down at texture level. The crate's `mountain_width / 5..10` rule is for the filter *driving* the look — as a sharpening pass over terrain that already has structure at that scale, larger cells compete with the base instead of detailing it. **Landed at 700 m.** (Note for future A/Bs: the first 1500 m capture came back 1920×1080 against the 700 m 3840×2160 and was thrown out — the viewpoint's own viewport is not always honoured; pass `THALOS_SCREENSHOT_SIZE` explicitly when comparing.) | NTR-REFL-ORBIT | **Orbital hull reflections show the real planet** (user 2026-07-29, asking whether RT would give nice orbital reflections — it would not: from orbit the ocean, atmosphere and clouds are analytic/volumetric composites with no geometry to trace, so reflection rays would return a bare rocky sphere; and cubemap parallax error scales inversely with feature distance, making the probe *nearly exact* in orbit and wrong only near the surface). The actual defect was much cheaper: the probe painted the planet as the constant `Vec3(0.25,0.35,0.55)` × Lambert × limb, so a roughness-0.08 hull reflected a smooth blue-grey blob. **Landed:** the disc now solves the exact ray-sphere hit per texel, rotates the normal into body-fixed using the rotation the renderer drew the body with (`PreciseRotation` — no tidal-lock special case), and samples the body's `ImpostorAlbedo`. That bake is now kept as CPU data in `ImpostorAlbedoRegistry` and feeds **both** the distant disc the player looks at and the reflection, so they cannot disagree. Normal solve is radius-normalised (`D/R = 1/planet_sin`) because the absolute-metre form loses most of its f32 precision to `|oc|²−R²` at orbital range; tested against an f64 solve from 30 km to 30,000 km. New `orbit-hull` preset + viewpoint entry, since every other orbital framing shows the planet rather than the ship and could not witness this. **Capture-verified as far as the cubemap, NOT yet to the hull.** New `planet_reflection` diagnostic on `orbit-hull` reports `impostor=true`, `disc_texels=142480` (36 % of the cube, 70.2° angular radius), `albedo_mean=(0.050, 0.105, 0.067)` — green-dominant real terrain albedo, against the old constant's blue-dominant (0.25, 0.35, 0.55) — and `albedo_spread=0.046` (≈ 51 % of mean luminance, so genuinely varying continents/ocean, not a flat tint). **But the captured hull shows none of it** (uniform pale blue-grey, `artifacts/visual/latest/orbit_hull.png`). **Both original hypotheses resolved 2026-07-30 from user screenshots + instrumentation (INC-20260730T015528Z), and the cause was a third one.** (b) *LightProbe not reaching the hull* — **FALSIFIED**: the probe's sun-disc glint is plainly visible as a sharp streak on the panels. (a) *`surface_blend = 0.50` at 200 km* — **real but minor**: it halves the planet's weight in the cube AND halves the flat ambient, so it roughly cancels. **The actual cause: `bevy_pbr::ambient::ambient_light()` applies to SPECULAR, not just diffuse.** `specular_occlusion = saturate(dot(F0, 16.5))` is 1.0 at a metal's F0 ≈ 0.85, so `GlobalAmbientLight` lands on mirrors at full strength as a direction-independent `F0 × ambient_color` term. The space fill is `AMBIENT_DAY_BRIGHTNESS = 1940` lux tinted `srgb(0.62,0.72,0.95)` — that IS the uniform pale blue-grey, and why the hull looked identical with a planet below it and in empty space. **Second bug found and FIXED:** the probe's orbital planet disc was the only lit term carrying no incident flux (sun disc, ground bounce and sky LUT all do), so it was bare reflectance; the old hand-tuned `Vec3(0.25,0.35,0.55)` had been standing in for `albedo × flux / π` (0.343 vs the physical 0.288), and switching to true albedo without it made the planet 3.8× dimmer. Now `planet_irradiance = flux / π`, which also makes the reflection react to heliocentric distance and exposure. Measured at 200 km: `planet_lux` 91 → **293** against `ambient_lux` **975**, i.e. the flat fill went from 10.7× to 3.3× stronger — still dominant. **Remaining, and a scope decision, not a nudge:** retiring or reducing the 1940 lux space stand-in changes every surface in orbit, and `update_sun_light`'s own comment names it as F7/W7 work ("which env-map IBL at photometric intensity will retire") — now unblocked, since the probe carries real photometric intensity. **Known gap:** the bake has no clouds, so the reflected planet is cloudless. **Recurrence tell:** compare `planet_lux` against `ambient_lux` in the same session — if the flat ambient wins, no cubemap content can show on a metal | verify | S | **user:** decide whether to retire/reduce the 1940 lux flat space ambient (F7/W7) — it is what is hiding the reflection | INC-20260730T015528Z · atmosphere.md §Reflective surfaces · ADR-20260724T224242Z |
| NTR-RT1 | **RT scene enabler + streaming-cost gate** (user direction 2026-07-24: targets are terrain shadows and mirror-steel ships; ADR-20260724T224242Z resolved the shape — Solari's `RaytracingScenePlugin` **only**, never `SolariLightingPlugin`). Add `bevy_solari` behind an off-by-default feature (**second lane fingerprint** — full graph rebuild on toggle, touches the capture host); **RT proxy entities** per tile and craft part (`RaytracingMesh3d` + `MeshMaterial3d<StandardMaterial>` + transform, **no `Mesh3d`** → RT-only, never rasters) so the visible surface keeps its `ExtendedMaterial`/Hapke. **Geometry half LANDED 2026-07-29 (ADR-20260729T070229Z), and it corrected this row's premise:** Solari's gate compares the *whole* attribute sequence for equality, so an EXTRA attribute disqualifies a mesh as surely as a missing one. Craft parts (POSITION/NORMAL/UV_0) reach it with one `add_raytracing_tangents` call and **do** share the visible handle; terrain tiles carry COLOR + UV_1 for the NTR-X4 spare-channel contract and can **never** be eligible, so they need a **separate RT-only twin** (`rt::rt_twin_of_tile`, derived from the raster mesh's own arrays so a reflection and the ground under it cannot disagree about where the ground is). Real duplicated geometry: **312 KiB/tile on top of the visible 347 KiB (1.90×)**, i.e. ~3.6 GiB extra to cover a 4 GiB resident set on a machine-wide budget already split between concurrent instances — which is why RT terrain is scoped to a **radius around `ViewAnchor`**, a necessity not a tuning choice. Eligibility is now a tested property of both meshers, including a test asserting the visible tile mesh is *not* eligible so a future change making it so flags the twin as dead weight; scatter meshes (grass/rock/tree/impostor) set `Mesh::enable_raytracing = false` or BLAS covers the whole scatter budget (Solari builds BLAS over *every* compatible mesh asset app-wide, not just RT-tagged ones). **Remaining, and why this stays open:** add `bevy_solari` behind the off-by-default feature (the second lane fingerprint), spawn the proxy entities, and take the measurement. **This row is a measurement gate, not a feature** — on Mira's cold descent report BLAS build + compaction throughput (budget is 400k verts/frame vs a ~7 M-vert cold fill ≈ 17+ frames of backlog), per-frame TLAS rebuild at ~1500 resident instances, VRAM delta, and frame-time percentiles vs the same run with the feature off. | NTR-X10 | **Ambient lit the night hemisphere on the tile path** (user 2026-07-25, planet from orbit: "our tile renderer has ambient lighting on the dark side"). Cause: `GlobalAmbientLight` is one per-camera value that `update_sun_light` derives at the *craft* — and from orbit `surface_daylight`'s altitude-aware terminator correctly reports full daylight (the craft isn't in the umbra), so the day-side sky/space fill (~1 940 lux, logged) was smeared over the whole visible globe. Ambient carries no `n·l`, so nothing zeroed it past the terminator. Fixed by re-spreading the ambient per fragment in `tile_terrain.wgsl` (`ambient_daylight_gate`): radial up from the new `center_ws` uniform, the shared `smoothstep(-0.06, 0.12, ·)` terminator, `floor + fill·daylight_fragment` with the craft's own gate (`lighting::SunDaylight`) divided back out so the near field stays an exact identity. Applied to the Hapke branch directly and to the PBR branch through `diffuse_occlusion`/`specular_occlusion`, which is the only thing `apply_pbr_lighting` scales its indirect terms by (flat ambient, env map, irradiance volumes) — direct light is untouched. **Evidence:** `artifacts/visual/runs/tile-night-ambient/` — `approach_after.png` (mira-approach: night side black past the terminator), `orbit_after.png` (mira-orbit: day side unregressed). **Remaining to close:** the matched pre-fix baseline (blocked mid-session by an unrelated in-flight workspace edit; reproduce by forcing the gate to `1.0`) and a surface-sunset no-regression pass on `cloud-sunset` / `runway-atmosphere`. **Sibling bug, not fixed:** the sun `DirectionalLight`'s *illuminance* is gated by the same craft-side `daylight`, so a craft inside the body's umbra should dim the lit crescent it is looking at — same root cause (one camera-side scalar over a whole visible globe), no evidence gathered yet | verify | S | — | ntr §6 |

| NTR-X16 | **Get udlod out of the running application — and out of the default build** (user 2026-07-26: "udlod should be nowhere in the running application path… not loaded, not even compiled by default"). Decisions taken with the user: the tile root **follows the view anchor** (one root at a time, not N); the ocean **ships the coast-atlas fallback** rather than waiting on a tile-path height lookup; udlod stays in-tree behind an **optional off-by-default feature**, not deleted. **Landed 2026-07-26 (compile-clean, 62/62 render tests):** **(1)** `rendering/map_terrain.rs` **deleted** — its own `MAP_TERRAIN_ENABLED` and `wanted_map_body` were both hard-parked (the map has drawn the baked impostor billboard at every distance since `bake_impostor_albedo_cube`), so ~600 lines of one of the larger udlod consumers were streaming nothing. **(2) Anchor-following tile root** — `ensure_tile_root` installed once on the first anchor-resolved body and stayed for the session, which is *the* reason udlod had to stay wired (it ground-rendered every other body). It now hands off when `ViewAnchor` settles on a different body for `HANDOFF_DWELL_S = 1 s`: `TileTerrainRoot::release_all` despawns the resident tiles (they are children of the big_space root, so nothing else ever would), clears the height mirror and the per-body ruggedness memo (a `TileKey` is only unique within a body), drops the `TILE_RENDERED` claim, and leaves the vacated body on a canonical `CpuPipelineHeightSource` so the propagator/colliders/HUD never see a missing height authority. **(3) BodySky/ocean's udlod height-atlas binding cut** — the ADR-20260720T185958Z signed-sea-field lookup resolved out of udlod's render-world registries and had *already* stopped resolving for every tile-rendered body, so the default path has been on the coast-atlas tail since tiles became default. Every body now takes it; bindings 7–10 stay in the layout bound to fallbacks with `tile_lookup.x = 0`, so the shader is unchanged. **Coastline fidelity at close range regresses on udlod-streamed ocean bodies (Pelagos)** — accepted; restoring it means an equivalent source from the tile height mirror, not resurrecting udlod. **Remaining (the actual compile-out):** `rendering/ground_terrain.rs` (1,731 lines) is two modules glued together — the legacy udlod ground *and* `BodySky` / `RealSpaceImpostor` / `AtmosphereTuning` / `update_body_terrain_atmosphere` / `build_terrain_scene_lighting`, which are the ADR-20260723T142945Z carve-out and must survive. Split it first (survivors out to a sky/atmosphere module, `TerrainFlattenRegistry` out to its own — it is renderer-independent and the tile path imports it), *then* make `thalos_udlod` an optional dep behind `legacy-udlod` (default off) and `#[cfg]` the call sites: `body_render`'s `lib.rs` re-export + `ground/{mod,pipeline,synthetic,height_source}.rs` (`RenderedGround::Udlod`, `GpuAtlasHeightMirror`, `new_udlod`), and the runtime's `terrain_residency.rs`, `tile_cache.rs` (udlod tiers), `surface_settle.rs` + `screenshot.rs` (udlod plateau probes — tile equivalents already exist beside them), `terrain_registry.rs` (`udlod_handle`), and `real_space.rs`/`spawn.rs`/`transforms.rs` (`PreciseRotation` — **written purely for udlod's Taylor path, nothing reads it otherwise, so it dies with the feature rather than needing extraction**). `THALOS_TILE_RENDERER=0` and the `renderer` compare axis become feature-only. | NTR-X12 | **Tile streaming/rendering throughput — close the gap to udlod** (user 2026-07-25: "tiles are quite slow in general… udlod is pretty fast for example"). Measured today: generation 5–8 ms/tile mean (bursts to 40 ms mean / 147 ms p95), landing ~120 tiles/s, working sets 2.2–4.4 k tiles at altitude and **6.5 k (distance rule) to 10.9 k (ruggedness rule)** at 30 m on Mira — so a cold low-altitude fill is ~90 s of streaming, and every resident tile is its own `Mesh` asset, entity and draw call. udlod is fast for a structural reason, not a tuning one: it is an atlas/clipmap, so "more detail" means writing texels into a GPU atlas that a fixed grid displaces in the vertex shader — no CPU meshing, no asset churn, ~1 draw call per ring. Ordered by payoff: **(a) horizon gating of *refinement* — LANDED 2026-07-25, capture-verified.** `above_horizon` (exact sphere-tangent test `p·c ≥ r²`, every tile lifted by the body's real `SurfaceQuery::height_range_m` so Thalos's ±9.8 km / Mira's ±13 km peaks are never culled) gates *refinement only* — a failing tile still enters the leaf set at whatever level it reached, so the sphere stays fully tiled and the coverage invariant, the height-mirror fallback and the impostor handoff never see a hole. It is view-**independent**, so unlike frustum culling it cannot pop when the camera turns. Measured on `massif-aerial`: **the whole ruggedness cost is paid back and then some** — 22 km 5,288 → 3,692 tiles and 23.1 s → **8.5 s cold stream, faster than the 2,874-tile baseline's 10.6 s**; 60 km 4,899 → 3,562 / 10.9 s; 160 km 4,419 → 3,427 / 10.5 s. Frames visually unchanged from the un-gated versions. Cutting non-visible tiles out of `desired` entirely would save 46–60 % instead of 28–40 %, but trades the no-holes guarantee — not taken. En route: INC-20260725T003418Z (point-sampled visibility froze refinement at the 384-tile MIN_LEVEL shell — a tile is up to 589 km across, so any per-tile rejection test must bound it as a *region*; `tiles::horizon_tests` gates that, and it is the trap waiting for frustum culling below). **Frustum gating still open** and now the riskier half: it is view-dependent, so it needs a generous cone plus `TileEye` carrying the camera forward. Subsumes the "view-direction admission priority" item on X1. **(b) GPU displacement off a height atlas** (one shared grid mesh instanced per tile, heights sampled in the vertex shader) — this is udlod's advantage and the X1 "GPU height mirror" item; keeps the quadtree residency logic, changes only what a resident tile *is*. **(c) tile-native provider** — 67² = 4,489 individual `sample_bands_d` point calls per tile is the raster→point→raster tax of ADR-20260722T105147Z; one block decode should be 5–10×. **(d) memoize the provider — LANDED 2026-07-26.** udlod has had a memory+disk tile cache since it was the default renderer; it did not come across with the handover because those wrappers are typed to udlod's own `TileProvider` trait, so the tile path re-evaluated the field on every despawn/revisit *and every boot*. `tiles::cache::CachedTileProvider` restates the same two tiers for `TerrainTileProvider` (LRU-by-bytes memory tier held in `TileCacheRegistry` so it survives `TerrainRebuildRequest`; write-through disk tier at `user/surfacetilecache`, ~107 KB/tile, temp-file+rename, no fsync), keyed by a per-request namespace that folds in `GENERATOR_VERSION`, the surface fingerprint, the body, and the **live** flatten regions — so a pad installed after spawn re-keys subsequent tiles instead of poisoning the cache. Payload shape (`TILE_RES`, halo, body radius) is validated on read, so drift or truncation degrades to a miss, never to wrong geometry. Motivation is measured: `tile_stream_bench` (new, `thalos_terrain`) puts the synthesis pipeline within ~20 % of the machine ceiling at ~870 tiles/s, so no thread-shape change can meaningfully shorten a cold fill — only not evaluating twice can. **Widening the outer synthesis pool was tried and reverted the same day**: the isolated bench prefers `cores − 2` whole-tile workers (1,035 vs 868 tiles/s) but in the running game mean per-tile wall time went ~6 ms → ~32 ms and the settle got *longer*, because the bench models an idle machine and the real one is also running the renderer, Avian, and udlod streaming a second body — the contention this pool is bounded to avoid. Note in `ground::tile_synthesis_pool`. **(g) don't stream the view the loading screen never shows** (found 2026-07-26): a runway/launch boot leaves the camera at the 200 km placeholder orbit until the deferred placement teleports it, and the streamer faithfully builds that orbital set first — ~2,600 tiles (~3 s of field eval) discarded the moment placement runs. Gating admission on `SurfaceSettle::placed` is the obvious fix and is **not** obviously safe: `finish_runway_spawn` waits for a `HeightSourceRegistry` entry before placing, so the ordering has a deadlock shape (the same constraint that parked BL-20260724T153622Z). Deferred rather than landed blind. **(e) incremental selection** — `select_leaves` walks all six faces and reallocates a ~11 k-key `HashSet` every frame, plus up to 16 2:1-balance passes with 4 probes per leaf; re-evaluate only nodes near the split/merge boundary. **(f) cheap experiment: fewer, larger tiles** — 129² at level L−1 covers the same ground as 65² at L with ¼ the entities and draw calls at equal vertex count, trading LOD granularity. Prior art to lift from: geometry clipmaps (Losasso–Hoppe) and CDLOD (Strugar) both delete the residency-churn bug class outright (no despawn grace, no bridge tiles, no cracks) but are a rewrite of the selection layer and awkward on a sphere — worth a read before committing to (b) | wip | L | — | ntr §6 · ADR-20260722T105147Z |
| NTR-X13 | **Tile residency had no memory budget → `DeviceLost` (out of memory)** (user 2026-07-25, ~90 s of repeated surface↔orbit flight on `just game launch`: "why did we run out of memory"; user direction: "we should have a memory budget for tiles"). One tile mesh is **347 KiB** of VRAM (4,481 verts × 56 B + 26,112 U32 indices) and the only cap in the renderer was `MAX_IN_FLIGHT = 24` — *generation concurrency*, not residency — so residency was whatever the geometric selector asked for, unconditionally, right after `SPLIT_FACTOR` went 3→6 (4× tiles) with the rugged cap at 18. Stacked under it: udlod's near-tier atlas is allocated **upfront** (`depth_or_array_layers: atlas_size` ≈ **890 MB/body**) and this run allocated it for Thalos too, because residency spawned 1.2 s before the tile driver claimed the body. **Landed:** (a) a **residency gauge** — `tile terrain residency:` every 5 s (resident / MiB / pending / desired / retiring / split scale) plus `tile_resident`/`tile_mib`/`tile_split_scale` in `mem_diag`'s JSONL; the pre-existing log counted only *cumulative* landings, so a 19,200-landing session was equally consistent with a 1 GiB working set and a 6 GiB one — the reason the crash was undiagnosable from the log; (b) a **byte-denominated budget** (`TILE_MESH_BYTES`, 4 GiB default, `THALOS_TILE_BUDGET_MB`, `0` disables) — bytes not tiles for the reason `tile_cache`'s payload budget already records, and set above every *measured* working set (worst known: NTR-X12's 10,900-tile 30 m Mira descent = 3.6 GiB) so the brake cannot silently coarsen a capture-verified framing; (c) the brake **bites in selection, never eviction** — the despawn rule is hole-free, so evicting residents would punch holes and blocking admission would deadlock (nothing retires until its replacement lands), whereas coarsening desire makes the ancestor desired and the fine tiles retire through the normal merge certificate; (d) the legacy atlas is **no longer allocated and thrown away** (`tile_claim_pending()` holds the dominant body's near-tier udlod spawn until the claim resolves, self-releasing after 10 s so an unresolved `ViewAnchor` degrades to legacy terrain rather than no ground). Tests: the byte denominator is asserted against a really-built mesh (an added attribute fails the test instead of the GPU — NTR-RT1's TANGENT is the next one), plus scale monotonicity, the controller's brake/deadband/recover, and disabled-budget parity. **Ruled out, so nobody re-derives it:** the churned meshes do *not* leak — bevy_render keys render-world removal on `AssetEvent::Unused`, and the slab allocator frees empty slabs. **Recurred 2026-07-25 20:08 UTC with the budget in the tree, and the diagnostics settled it: the budget was per *process*.** Two full game instances were live for the 10.8 min up to the crash (pids 10560 + 8376, dying three seconds apart — the card giving out, not one process misbehaving), each entitled to the whole 4 GiB, neither ever braking (`split_scale` 1.00 in both) because each was individually far inside its own share at 735/556 MiB. An earlier pair the same day peaked at 3.57 GiB of tiles *concurrently*. Fixed by making the budget a **machine-wide** figure divided by the live renderer count (`tiles::vram_share` — mtime heartbeats under the temp dir, stale after 10 s; timestamps not locks, because the OOM ends in an abort that runs no destructor). A single instance is bit-identical to before, so no capture-verified framing moves; two instances get half each. The gauge now reports `instances`, so a budget that suddenly halves reads as a peer starting rather than the brake misbehaving. **Open, and the reason this is `verify` not `done`:** the crash came at ~1,966 km AGL *after* the churn, at a framing needing few tiles, while peak residency was minutes earlier near the surface — a shape that suggests accumulation on top of the working set. The recurrence sharpens this rather than closing it: tiles were 1.29 GiB of ~12 GB at the moment of death, so tile residency was **not** the dominant consumer either time. Two candidates remain: mesh-allocator slabs pinned by stragglers (a slab frees only when fully empty, and they run to 512 MiB) and per-visit scatter rebuilds. The differential is a `THALOS_MEM_DIAG=1` run with `--features gpu-counters` over the same flight, plus `THALOS_NO_SCATTER=1` to bisect scatter — tile MiB plateauing while `render_meshes`/`wgpu_buffers` climb means the ground is inside budget and the growth is elsewhere | verify | M | **user:** repeat the flight that crashed — expect no OOM; paste the `tile terrain residency:` lines (is `split scale` ever below 1.00, and does it recover to 1.00 in level flight?). Then the `THALOS_MEM_DIAG=1` + `gpu-counters` run for the accumulation half | INC-20260725T012104Z-tile-residency-had-no-budget · ntr §6 |

| NTR-X15 | **Tile ground jittered a decimetre against everything built on it** (user 2026-07-25, following an ascending craft: "the runway and roads etc are still z fighting with the ground"; discriminators: only while the sim runs, stable-but-wrong when paused — "either submerged or on top, it doesn't settle"). Not z-fighting: tiles were `ChildOf` the body's **rotating** big_space grid, so their planet-radius body offset was rotated to world by the grid's **f32** `Transform.rotation`. f32 quat ULP at 3,186 km = mean 0.055 m / p95 0.165 m / worst 0.256 m, re-rolled every frame — straddling the 0.12 m asphalt lift, hence the binary flip. The trap was already documented twice (`update_runway_transform`, `transforms.rs`) and every other consumer dodged it; the tile module doc claimed the opposite. Fixed by placing tiles on the **root** grid in f64 each frame from `surface_orientation_authored`, leaving f32 to act only on in-tile offsets (≤0.04 m), driver pinned `.after(sync_solar_system_state).before(TileStreamSet)`. **Compile + 17 tests + capture clean; the artefact is motion-only and no still can show it — needs an in-game ascent past the space center to confirm** | verify | M | — | INC-20260725T195500Z-tiles-rode-an-f32-body-rotation · ntr §6 |
| NTR-X17 | **Transient tile seams during fast orbit at altitude** (user 2026-07-31: "zooming far out and orbiting the camera very fast, I can sometimes see the terrain tiles having seams before it settles"). Mechanism: inter-level junction cracks — while streaming settles, the resident mosaic transiently juxtaposes tiles *levels* apart (freshly-revealed coarse ground beside lingering fine tiles), whose shared edges disagree by hundreds of metres, far past the old skirt formula's 150 m clamp, so the crack read as see-through seams until junctions collapsed back to ±1 level. Fix landed: every border vertex now hangs a radial skirt curtain to the body-wide floor sphere (`radius − height_range_m`), which no partner tile at any level can undercut — correct by construction, same vertex/index count, zero VRAM delta; walls bury under the neighbour's surface once settled. Tests: `tiles::skirt_tests` (junction-crack falsifier + floor-curtain invariant). Verify: reproduce the fast orbit + zoom-out and confirm cracks now read as terrain-coloured walls at worst (a settled aerial still, e.g. `spaceport-aerial`, should be pixel-uneventful) — capture lane was locked by a concurrent session at land time | verify | — | — | ntr §6 |
| BL-20260725T012104Z | **A placeholder trajectory can pull an 890 MB atlas for a body the player never visits** (found 2026-07-25 while accounting for the OOM in INC-20260725T012104Z, deliberately not fixed — it needs a decision, not a patch). In the crash log Pelagos was spawned at the **near** tier (`atlas size 384, lod 16`) at boot alongside Thalos, on the launch pad. `compute_wanted_residency` only reaches `Near` via rule 1 (dominant body = Thalos) or rule 2 (predicted encounters / close approaches inside `preload_lead_time_s`), so rule 2 fired — plausibly off the pre-placement orbital *placeholder* trajectory, before the craft is seated on the pad. Cost is ~890 MB of VRAM, and `despawn_debounce_s` is 30 s of **sim** time, which does not advance while the sim is paused (every scenario starts at warp 0×) — so it can persist indefinitely on a paused boot. Decide whether residency should read predictions at all before the craft's real state exists (a `CraftRegime`/placement gate), rather than tuning the debounce. **Decided and landed 2026-07-25** after the 20:08 UTC recurrence showed the stranded Pelagos atlas live in *both* concurrent instances (~1.7 GB wasted across the pair): rule 2 is gated on `placement_settled` — the `step::PLACEMENT` loading step, which `loading::steps_for` registers **only** for deferred-placement scenarios, so `orbit`/EVA keep the old behaviour bit-for-bit. Deliberately not `CraftRegimeState`: the regime resolver classifies the placeholder orbit perfectly happily, and the question is "is this state real yet", not "what is the craft doing".
