| BL-20260730T014719Z-orbit-difficulty | **Orbit was too cheap — realistic stage masses + nozzle back-pressure** (user 2026-07-30: "super small rockets can easily reach orbit… we want the physics to at least somewhat resemble reality"): two structural causes fixed. (1) Tank walls were 12 kg/m² bare skin → stage dry fractions ~2.6%, so one small stage carried ~12.7 km/s and single-stage orbit was trivial; raised to 30 kg/m² stage-structure areal density → real 4–8% fractions (Thalos orbit needs ~6.3–6.6 km/s: 5.3 km/s circular + losses). (2) Rockets ran vacuum thrust/Isp at the pad; added catalog `sea_level_isp` + `Engine::pressure_thrust_factor` — thrust falls linearly with ambient pressure at fixed mass flow, effective Isp follows, so vacuum bells (Boreas 380 s → 120 s at 1 atm) are no longer viable first stages. Consequences: Typhon re-rated 2.4 → 3.6 MN / 3 t dry (the old rating could not lift the Atlas once bottom-engine-only staging + back-pressure were priced in; stage-1 pad TWR now guarded in `atlas_sample_is_an_orbital_rocket`), and Saturn's first stage swapped Boreas → Typhon. Drag was investigated and ruled out as the cause (bubble applies Cd 1.0 on frontal area through the whole sub-Kármán ascent — if anything ~2× too draggy for a slender stack; follow-up candidate: shape-based Cd ~0.4 once feel is confirmed). Closes with a user launch session: `just game launch` Saturn to orbit should now demand real staging and a sane gravity turn, and a tiny single-stack should fall short of orbit | verify | — | — | aerodynamics.md · parts.ron |

(2) **Ground floor** — the camera's radius is clamped to the sampled terrain height + 2 m (`None` while the surface is cold, never a datum fallback). (3) **`freecam::panel`** — the speed with a real-world reference, a log-scale drag slider over the full 1 m/s–10 000 km/s range, both mode switches, and anchor/altitude readouts; `SliderFormat::Custom` added to `thalos_ui` for the log readout. | BL-20260729T064914Z-prediction-event-scan-resolution | **Decouple trajectory event scanning from render sampling.** The crossing scan and the render-sample density are one knob (`CoastRequest::sample_count_hint`), which under-resolves events two independent ways. **(1) Past one revolution:** a bound coast caps `target_time` at one period and then jumps to the horizon on a single *event-unchecked* straggler sample (`ship_propagator.rs:289-310`, `:487-505`), so every SOI entry on revolutions 2..N is invisible — a multi-revolution phasing intercept cannot be planned. Reproduced on the shipped `solar_system.ron`: a trans-Mira transfer returns `Horizon`/`StableOrbit` where a chunked walk of the identical trajectory finds `SoiEnter(Mira)` at 2.42 periods. Nothing chunks above this layer (`flight_plan.rs:541-558`, `:624`, `propagation.rs:135`, `events.rs:193-196`). **(2) Within a revolution:** the collision scan's step is bounded only by the render hint, so prediction probes the ground track ~1.2% of orbital radius apart (≈39 km on Thalos) while the live path probes metres — 13/24 missed impacts vs 0/24 in the repro. **Do NOT re-key `needs_subdivide` to altitude** (the filed fix): `path/\|q\|` is correct for its actual purpose, bounding Hermite arc error, and altitude-keying subdivides to `MIN_STEP_S` across a whole grazing pass. The fix is a `SampleMode { Render(n), EventsOnly }` on `CoastRequest` plus a physically-derived scan stride, which also lets `advance_vessel` stop building 32 samples/frame it discards. The one-period cap itself is deliberate and load-bearing — keep it. Contradicts `lib.rs:17-19`'s claim that the two paths "can never numerically diverge"; reachability for (2) is narrow (needs tall terrain narrower than ~1.2% of orbital radius) | next | M | — | reviews/20260729T063800Z-propagator-trial.md `propagator-astro-1`/`-2` · repro/ · simulation.md |
| BL-20260729T064914Z-collision-refine-unimodality | **`refine_collision` discards real terrain impacts.** It locates a detected impact with `golden_section_extremum` (`ship_propagator.rs:1111-1118` → `:966-994`), a bracket-shrinker that requires a **unimodal** objective; the objective embeds a procedural height field, which is not. On convergence to the wrong local minimum it returns `None` and the caller falls through to `samples.push(...)` (`:472-475`), throwing away a crossing detection already found. Measured over 720 scenarios: 10 `None` returns, **10 of them real crossings** — the "Hermite false positive" escape hatch never once fired legitimately, across four terrain configurations. Consequence is a *mislocated* impact (up to one step late, ~40 s / ~300 km at LEO speeds); 9 of 10 are re-caught by a later step, total loss 1 in 720. Fix: bisect on the bracket `interior_min_altitude` already found, **with a fallback** — that routine probes the Hermite curve while `refine_collision` roots the Kepler curve, so the sign change is a strong hint, not a guarantee. `:929-941` `refine_crossing` is NOT affected (unimodality holds for distance-to-sphere). Smaller than its sibling: the same sweep found 477 steps where a real root existed and detection never fired at all | next | S | sequence after BL-20260729T064914Z-prediction-event-scan-resolution (detection density dominates) | reviews/20260729T063800Z-propagator-trial.md `propagator-astro-4` · repro/astro-4-golden-section-replica.md |

| BL-20260729T073116Z-landing-gear-robustness | **Landing-gear & crash-contact robustness** (user 2026-07-29: hard landings regardless of flare; nose wheel buried in terrain with no brakes + long slide; craft frozen standing on a wingtip). Four fixes landed together (INC-20260729T073116Z): **asymmetric oleo damping** (soft ramped compression ζ 0.4, firm rebound ζ 1.2 — removes the touchdown damper step-spike that made every landing a slam); suspension ray starts `ray_start_lift_m` **above** the strut top + analytic `HeightSource` fallback (a hull driven below the surface can't silently unload its wheels and lose brakes); `terrain_floor_backstop`'s velocity correction became a **contact impulse (normal + Coulomb friction, with torque) at the deepest hull support point** (a wingtip-first arrival topples flat instead of freezing in attitude); `detect_terrain_impact` measures radial **sink rate**, not full speed — which un-masks the crash tolerance, restored from the TEMP 1e9 to 12 m/s. **Round 2 (user play session, same day — wheels still visually sink; taxi tip-overs; spoilers up while parked):** (a) **ground control regime** — with weight on wheels, reaction-wheel roll/yaw torque is masked to zero (`wheel_torque_ground_mask`, applied identically in `control_bus` normalization and `apply_local_forces` realization; pitch kept for takeoff rotation); (b) **spoilers airspeed-gated** — deploy ≥30 m/s, stow <20 m/s (hysteresis), so the B latch is wheel-brakes-only when parked/taxiing; (c) **visual strut swallow** — `sync_gear_compression` slides each gearbox mesh up by its live compression (`GearVisualCompression`), wheels stay on the pavement. **Round 3 (user report: belly-on-runway at 86 kn, "stuck at full compression", recovered only by lifting off — screenshot was from the pre-fix session, but two real gaps fixed):** (d) **destroyed craft kept their floor** — `terrain_floor_backstop` + `apply_surface_friction` skipped `is_destroyed`, so once the 12 m/s tolerance made destruction reachable a crashed wheeled craft (no gear forces, hull layer-filtered from ground) would have fallen through the planet; both now run for wrecks, which settle and grind to a stop on the hull-contact impulse; (e) **instrumented the exact failure**: 1 Hz `gear_contact` + `backstop_intervention` events (tooling.md) and a tested `just diag` check `gear_carried_by_backstop` that names the belly-slide signature (gear down, zero weight-on-wheels, backstop carrying) | verify | — | **user:** `just game runway` + a landing: (1) parked spawn → spoilers stowed, brakes hold; (2) taxi with full stick roll/yaw → craft must NOT tip; (3) takeoff rotation still works at speed; (4) gentle flared landing → progressive, no slam, wheels visibly on the pavement through the stroke; (5) hard nose-first touchdown → nose wheel keeps braking; (6) gear-up/over-banked → topples flat, no wingtip stand; (7) >12 m/s sink → destroyed | surface.md · surface_local.md · aerodynamics.md · INC-20260729T073116Z |

| BL-20260730T010253Z-per-domain-display-units | **Display units resolve per domain, not from one global switch** (user 2026-07-30: "when flying a plane, i generally prefer feet and knots… alongside the global switch, on metric, we can choose to use imperial for areas where that is classically the case"). Aviation is unit-conservative in a way the rest of the world is not — feet, knots, ft/min, and nmi are the *instrument* units in metric countries too — so `UnitsSettings` gained `aviation: AviationUnits` (`Aeronautical` \| `FollowGlobal`, default `Aeronautical`) and **`UnitsSettings::system_for(UnitDomain)` is now the one canonical accessor**; reading `.system` from a panel is a defect. `UnitDomain::Aviation` = PFD tapes/readouts, atmo TAS/q/Mach pill, MFD nav display; `::General` = orbital altitude/apsides, Δv, staging mass, map scales. **The instrument picks its domain, never the craft or the regime** (regime- and craft-class-driven resolution considered and rejected: a tape whose unit changes mid-climb is worse than one consistently in the "wrong" system, and regime coupling drags `CraftRegime` into the HUD). **Round 2 (user played it 2026-07-30 — screenshot confirmed the aviation instruments correct, and found the gap):** `ALTITUDE 974 m` and `SURFACE VELOCITY 93 m/s` are the two readouts "the instrument picks its domain" *cannot* decide, because those widgets serve both worlds — the same box shows apoapsis on a transfer and approach height on final. Added `UnitDomain::shared(airplane_flight)`, resolved from `FlightContext::airplane_flight()` = `in_atmosphere && winged`, where `winged` is the live `AeroConfig::lift_slope > 0` (no craft class exists; this is the honest proxy). So a **plane** in air reads ft/kn, a **rocket** in the same air keeps m/s, and everything in vacuum keeps the global system — the user's "type of thing **and** situation". The whole altitude cluster (current altitude *and* AP/PE) resolves through one call so the side-by-side panels can't disagree. Note this partly reverses round 1's blanket "never the regime" rule: it holds for instruments that *have* a static answer, and cannot for shared widgets. Also collapsed the duplicated `IN_ATMOSPHERE_DENSITY` (atmo panel + MFD had their own copies) into one `hud::` constant. **Shipyard wired in the same round** (`UnitDomain::General` — the editor is not a flight instrument, so it follows the global switch): `shipyard_editor::core::format` no longer converts anything, its `format_delta_v` was a byte-for-byte duplicate of `hud::format::delta_v` and is deleted, `format_mass_kg` became `hud::format::mass_large` (vehicle-scale tiering, deliberately coarser than the HUD's stage readout); new `hud::format` fns `length` / `length_display` / `thrust` / `area` / `impulse` / `mass_large`; top bar, staging panel, palette summaries, and the inspector's 18 inline unit strings all routed through them. Two live-update paths were needed: the **palette is built once at Startup** (new `PalettePartSummary` marker + `refresh_summaries` gated on `units.is_changed()`) and the **inspector bakes its unit into `SliderFormat` at spawn**, so `UnitSystem` joined `InspectorKey`. The edit sliders convert display-only via a new unit-agnostic `thalos_ui::SliderFormat::Scaled { factor, suffix, decimals }` — stored value, range, and step stay SI, so no snapping or round-trip change (**`cargo check -p thalos_ui --all-targets` is green**, including `kitchen_sink`). Fixed on contact: the atmo panel's inline `psf` literal moved into `hud::format::dynamic_pressure`; the ND was **fully metric** and is now nmi/ft (`ground_distance`/`ground_range`/`altitude_delta` replace its private `fmt_range`/`fmt_distance`, deleted); the PFD's `APPR RWY … km` was hardcoded while the tapes beside it were unit-aware; and the **V/S box had no unit label at all** — now a second line under `V/S`, which matters more once the unit varies independently of the global setting. `format.rs` + `units_settings.rs` gained **15 tests** pinning every conversion factor against its definition (1 nmi = 1852 m, 1 ft = 0.3048 m, 1 lbf = 4.448222 N, 1 m² = 10.7639 ft²), the metric↔imperial magnitude agreement, the slider-vs-string factor agreement, and the full resolution matrix — **all 15 pass** (run standalone via a rustc harness). **`just check` was blocked in both rounds by other agents' in-flight work**, a *different* set each time: round 1 an untracked `rendering/flow.rs` + a `rendering/mod.rs` declaring a nonexistent `reentry` module + `reflection_probe.rs`; round 2 a half-landed `CASCADE_COUNT` 3→4 bump (`body_material.rs`) whose consumers in `sun_shadow.rs`/`grass.rs`/`ground_terrain.rs` still declare 3-element arrays. So the game crate never compiled end to end. Both times rustc reported **body-level** type errors (E0599/E0308/E0063) in *those* files, i.e. name resolution and body type-checking ran crate-wide and flagged nothing in the ~20 files changed here — strong evidence, but inference, not a green build. `thalos_ui` (the slider change) IS verified green. Clippy unrun on the game crate | verify | S–M | **user:** re-run `just check` once the worktree is clean, then `just game runway-approach` with Measurement = Metric. Flying the plane: PFD speed/alt/V-S tapes in kn/ft/(ft·min⁻¹) with the V/S unit labelled, atmo pill in kn + psf, ND range/DTG/XTK in nmi + GS error in ft, **and now ALTITUDE in ft + SURFACE VELOCITY in kn** — while Δv and staging stay metric. Then check the contrast cases: a **rocket** in the same atmosphere must keep m/s and metres (the winged test), and in orbit everything returns to metric. Flip Flight instruments → "Follow global" and confirm all of it goes metric. Finally `just game shipyard` with Measurement = Imperial: masses in lb, dimensions and sliders in ft, thrust in lbf, wing area in ft² — and toggle the setting with the editor open to confirm the palette summaries and inspector sliders re-render live | architecture.md §units_settings · hud/format.rs · units_settings.rs |
| BL-20260730T222419Z-stuck-drag-ate-throttle-keys | **Shift/Ctrl stopped ramping the throttle mid-session** (user 2026-07-30: "shift and ctrl don't work for throttle anymore" — everything else fine, sim running, Z/X still snapping). Not an input-binding problem: Shift/Ctrl are the only flight keys also bound in a *higher-priority* context, and `GameManeuverPrecisionContext` (priority 90, `consume_input: true`) eats them above `GameFlightContext` (20) whenever `InteractionMode` is a drag. The mode could be stranded there permanently, two ways: the `Pointer<DragEnd>` observers only fire for a **live** hitbox (a handle despawned mid-drag takes the event with it — `manage_arrow_handles` guards only the one case its author hit), and `maneuver_input`'s release check sat **below six early returns** (no window, cursor off-window, no `ActiveCamera`/`SimulationState`/`SolarSystemState`, and `prediction() == None` — which is the *normal* state for an atmospheric aircraft) plus a `not_game_paused` gate. Release the button with any of those true and the drag never ended. **Fixed by giving the invariant its own system**: `end_drag_on_release` runs unconditionally, outside the pause gate, before the context sync — it reads only the intent and the mode, so it has no early return to hide behind and no state where it declines to run; the per-arm release handling in `maneuver_input` is deleted (which also drops its needless `ManeuverPlan` write-lock). Removes the class: any future early return added to that system, and any new mid-drag despawn, is now harmless. 4 tests pin the invariant in a world holding *none* of the resources `maneuver_input` needs — the exact state the release used to be lost in. Two hypotheses ruled out by one fact (the HOTAS lever's reclaim `return` sits *above* the Z/X branch, so a reclaiming lever would have killed Z/X too; the paused-clock path needs a paused sim): the stick, and `SimClock` — the latter split out as its own row below | verify | S | **user:** `just game runway` (or any mode) — Shift/Ctrl should ramp the throttle again. To confirm the mechanism rather than just the absence: open map view, drag a maneuver arrow, let go with the cursor **outside the window**, return to flight — Shift/Ctrl must still work, and right-drag camera orbit must still work (a stuck mode blocks both from the same resource) | INC-20260730T222419Z · maneuver/interaction/input.rs `end_drag_on_release` · gameplay/input.md §Context Model |
| BL-20260730T222420Z-throttle-ramp-frozen-while-paused | **The Shift/Ctrl throttle ramp is scaled by *sim* time, so it does nothing at warp 0×.** `handle_throttle_input` ramps by `THROTTLE_RAMP_RATE * clock.delta_secs_f64()` (fuel.rs:301) and `SimClock` returns exactly 0 under every pause source — including warp 0×, which is how **every spawn scenario starts** (`spawn::apply_initial_warp`). Z/X are snaps and don't touch `dt`, so on the pad the two throttle controls disagree: the snap works, the ramp is dead. Throttle is a *control input*, not a simulated quantity — KSP lets you set it while paused, and the pre-launch throttle set is the canonical reason to want it. Found while diagnosing the sibling row above and ruled out as *its* cause (that report was with the sim running), so this is unverified against a user complaint — but it is the same defect shape either way. **Do:** ramp on `Time<Real>` (presentation/UI animation already does; CLAUDE.md's sim-pause boundary says UI and presentation use real time) and check nothing downstream assumes `commanded` only changes on a sim tick. Cheap; the only judgement call is whether a paused throttle change should dirty the prediction | next | XS | — | fuel.rs:301 `handle_throttle_input` · sim_clock.rs `sync_sim_clock` · CLAUDE.md §One sim-pause boundary |
| BL-20260729T064914Z-burn-frame-divergence | **Prediction tracks the burn frame; the autopilot flies it inertially fixed.** `rk4_burn_step` re-derives the PRN basis from instantaneous state inside every k1..k4 stage (`ship_propagator.rs:797-813`), so predicted thrust rotates with velocity. Execution latches one world direction at ignition (`autopilot.rs:647-652`; the `Burn` arm destructures with `..` and touches only throttle, and `attitude_target()` bypasses `NavigationMode::ManeuverNode`), and terminates on the direction-blind scalar `delivered_dv` (`simulation.rs:998-1001`). Measured, accounting for burns being *centred* on `node.time` so the frozen direction is the midpoint prograde: 250 kN/75 kg·s⁻¹, 2000 m/s from 200 km → 4.09° arc, 0.082% Δv error, 5.7 km apoapsis on 24,674 km (0.023%); at 25 kN/7.5 kg·s⁻¹ → 40.9° arc, 1.78% Δv, 534 km apoapsis. Small today, grows linearly with burn duration, worst for low-thrust stages — and no Thalos stage with that TWR has been shown to exist. **Not a spec violation:** `simulation.md`'s `BurnFrameBehavior`/`ManeuverFrame` are marked "target design, unbuilt" (`:8-29`, `:872-924`), so the original spec-compliance framing was refuted. Pick one behaviour deliberately and make both halves use it | later | S | — | reviews/20260729T063800Z-propagator-trial.md `propagator-astro-5` |

| BL-20260730T225312Z-shadow-near-cascade | **Near-field shadow resolution: a 4th (near) cascade + boxes square on the ground** (user 2026-07-30, runway chase screenshot: "why is the shadow so pixelated… I want much higher fidelity shadows in general"). Two causes, only the second non-obvious: cascade 0 was ±400 m = 0.22 m/texel, coarser than the gear/wing/panel the camera looks at; **and** the box was square in the LIGHT plane, so projecting ground→light compresses the sun azimuth by `sin(elev)` and the ground texel came out ~3.9× coarser along the shadow's length at the shot's ~15° sun (≈0.85 m) — the elongated staircase that was actually reported. PCSS was not at fault and could not help: the true penumbra at ~30 m separation is 0.14 m, *below one texel*, so it correctly took its sharp path; the contact tier marches 0.6 m by design. Landed: `CASCADE_COUNT` 3→4 with the new cascade at the **near** end (±64 m, ~0.03 m/texel); V half-extent scaled by `sin(elev)` so the ground footprint is square and the ground texel isotropic; explicit per-cascade up-sun caster-height budget (`CASCADE_MAX_CASTER_M`) replacing the old implicit one (which rasterized casters up to ~`half` TALL — 4 km for the outer cascade); azimuth-aligned light basis, transport kept only for the near-zenith degenerate case; craft-local mode opts out (no ground plane in orbit) and runs 2 cascades so a tall stack still fits. Predicted near-field ground texel at a 15° sun **0.85 m → 0.086 m**. Cost: +1 depth pass, +67 MB VRAM; draw cost near-nil (the new box is 64 m). Binding convention: field name = cascade index, binding number = each material's next free slot (renumbering ten shaders expressed an order nothing reads). `cargo check` + `clippy` clean, diag tests pass, all 10 shader call sites audited for 4-map arity. **Capture-verified in part:** `runway-atmosphere`, `spaceport-aerial`, `paved-ground`, `massif-valley` all render with **no shader/pipeline error in the runtime lane** (BL-20 checked explicitly) and tiles fully resident; `massif-valley` ridges still self-shadow with no acne or banding, i.e. no mid/far regression. **The rig's own gauge is the quantitative evidence**, measured live at a 12.9° sun (`sun_sin_elev` 0.223, `active_cascades` 4): `cascade0_texel_u_m` **0.219 → 0.035**, and the ground texel ALONG the shadow — the axis that produced the reported staircase — **0.98 m → 0.107 m, a 9.2× improvement**. | BL-20260729T064742Z-plume-view-ray | **The plume became a volume: view-ray integral replaces the cylindrical billboard** (user 2026-07-29: "engine plume is just a flat billboard… sometimes the illusion falls apart at certain angles"). Reproduced first on an elevation sweep of the `plume` preset: clean at 1°, a flat triangular flap with hard straight edges at 80°, and at 90° — straight down the exhaust — **the plume disappeared entirely**. Structural, not tuning: a strip locked to the exhaust axis and rotated to face the camera covers *no pixels* once the view lines up with that axis, which is exactly where a real plume is brightest (the line of sight runs the whole column). The old "degenerates to a sliver, which is correct end-on" fallback could not help. Two lesser angle defects fell out with it: the perpendicular-chord assumption under-estimated path length by ~1/sin(view-to-axis), so the column thinned exactly where it should thicken; and turbulence azimuth came from the billboard's own lateral coordinate (`asin(x)`), so the whole eddy field counter-rotated with the camera. **Fix:** a closed proxy prism sized by a conservative `bound_radius` (rasterization bound only, never seen, front faces kept so each ray shades once), and a fragment that marches the *actual* view ray. The emission model is untouched — the compact-support profiles become local densities whose perpendicular chord integrals are the exact closed forms they replaced, so side-on accumulates the same optical depth; core/sheath keep independent transmittances. Turbulence now samples true engine-fixed azimuth through a period-wrapped noise (integer periods 44/94/22, or a stationary seam appears down one meridian). One measured consequence handled: `pow(fc,3)` was authored against a per-chord `fc` and dilutes to 0.547 of its on-axis peak inside the integral (visibly softer shock train in matched captures) — `SHOCK_NODE_MARCH_GAIN` restores it analytically, not by eye | verify | M | **agent:** angle sweep captured before/after (`artifacts/visual/runs/plume-angle{,-fixed}/`): 80° flap → rounded foreshortened glow, 90° absent → bright annulus around the hull, side-on unchanged incl. shock cells. **BLOCKED:** `plume-skyline` (the composite-ordering probe plume.md requires for any plume geometry/blending change) could not be run — the capture lane hit a GPU OOM on that terrain-heavy scene (BL-20260729T014529Z) and quarantined. **user:** run it, plus a play session for the orbiting-camera cue, which is the half of the defect a still cannot show | plume.md §The view-ray integral |
| BL-20260730T014500Z-flow-signals | **One aerothermal signal boundary for every vehicle flow effect** (`rendering::flow::FlowSignals`). Contrails, plumes, reentry plasma, vapour cones, heat haze and pad dust are all participating media attached to a vehicle driven by the same freestream numbers; without one boundary each re-derives ambient conditions and they disagree exactly when several are on screen (reentry with the engine lit). Publishes density/pressure/temperature/speed-of-sound/airspeed/Mach/q/stagnation-temperature/Sutton–Graves heat flux plus flow direction (render *and* craft-local) and craft bounds; single writer `update_flow_signals`. **Deliberately not derived from `AeroReadout`** — that only exists inside the Avian bubble, which is precisely where reentry is not, so this resolves from canonical ship state and is valid orbit-to-touchdown with no regime switch. Airspeed is relative to the **co-rotating** airmass (spin is a few percent of entry speed and heating goes as v³). The plume now takes its ambient half from here instead of its own atmosphere lookup, and the proxy-hull mesh builder is shared. `FlowDebugOverride` overrides **inputs**, not derived quantities, and cannot override environmental presence: `in_atmosphere` comes only from the real atmosphere sample at the craft's real altitude. **2026-07-31 correction:** `flow_from_*` now publishes the upstream side (`air_relative`), not the air's craft-space velocity (`-air_relative`); the old double inversion put live vapour/reentry geometry ahead of a vehicle while the hand-authored probe looked correct (INC-20260731T003643Z) | verify | M | **agent:** focused flow suite passes (6/6), including upstream sign, debug density unable to create an atmosphere, heat flux needing both speed *and* density, and the sign of the R_n dependence. | BL-20260730T014501Z-reentry-shock-layer | **Reentry shock layer** (`rendering::reentry` + `reentry.wgsl`): the plume's emission chain on a hull-fitted ellipsoidal shell, ray-marched, driven entirely by `FlowSignals` — heat flux sets radiance, stagnation temperature sets colour, density sets optical depth, Mach sets fade-in and standoff. Shell is a plain identity-parented child with the freestream as a **craft-local uniform**, so nothing is counter-rotated and no query touches the craft `Transform`. Load-bearing: the Wien reference *equals* the 11 000 K real-gas cap (ideal gas gives 36 000 K at Mach 25; without both halves every entry above ~Mach 8 blows out to identical flat white); compact support at **both** shell ends; the march stops at the body so the leeward shock is not painted over the hull. **NOT DONE — the shell is fitted to a wrong body measurement.** The bounds sweep resolves a near-cubic 2×2×2 m box from only 3 descendant meshes on a visibly elongated craft, so the glow streams off one end instead of wrapping the windward side (capture: `artifacts/visual/latest/reentry.png`). | BL-20260730T024500Z-vapor-cone | **Transonic vapour cone** (`rendering::vapor_cone` + `vapor_cone.wgsl`): the condensation collar around an airframe near Mach 1, as a sunlit scattering shell. Third consumer of `FlowSignals`, and the one that breaks the pattern the plume and shock layer set — **it is not an emitter**. The visible cloud is water droplets scattering sunlight, so the source term is illumination through a Henyey–Greenstein forward-scatter lobe (which is most of the look: bright toward the sun, soft grey away from it) and the material *blends* rather than adds, because a cloud occludes what is behind it. Shading it like the plume gives a self-lit cone that looks identical at midnight. **Also not the shock** — the shock is invisible; the cloud is condensation behind the expansion, which is why it lives in a window around Mach 1 (0.75→1.35, peak 0.92–1.05) rather than everywhere supersonic. Three gates must open at once — transonic Mach, humidity ≥ 0.35, q ≥ 8 kPa — and that conjunction is why it reads as a photographed moment rather than permanent decoration. Adds `relative_humidity_frac` to `FlowSignals` as a **named stand-in** (exponential altitude profile; the atmosphere model has no water vapour) — the cloud system's weather field replaces it, and contrail persistence will want the same signal. **2026-07-31 correction:** live direction is fixed at the shared producer; the probe now boots the atmospheric cruise instead of manufacturing dense air around an orbiting craft. It records transition-only `vapor_cone_state` events, and `just diag` reports a configured probe that ends with any gate closed | verify | S | **agent:** vapor suite passes 9/9; diagnostic reader passes 2/2; focused flow suite passes 6/6. | BL-20260730T024501Z-flow-craft-bounds | **Craft bounds for flow effects, and the shock layer's luminous envelope.** Root-caused the reentry shell's mis-fit through three wrong guesses, each killed by a capture: not the optical-depth normalisation, not the windward field (a debug render showed it correct), but (1) half-extents measured as `max(|corner|)` **about the craft origin** — a rocket's origin is not its centre, so the fitted ellipsoid bulged as far past the nose as the hull reached behind it and that phantom cap glowed; (2) the sweep folding the effects' own proxy hulls (the shell measured itself) — fixed with `FlowProxyMesh`; (3) **a permanent latch on a consistently-wrong measurement** — the sweep runs in `Sync` but transform propagation is `PostUpdate`, so a child spawned that frame folds in near the origin, giving a box that is wrong, small and perfectly stable, satisfying both 'every mesh folded' and 'stopped growing'. Measured: consecutive captures of one scenario resolved the same craft as 2.0x5.6x2.0 m and 2.0x2.0x2.0 m with identical mesh counts — the vehicle's size was nondeterministic run to run. Now a true min/max AABB with a published centre, plus periodic re-arming. **Separately: the standoff is not the glow.** Modelling only the shock gap put an 8 cm sheath on a 4 m craft — the right number for a shock position, invisible as an effect. The luminous envelope (shock layer + thermal boundary layer + hot afterbody gas) is now its own quantity, several times the standoff and floored against body size; the standoff keeps shape, Mach response, and where the layer peaks | verify | S | **agent:** `just screenshot reentry` now shows an amber layer hugging the windward side, bright at the leading edge, sweeping aft. | MIRA-V7 | **Large craters are far too shallow to read at survey scale.** `terrain_baker diagnose` now profiles across the highest-legibility landmark: r 25.7 km, age 0.32 Gyr, degradation retained 0.977 (i.e. essentially undegraded), record depth 639.9 m / rim 1133.6 m, **measured rim-to-floor 820.9 m across a 51 km-wide feature — a depth:diameter ratio of 1.6%**. Real complex craters run 4–10% (Copernicus 3.8 km / 93 km). At the rim preset's 74 km standoff that dip subtends <1°, which is why three successive framing fixes all still rendered open plain. The crater exists in the height field and is not degradation-limited; the authored depth law itself is the constraint. Fix belongs in the compatibility producer's crater profile (`crater_profile.rs` / `morphology_for_radius`), and must weigh gameplay + collision + whole-body look, so it wants a decision before a patch. **Root cause found and fixed 2026-07-22:** the *complex* branch of `crater_dimensions` used `depth = 196 * D^0.301`, i.e. the **simple**-crater `d/D = 0.196` pasted into the complex formula while keeping the complex exponent. Pike (1977) complex is `d = 1.044 D^0.301` km. The rim coefficient beside it (236, from `0.236 D^0.399` km) was always right, which is what made the mismatch visible. Fixed to 1044; peak-ring and multi-ring rescaled (1342 / 1785) for continuity at the D=137 km and D=300 km boundaries; simple→complex now blends over a transition band because a hard switch left a 2x depth cliff. Verified: Copernicus-scale D=93 km now 4.09 km vs ~3.8 km measured; the probe landmark went 639.9 m → 3624 m record depth and 820.9 m → 3736.9 m measured rim-to-floor (d/D 1.6% → 7.3%). `mira-approach` now shows a large crater with a lit rim and shadowed interior where it previously showed none. **Still open:** the `mira-rim` close framing does not yet compose — with the landmark now chosen by surviving relief it lands on a deep bowl and frames the smooth floor. | BL-34 | **`mira-eva` renders black.** The canonical eye-level probe returns an all-black frame with only a thin lit horizon rim; a bright body sits *below* the horizon line, suggesting the site is unlit. **Not caused by the BL-33 lighting-normal work** — matched captures with the shaders stashed to HEAD are byte-comparable black (`artifacts/visual/runs/bl33_eva/`). **Both benign explanations are falsified — this is a real render bug.** Instrumented 2026-07-22: the site logs **solar incidence 34.6° (lit)** — a high sun, not night; and `terrain_baker diagnose` reports camera clearance 0.71–114.38 m over 64 azimuths, so the eye is above the surface, not buried. Lit ground at eye level is rendering black while a thin horizon rim stays bright. Leading candidate is `terrain_self_shadow`'s horizon march (W12): at a 1.7 m eye height the marched horizon is extremely close, so a small bias/step error would shadow every fragment while leaving distant terrain that pokes above the march unaffected — which matches the bright-rim-over-black-foreground signature exactly. **ROOT CAUSE PINNED 2026-07-22 — it is a geometry/height-parity bug, not shading.** The self-shadow hypothesis was wrong on inspection (that march is per-fragment, so it would darken `mira-approach` identically; it does not). Two typed comparisons settle it: **`terrain-lighting`** shows *fullbright* rendering only sparse slivers rather than solid ground (MAE 1.28/255, 2.6% px) — the inverse of INC-0009, where fullbright proved complete opaque coverage — so there is almost no terrain geometry in front of the camera; and **`terrain-culling`** shows *two-sided* revealing large terrain swathes that backface culling hides (MAE 7.73/255, 7.8% px). **The EVA eye is inside the rendered mesh, seeing culled backfaces.** The eye is placed from the CPU `HeightSource` (`diagnose` reports 0.71 m minimum clearance) while the rendered mesh sits higher at that point, so a metre-scale CPU-vs-GPU height disagreement buries a 1.7 m eye. Scattered rather than uniform patches in the two-sided frame match a mesh weaving above and below eye height. This is the CPU/GPU height-parity tolerance MIRA-3's exit criteria already names — fix the parity, do not paper over it by raising the eye. Evidence: `artifacts/visual/runs/bl34_eva/`. **PARKED 2026-07-22 — superseded by the renderer replacement.** The udlod custom render path is being replaced with a Bevy/`big_space`-native renderer tailored to diffusion terrain, and this defect lives entirely inside udlod's tile/LOD machinery, so debugging it is work on a layer being deleted. **Keep the falsifications** — they are equally true of the new renderer and save re-deriving them: the cause is NOT package ancestor-fallback shear (rebaked at a 10 m prune budget, 0% pruned, max declared error 0.026 m, frame unchanged), NOT backface culling at grazing range (two-sided at 50 m eye height is MAE 0.08/255), and NOT `cull_behind_view` (notches survive with it disabled). The eye-height signature is the useful reproduction: 1.7 m black / 50 m serrated horizon / 2000 m clean, i.e. the failure scales with how grazing the view is. Re-open against the new renderer only if it reproduces. | later | S | — | MIRA-V7 · INC-0009 · `eva_surface_context` |
| BL-35 | **Mira terrain silhouette is torn — black slivers where faces should be** (user in-game frames 2026-07-22; Thalos never shows it). Ranked hypotheses, all Mira-specific: **(1)** package ancestor-fallback shear — the pyramid declares a **256 m fallback budget** with per-level max declared error 226/255/246 m, so adjacent tiles resolving at different levels can differ by ~250 m of height; at a grazing view that is a black gash along a seam, and Thalos cannot reproduce it because `ProceduralSurface` is continuous so neighbours agree exactly. **(2)** the global `approximate_height` scalar: `tile_tree.rs` samples *one* height under the camera per frame and `vertex.wgsl` uses it for every vertex's `approximate_view_distance`, which drives `compute_morph` — standing 3.6 km down a crater floor mis-estimates distance for all distant vertices, so neighbouring tiles morph to inconsistent LODs and crack. Mira's relative relief is 3x Thalos's (±6147 m/869 km = 0.71% vs ±7445 m/3186 km = 0.23%) and MIRA-V7 increased it. **(3)** backface culling is why the cracks read black, not why they exist (the `terrain-culling` compare fills them two-sided). **Very likely the same root cause as BL-34**, where the EVA eye sat inside the mesh at 0.71 m clearance — what a few-hundred-metre seam shear does to a 1.7 m eye. **Discriminator run 2026-07-22 — hypothesis (1) FALSIFIED.** Rebaked at a 10 m prune budget: 0% pruned at every level, max declared error 0.026–0.094 m (was 226–255 m), i.e. every ancestor fallback eliminated. The frame is unchanged. Package shear is not the cause; budget reverted to 256 m. **BL-34 and BL-35 are the same bug.** An eye-height bracket at the identical site is decisive: 1.7 m = entirely black, 50 m = renders but the horizon is visibly serrated with dark notches (reproducing the user's in-game frames), 2000 m = nearly clean. So terrain fails along the *silhouette* and the failure scales with how grazing the view is; at 1.7 m the whole frame is grazing, so everything tears out. Remaining candidates: per-tile bounding/frustum culling computed from a mean height (tiles whose terrain rises above the horizon get culled as below it), `cull_behind_view` on the surface tier, and the global `approximate_height` morph error. Next: pin `approximate_height` to the body mean, then disable `cull_behind_view`, capturing at 50 m where the notches are legible but the frame is not black. Evidence: `artifacts/visual/runs/bl34_eva/eye_{50,2000}m.png`. **PARKED 2026-07-22 — superseded by the renderer replacement.** The udlod custom render path is being replaced with a Bevy/`big_space`-native renderer tailored to diffusion terrain, and this defect lives entirely inside udlod's tile/LOD machinery, so debugging it is work on a layer being deleted. **Keep the falsifications** — they are equally true of the new renderer and save re-deriving them: the cause is NOT package ancestor-fallback shear (rebaked at a 10 m prune budget, 0% pruned, max declared error 0.026 m, frame unchanged), NOT backface culling at grazing range (two-sided at 50 m eye height is MAE 0.08/255), and NOT `cull_behind_view` (notches survive with it disabled). The eye-height signature is the useful reproduction: 1.7 m black / 50 m serrated horizon / 2000 m clean, i.e. the failure scales with how grazing the view is. Re-open against the new renderer only if it reproduces. | later | M | — | BL-34 · MIRA-V7 · `package.rs` fallback budget · `tile_tree.rs:824` · `vertex.wgsl:40` |

Disc and approach both inspected: smooth between craters, craters read as bowls. Remaining V2 scope: opposition surge, terminator response. | BL-33 | **Airless crater speckle — FIXED (lighting normals; height aliasing ruled out by measurement):** *(Renumbered from a colliding BL-24 — Track 3's perspective-handoff row was minted concurrently and is `wip`; this row kept no external references.)* sub-bake-threshold craters composed LOD-invariantly alias into dark ragged specks when a whole crater falls below one tile texel (visible in `mira-surface` at 46 km after the 2026-07-21 merge removed the f32-acos rim sawteeth). Next candidates, in order: (1) the shader-side airless normal/albedo path under grazing incidence — INC-0009 fixed the same symptom class there and the `mira-rim` grazing capture is far worse than the oblique one at the same range, which is a lighting-response signature not a geometry one; (2) the height-atlas relief normal (derivative/mip selection) as distinct from the height values themselves, which the audit shows are fine; (3) the Hapke response itself at high incidence. The LOD-invariance rule is **not** in tension after all — no ADR needed unless a later candidate reopens it | verify | M | — | feature_compositor.rs · INC-0009 · mira-surface probe · **2026-07-22: reproduced far more severely by `mira-disc`/`mira-approach`/`mira-rim`; MIRA-V6 shows it blocks the close crater framing outright.** **Height-aliasing hypothesis FALSIFIED by measurement** — `terrain_baker diagnose` gained an alias audit (point sample at texel spacing vs box mean of the same span at 1 m). Baseline residual is only 0.148 m @ 25 m texel and 5.0 m @ 400 m against 107 m of within-texel relief, i.e. <5%: the height field is already effectively band-limited, because within-texel relief is dominated by the smooth slope across it and a midpoint sample of a ramp equals its mean. Three candidate fixes (footprint-faded crater compose; threshold-faded fBm octaves; sinc box-transfer fBm octaves) each made the residual 5–10× **worse** and were reverted. Search redirects to the **shading** path — normals/albedo — matching INC-0009, whose identical symptom class was also not geometry and was fixed shader-side. **ROOT CAUSE FOUND AND FIXED 2026-07-22 — it was the lighting normals, three compounding attenuations of resolved macro relief:** (1) `HEIGHT_NORMAL_WEIGHT` 0.35 applied only a third of the height atlas's normal; (2) `stable_normal` pulled another 15% back to the sphere; (3) `shade_hapke_surface` clamped n·l against **body curvature** plus a headroom that collapses to its 0.05 floor below `geo_n_dot_l` 0.15 — at 83° incidence that caps n·l near 0.17, so a crater wall tilted 30° sunward (true n·l ~0.60) shaded 3.5x too dark. Crater interiors could never exceed body-average brightness whatever their walls did: flat dark patches instead of bowls with a lit far wall, worst at grazing. These were blunt mitigations for INC-0009 that suppressed the signal rather than filtering it; its real fix (footprint-filtered octaves) has since landed. Now: weight 0.85, and `ThalosSurface` carries a separate `relief_normal_ws` so the micro-facet headroom references *resolved terrain* while `geo_normal_ws` stays sphere-outward for the grazing-view visibility floor — conflating the two blacked out the entire eye-level frame, which is exactly what the existing comment warned about. `mira-approach` verified: craters read as bowls with lit far walls |

| BL-20260726T225009Z | **Canopy coverage is savanna-sparse — the wet belt should read as forest.** Fallout of the §4.4 canopy unification (landed 2026-07-26): coverage is now `climate envelope × stand structure`, and the two multiply down hard. The `forest-stand` site finder maximises real coverage over the whole daylight hemisphere and its **best is 0.236** — scattered individuals and small clumps, closed canopy nowhere. Trees are also visibly *sparser than before* the unification: placement previously clumped on the stand field alone (peaking ~0.93) gated by a gentler `woody_biome_gate`, so the envelope factor is new attenuation. Do **not** just lower `STAND_LO`: per the cloud-field rule, *measure the coverage distribution first* (`just map` + a body-scale sweep of `canopy_coverage`) and decide whether the envelope, the stand window, or both are miscalibrated — a threshold inherited across a scale change is how coverage silently drains. Target: closed canopy (coverage > 0.8) over a real fraction of the wet belt, plains still genuinely treeless | next | S–M | after §4.4 (landed) | vegetation.md §4.3–4.4 |
| BL-20260726T225010Z | **Distant groves render brighter than the meadow — impostors take no sun shadow.** `tree_impostor.wgsl` passes `sun_shadow = 1.0` ("fully lit for now"; sun-shadow on impostors was deferred to its Phase 2b) while terrain at the same distance *does* receive cascaded + self-shadowing. Measured stand-vs-open-ground luma at matched screen distance in `forest-stand`: **0.963 near / 0.987 mid / 1.031 far** — canopy correctly darker up close, crossing over and inverting past the 2.4 km impostor handoff, which is exactly where the far band takes over. Canopy must be darker than meadow *and* fall with distance (vegetation.md §14). Note trees past `TREE_SHADOW_CASTER_MAX_M` (6 km) deliberately don't *cast*; this row is about them not *receiving*. Cheapest correct fix is probably the W12 heightfield horizon term rather than extending cascades. **2026-07-30 review update (reviews/20260730T011353Z §8, refuter-confirmed):** the fix note above is partly wrong — no cascade extension is needed, cascades 1–2 are already pinned to 3/6.5 km to cover the caster band, and impostor rings 0–1 now *CAST* into them (`SHADOW_CASTER_LAYER`) while the fragment never samples them: cast-without-receive. LOD is slant-keyed (`view_d = √(d²+agl²)`), so any view ≥ ~1.2 km AGL renders the entire forest below as non-receiving impostors — aerial framings, the keystone's target surface. Fix here: sample cascades 1/2 in the impostor fragment (1–3 depth taps per card pixel; the standard-path block-binding pattern already exists in the material family), delete the stale `tree_impostor_standard.wgsl:309-311` "beyond the cascade region" comment, and fix vegetation.md §12 ("impostors do not cast" contradicts code). W12 remains the answer only past `TREE_SHADOW_CASTER_MAX_M` (6 km). **Landed 2026-07-30:** `TreeImpostorExtension` carries the `ShadowCascadeBlock` + three depth maps (fanned by `sync_shadow_receivers` beside the mesh trees — identical machinery), the fragment folds `sun_shadow_factor × cloud_t` into the direct-only gate exactly as the mesh tree composes it, the stale comment is replaced with the cast-without-receive rationale, and vegetation.md §12 now states the true contract (rings 0–1 cast AND receive; rings 2–3 neither; W12 owns the far field). Verified: BL-20 pipeline validation passes with the new bindings; survey-framing A/B is a no-regression identity (high sun ⇒ per-card shadow deltas are sub-pixel there, as expected). **The discriminating look needs what no preset stages — low sun with a terrain/canopy shadow crossing an impostor stand at 2–5 km**: the valley's impostor trees should now darken with the ground instead of glowing above their own cast shadows, and the 1.2 km mesh↔impostor swap should no longer pop shadowed→lit | verify | S–M | **user:** low-sun pass over relief with forest — shadowed valleys' distant trees dim with the ground | vegetation.md §12, §14 · gfx §3 (F6) · reviews/20260730T011353Z §8 |
| BL-20260730T012904Z-cloud-phase-sign | **Cloud phase function is sign-flipped** — `clouds_compute.wgsl:945` computes `dot(ray_dir, -sun_dir)`, the negation of the scattering cosine, so the HG forward lobe (g 0.8) peaks **anti-solar**: glare/silver-lining renders 180° from the sun (~23× asymmetry at the shipped lobe mix; the 2.2 clamp never engages), the powder term and `ms_aniso` inherit the flip (their comments are arithmetically false), and the far tier (symmetric n·l) disagrees with the near tier by sun azimuth at the handoff. The flip predates every capture round, and all recorded round-8 checks are direction-agnostic — which is why no capture caught it. Fix is one sign plus the two false comments, **but** the powder constants (0.35/0.35), `MS_ANISO`, and possibly the forward clamp were tuned against the flipped geometry: a sign-only fix brightens sunward thin edges ~23×, so the change owns a `cloud-sunset`/`cloud-cruise` recapture/retune pass. **Landed 2026-07-30, agent A/B capture-verified** (`artifacts/visual/runs/phase-sign/`): sunward deck gains warm lit fringes and toward-sun gradients (was flat grey-mauve beside the sun), nothing clips (the 2.2 forward clamp bounds the restored spike — ivory, not blown white); `cloud-cruise` visually unchanged (thick cells are MS-reservoir-dominated, as predicted); anti-solar framing (`AZIMUTH=180`) shows matte masses with no bright rim — the unphysical away-side silver lining is gone. Powder/`MS_ANISO` left untouched with a tuning caveat comment at `powder_term` (they were tuned against the flipped geometry). | BL-20260730T012905Z-cloud-shadow-march-span | **Cloud-shadow march cannot cover the shell** — two refuter-confirmed defects in the `cloud_shadow` compute entry: **(a)** `step_m = min(span/20, 400 m)` caps the marched sun beam at 8 km against a ≥10.5 km zenith chord (shell 900–11,400 m; storm tops ~11.2 km, cirrus veil 8.7–10.9 km — the header's "~1–3 km slab" premise is false as authored). Marched ceiling ≈ 900 + 8000·sin(elev) m: fine above ~25–33°, but at 15° only the bottom ~2 km integrates and at ~12° a high-base deck (base 2685 m) gets **zero samples in cloud** — an opaque overcast lays no shadow, exactly when real shadows are longest. **(b)** the clear-air early-out samples a single level-0 weather texel at the *unclamped* mid-chord — displaced ≥1 weather texel below ~47° sun, ~18 texels at the 3.4° floor — so a solid texel over a down-beam clear lane writes transmittance 1: migrating holes under lane-cut overcast. The view march's 25 km context clamp + coarse-mip probes (BL-33 finding G) were never propagated, and the shadow entry's "exactly as the view march takes it" comment is stale. Fix: march the density-bearing span (start at the local base crossing, distribute the budget, feed the resulting step into `filter_m` per the alias contract); clamp the context anchor ≤25 km past entry and cull via a coarse-mip region probe. Verify with `THALOS_CLOUD_SHADOW=show` at ~45° vs ~15° over the same deck. **Landed 2026-07-30, agent capture-verified** (`artifacts/visual/runs/shadow-span/`): (a) the 400 m cap is deleted — 20 steps distribute over the whole beam span with `filter_m` band-limiting to the stride (cost unchanged: same step count, the field homogenizes per the LOD contract); layer-band clipping was **rejected**, not omitted — a mid/region-derived band cannot conservatively bound a lone storm top, the exact mass the cap lost; (b) context anchor clamped ≤25 km past entry + mip-4/mip-5 region probes guard the cull, mirroring the view march's finding-G pattern verbatim. Evidence: `THALOS_CLOUD_SHADOW=show` on `cloud-godray` before/after — the broad RED band (coverage present, zero marched depth: the truncation's categorical diagnostic signature) is eliminated; beauty pair shows broader, deeper ground shadow under the same deck. Small cloud drift between shots is resident-host sim time, not the change | verify | S–M | **user:** live low-sun pass over broken cloud — shadows should now deepen (not vanish) as the sun drops; sunset shadows reaching the ground are new behaviour, judge whether the strength reads right | reviews/20260730T011353Z §3, §6 · clouds_compute.wgsl |
| BL-20260730T012906Z-night-shadow-standdown | **Sun cascade never stands down at night and gates moonlight** (found independently by three review lenses) — `sun_shadow.rs:777` publishes `gate.x = SHADOW_STRENGTH` (0.88) whenever a terrain body is near; the only deactivation is losing the body, the cascade cameras keep rendering casters from a below-horizon sun all night, and every direct/indirect split applies the sun-axis factor to the **summed** direct term — which at night is the 60-lux MoonLight (deliberately ~12–15× the night floor). Moonlit canopy/rock/hull/ground gets up-to-88 % dark patches following an invisible underground sun, plus three 4096² cascade renders per frame for a light at zero illuminance. Fix (refuter-corrected): gate = 1 for any sun above the horizon, ramping to 0 only below (e.g. `sin_elev ∈ [-0.06, 0]`), deactivating the cascade cameras in lockstep — do **NOT** multiply by `SunDaylight` (fractional up to ~7° elevation; would fade golden-hour shadows exactly when longest). `gate.z` (contact) rides along safely — documented direct-sun-only. Twilight moon-share mis-gating remains second-order. **Landed 2026-07-30:** `SHADOW_NIGHT_FADE_SIN = 0.06` — full strength above the horizon, linear fade to zero over ~3.4° below it, surface mode only (craft-local exempt: the tangent-plane test is wrong at orbital altitude and the hull cascade is harmless unlit); fully-night takes the existing rig-off path, so the cascade cameras deactivate and the night depth renders stop. The 1 Hz `stability_gauge` diagnostic gained `sun_sin_elev` + `night_fade` as the recurrence tell. **Day path proven untouched by instrument, not eyeball:** `spaceport-aerial` recapture logs `sun_sin_elev 0.223 / night_fade 1.0 / active_cascades 3` — gate bit-identical to pre-change at any sun above the horizon | verify | S | **user:** a night/moonlit surface session — moonlit ground, trees, and hull should show NO shadow patches (there is no night `ScreenshotPreset` yet; one would let agents close this class headlessly) | reviews/20260730T011353Z §11 · sun_shadow.rs · shadow.wgsl |

| BL-20260730T012908Z-probe-photometric | **Reflection probe: planet disc + starfield never got the photometric conversion the sun disc got** — the probe declares one unit system (`reflection_probe.rs:93-98`) and `sun_disc_radiance = flux·SUN_DISC_GAIN` honours it, but `planet_color (0.25,0.35,0.55)` and `starfield_tint 0.015` are unconverted constants: the hull's reflected planet is ~2–4× under-bright at the homeworld (Lambertian would be ≈ albedo·flux/π ≈ 0.95), and the error's **sign flips with focus distance** (~10× too bright at outer-system focus, breaking exposure invariance). On a metallic-1.0/roughness-0.08 hull the specular probe is the dominant broad-field light — this is the concrete "ship in space reads dark" mechanism (the flat space-ambient half was dismissed by-design: eyeball-tuned with its tint in place, INC-20260724T204059Z; brightness retunes stay with GF-CAL). Fix (refuter-corrected): apply the flux-derived factor inside `orbital_sample` to **both** branches — the painted constants *and* the baked `ImpostorAlbedo` path (`:942-944`), which is the branch that actually renders at the homeworld. Stand-in retires with F8b (frozen), but the internal inconsistency is fixable now. **Closed 2026-07-30 in two halves:** (1) the planet-disc factor had **already landed concurrently** with the sprint merge (`planet_irradiance = flux/π`, applied in `orbital_sample` to both branches with a `planet_lux` diagnostic — the row's mechanism, fixed independently before this row was picked up); (2) the last unconverted term, `starfield_tint`, now carries the exposure `gain` (`STARFIELD_RADIANCE × gain`) — deliberately NOT heliocentric flux, since stars are sun-independent; the doc comment records the distinction. At the homeworld `gain ≈ 1` is an identity by construction, **proven by capture**: `orbit-hull` before/after MAE 0.03/255, 0.1 % px > 2 (boot jitter; `artifacts/visual/runs/probe-photometric/`). | BL-20260730T012909Z-light-gate-anchor | **Sun/moon illuminance is gated at the craft, not the view** — `lighting.rs:517-604`: `daylight`·`horizon_vis` evaluated at the craft scales the single global `DirectionalLight` every standard-path surface shares; `horizon_vis` (W12 march, active < 15 km AGL) has **no floor**. Sharp case: craft parked in a valley at low sun → every sunlit ridge, tree, and structure in a freecam/god-view/viewpoint frame goes unlit under a daylight sky, and raising the camera does not restore it — since trees joined the standard path this extinguishes whole forests. The shadow rig (INC-20260724T232104Z) and celestial visibility (BL-12) already moved to the ViewAnchor for exactly this class of bug. Scoped fix (refuter-corrected): the **terminator** term may follow BL-12 to the ViewAnchor; the **horizon** term must NOT be view-anchored (it exists to drop the parked craft into mountain shadow — view-anchoring relights it whenever the camera swings clear); with one global scalar light no anchor is correct, so the horizon term's real resolution is per-receiver sun visibility (NTR-RT2 / W12r) — interim, bound its effect when view and craft separate. Moon gate (`:740-753`) has the identical anchoring. **Landed 2026-07-30 exactly as rescoped:** sun + moon terminator gates (and moon selection/phase/horizon-rise) evaluate at the resolved `ViewAnchor` via `AnchorBody::cam_world` (craft fallback when unresolved); the W12 horizon term stays craft-anchored but its effect fades over 1–5 km of view–craft separation (`horizon_share`), so a freecam ridge frame can no longer be extinguished by the craft's valley while the sky shows daylight. `ambient_light` diagnostic gained `gate_body` / `view_craft_sep_m` / `horizon_vis` as the tell. **Day path proven unchanged by instrument:** old and new sessions log bit-identical `sun_lux 10173.173828125` at the same preset; anchor resolution verified live (`sep 0 → 305 m` as the capture camera detaches). | CLOUD-5 | One-world cloud sun transmittance, matched godrays, surface receivers and overcast environment response. **Slice 1 landed 2026-07-25 — the view-anchored near cascade, on the tile renderer** (user ask: "realistic cloud shadows on the terrain"). Producer is a `cloud_shadow` compute entry in `clouds_compute.wgsl` off the *same* bind groups as the view march, so it integrates the identical `get_cloud_map_density` field/config/weather/strata at the view march's own primary-beam extinction — one cloud distribution, no second field to disagree with the sky. 512² over the tangent plane under the view anchor (`clouds::shadow_frame::CloudShadowFrame`, body-fixed, octave extent ladder 12× altitude clamped to 8–64 km ⇒ 31–250 m/texel — sized for coverage, since a payload capture showed 1.5× altitude leaving everything but the basin under the camera outside the map; unsnapped because every rung oversamples the authored erosion scale); a texel holds the transmittance of the beam **through that plane point**, so receivers look up by intersecting their own sun ray with the plane — the parallax the rejected coverage-map projection cannot produce. Receiver is one shared sampler (`thalos::cloud_shadow`) folded into `tile_terrain.wgsl`'s existing direct-sun gate (inheriting `SHADOW_FLOOR`: that gate multiplies the whole lit colour, so a physical `exp(-τ)` would erase the sky fill with it). Capture axis `just compare <scene> cloud-shadow` (off/on/raw, per-shot via `CloudShadowConfig`). **Verification state:** mechanism proven, production framing still missing. The cascade was capture-verified to carry a real, structured field (transmittance/coverage/optical-depth payload, `artifacts/visual/runs/cloud-shadow/overcast_payload.png`, taken with Thalos's authored `coverage` temporarily at 0.95), and the extent ladder was fixed from measurement (`spaceport_payload.png` showed 1.5×-altitude covering only the basin). What is NOT yet shown is a lit before/after over terrain at production weather, because **every land preset we have sits in an authored clear lane**: `cloud-runway` and `spaceport-aerial` both read weather coverage ≈ 0 even at 20× the coverage scale (independently confirmed — the udlod render of `cloud-runway` has a cloudless sky), and the one reliably cloudy site (`cloud-cruise`, sun elevation 35°) is over ocean. **Slice 2 landed 2026-07-29 — atmosphere-march shafts (godrays), with the cloudy-LAND preset this row's verification called for.** `integrate_atmosphere_multiscatter_occluded` (`thalos::atmosphere`) gates the BodySky/ocean march's per-sample sun term by `cloud_sun_transmittance` — cloud gaps become crepuscular shafts, shadowed air loses its airlight; single-scatter only (the ms-LUT term stays unshadowed so shadowed air reads blue, not black; overcast ambient is its own slice). Bindings 13–15 on `BodySkyMaterial`, filled by `update_body_terrain_atmosphere` for the active cloud body only, mirrored to the ocean material. New `cloud-godray` preset scans the 25°-solar-elevation circle for the broken-cloud texel (coverage nearest 0.55 — max coverage would find shaftless overcast) over land ≥ 30 m and looks sunward near ground; new `godray` axis (`THALOS_CLOUD_GODRAY` off/on) toggles only the march term. Agent A/B verified (comparisons/cloud-godray/godray): diff concentrates in the air in front of dark terrain and the horizon sky gaps, surface shadows identical across variants; site probe landed coverage 0.549 over 644 m land. Known limit: the cascade's ~3.4° sun-elevation stand-down excludes true sunset shafts until a sun-aligned/cube tail exists; clear-air shafts are subtle by construction (haze scales them). **User verdict pending on the look.** **Remaining work, in order (revised 2026-07-30 by the graphics-lighting review, reviews/20260730T011353Z §4–§7 — the old receiver list was stale in two directions):** coarse body-fixed cube tail (today the term feathers to lit at the cascade border); **receivers — mesh trees + impostors NOW receive** (landed with the tree standard-path port; `tree_standard.wgsl:308`, `tree_impostor_standard.wgsl:312`, fan-in verified populated), **structures/runway + hull receivers LANDED 2026-07-30** (`ShadowReceiveExtension`/`ShipPartExtension` carry the `CloudShadowBlock` + map, fanned by `apply_craft_shadow` in `Last` — same-frame by construction; both shaders fold `cloud_sun_transmittance` into the direct-only gate). Verified: both presets pass BL-20 pipeline validation with the new bindings; clear-sky identity by pixel diff (runway/spaceport before/after ≈ boot jitter); **the structures-dim-under-a-deck look is NOT yet capturable** — every structures site sits in an authored clear weather lane and the coverage-scale override multiplies a ~0 weather channel (the CLOUD-5 measurement), so that half is a live-weather/user observation. Then grass/rocks; **tree fan one-frame-stale block FIXED same change** (reviews §5: cloud-block fan moved out of `update_tree_material` into `apply_tree_cloud_shadow` in `Last` — the name the tree shader's comment already promised — and `update_body_terrain_atmosphere` ordered `.after(drive_clouds)`; the 2–16 texel orbital-speed canopy jitter was invisible in paused captures by construction, so the live gate is a moving-camera session); overcast ambient/IBL response; incremental/temporal cascade update (it re-marches every frame). **Known limit added (reviews §7, latent):** the cascade has no receiver-altitude term — a summit above the 900 m cloud base takes shadow from cloud strictly below it, and the godray term darkens air *above* the deck; the map's alpha channel is spare for an extinction-weighted deck height + receiver-altitude fade. | BL-20260723T214730Z-cloud-tier-fill-parity | **Near/far cloud fill parity at mid-altitude** — the surviving defect of the 2026-07-23 ascent investigation. Measured with the new ownership-bypassing `far-only` diagnostic at one framing (spaceport-aerial, ELEVATION=65 DISTANCE=60000): near-only fill 0.109 vs far-only 0.989 on the SAME live weather cube — the far tier renders a moderate-strata field ~9× denser than the near tier's quantile fill. **RESOLVED as the alignment bug — see INC-20260723T221126Z.** The runtime cube re-upload scrambles face/mip layout on the GPU (both `image.data` mutation and wholesale `images.insert`); the NEAR marcher was the consumer of the corrupted copy, so the volumetrics flew a garbage field while impostor/composite rendered the correct spawn-time field — no calibration could ever align them. **Fix landed 2026-07-24:** runtime cube mutation eliminated; `sync_cloud_weather_binding` handle-swaps the compute pass onto the active body's spawn-uploaded cubes (`BodyCloudCubes` registry); one correctly-uploaded field serves every consumer. Agent-verified: same-framing tier A/B agrees (both clear at the probe site, matching the impostor), cruise near→far pattern-coherent, runway ground view matches the impostor, planet disc clean. **User live verdict 2026-07-24: positioning consistent — alignment closed.** **Thickness parity + transition morphology landed 2026-07-24** (cloud doc round-5 checkpoint): re-measured from scratch on the fixed cube at a CPU-probed cloudy site (`THALOS_RUNWAY_SITE="22.0,153.0"`, the spaceport column is authored clear; probes in `solar_system_state::cloud_site_probe`) — baseline near fill 0.044 / amp 0.013 vs far 1.000 / 0.419 (~32× amp). The pairing is now DERIVED, never hand-tuned: `clouds::fill_lut` Monte-Carlo-marches the near density math (CPU mirror of the marcher + its 64³ noise volume) over the body's actual weather cube at spawn, fits the near tier's 8-node threshold curve toward the fill=strata contract, and emits the far tier's opacity LUT `E[1−T_col | strata mean]` — far = near by construction (`BodyCloudFill` → compute `fill_threshold_nodes` + composite `fill_response`). Derivation must sample the BILINEAR field (point-texel conditioning lit the inter-cell ramps); monotonicity in the fit must be by construction (clamp-after-move froze it). Transition: far tier gains footprint-gated body-fixed sub-texel morphology on the LUT input (anchored at the occupancy-weighted chord position — argmax anchoring cut seams; INTEGER hash — the float Hoskins hash traces straight lattice seams at planet-scale coordinates), the strata 0.75-mip floor no longer blurs resolved footprints, and the near tier softens edges/retires erosion through the reach + entry windows (`soften`). A/B after: near 0.231/0.057 vs far 0.543/0.185 (amp 32× → 3.2×; residual is regional conditional heterogeneity + morph amplitude, judged visually). New reliability tell: one cold capture rendered zero clouds with exit 0 — `ActiveCloudBody` never activated (no "composite frame override" log line); re-run was clean. **User live verdict 2026-07-24 (screenshots):** registration/parity largely PASS ("starting to be pretty accurate… a lot of it is quite accurate") — but the transition still fails the bar: the far sheet ends on the camera-relative ownership arc (reads as a hard scalloped curtain edge, worst in motion), swirl filaments at thin sheet skirts (residual grazing layer-clip family), clouds read uniformly GRAY (energy/ambient budget, CLOUD-4/5 territory — see the reference systems' multi-directional ambient), and near→far detail loss is a visible cliff. **Tier A/B 2026-07-30 (`just compare blobby-clouds cloud-tier`) puts a picture to this row:** at 105 km the near tier renders lobed, shaded, volumetric clouds — and runs out of reach after the foreground; everything beyond is the far tier, which draws **flat 2-D white stickers** with hard angular outlines, no relief and no self-shading. That, not silhouette noise detail, is the user's 2026-07-30 "clouds read geometric at a distance" verdict. Root judgment: every remaining symptom is the two-representations-mid-air architecture itself — reference systems (Blackrack, MSFS) run ONE raymarched representation across the visible range and LOD by footprint (sampling rate + density bandwidth), swapping representation only where cells are sub-pixel → superseding direction filed as BL-20260724T003705Z-cloud-single-representation-reach. Still worth keeping from this row: (b) port the LUT into `solid_planet.wgsl` (composite↔impostor swap consistency); (c) minimal repro of the cube re-upload corruption (upstream report); (d) CLOUD-7 advection must version weather via new assets + handle swap, never in-place mutation. **Falsified (do not re-derive):** quat convention/conjugate (quats bit-identical), wind-angle misregistration (~66 m in cold runs), strata dead zone (fixed separately via layer-relative strata), per-segment overlap dilution (fixed via analytic clip), uniform-layout scramble (Rust/WGSL field orders match), spawn-vs-live content divergence *as the residual cause* (persists on the live cube); hand-fitted threshold constants (twice: too steep an exceedance cliff, and the last fit was against the corrupted cube) | done | M | — | cloud round-5 checkpoint · cloud_fill2 A/B evidence 2026-07-24 |
| BL-20260729T061228Z-orbital-cloud-floor | **Clear sky was never clear from orbit — a gated anvil term was clamping the whole planet** (user 2026-07-29: "monotone cover of blobby looking clouds… too much cloud overall"). `cloud_weather_probe`'s new TRANSFER table: the clearest coverage decile — 591,400 texels, **28% of the planet** — emitted strata 0.304 and rendered at 0.262 (far tier) / 0.340 (impostor); only **4.8%** of the disc came out ≤0.02 opacity against **13.4%** genuinely clear in the coverage channel. Cause: `mass = mass.max(anvil_shape * anvil_profile * storm_w)` — the gate multiplies the value that is then used as a **floor**, so outside a storm anvil it degrades to `mass.max(0.0)`, and the realization `smoothstep(-0.035, +0.035, mass)` is CENTRED on zero, emitting `areal_fraction = 0.500` planet-wide. Invisible in the near marcher and `fill_lut`, which carry the same `max` but realize with `smoothstep(0.0, …)` where a zero floor maps to zero density — which is exactly why the near view had real gaps and the orbital view was a whiteout. **Landed 2026-07-29:** all three mirrors blend by the gate instead (`mass += (mass.max(anvil_mass) - mass) * gate`); `fill_lut`'s `if gate > 0.0` guard also replaced (it stopped the exactly-zero case but not a tiny positive gate). `FILL_LUT_VERSION` 9 → 10. **Forced second fix:** with the floor gone the planet rendered 0.34× authored coverage — the formation threshold `1.03 - 0.70·cov` had been co-tuned with the bug and sweeps almost entirely outside `shape`'s own support (σ ≈ 0.09 about 0.562), so nothing formed on its merits and the floor was supplying the cloud. Threshold is now DERIVED from the `(1-c)` quantile of the `shape - vertical_narrow` comparand (probe's THRESHOLD FIT table), not fitted by eye. **Forced third fix:** the deck then rendered as flat pancakes — `vertical_narrow`'s dome coefficients were scaled against the old 0.70 threshold span, so at h→1 the cumulus term hit 0.42 ≈ **4.7σ** of `shape` and cut every column's top half off. Rescaled by 0.31 (0.012/0.130/0.093); the threshold-range ratio and the σ argument agree on that factor. `FILL_LUT_VERSION` → 11. **Agent-verified by capture 2026-07-29:** `strata/coverage` 1.296 → 0.668, clear disc 4.8% → 56.6%; planet reads as ocean + land + frontal cloud systems, cruise deck has rounded lobed tops. **Remaining user gate:** does the sky feel right live, and is the deck still too shallow (fair-weather cumulus, no towers) — the latter overlaps BL-20260723T165923Z and the user's 2026-07-29 multi-étage ask. | verify | M | — | INC-20260729T061228Z-orbital-cloud-floor · solar_system_state.rs · fill_lut.rs · clouds_compute.wgsl |

| BL-20260730T195500Z-cloud-cell-evolution | **Cell-scale cloud evolution (the cheap half of CLOUD-7)** — landed 2026-07-30. `cloud_cell_field` gained a sim-time axis: each octave's sampling offset advances along its own fixed direction at 4 / 12 / 48 decorrelations per sim-day (6 h / 2 h / 30 min), so cells morph in place rather than sliding, at zero extra samples. Both tiers read ONE value (`CloudsConfig::cell_evolution_s` → near `config.cell_evolution_s` and far `cloud_time.x`) so they cannot disagree on shape. Sim time, not wall clock, so captures stay reproducible and time warp accelerates weather. **Two traps recorded in the tests:** (a) a translation is the ONLY safe animation here — the field is stationary under it, which is what keeps `fill_lut`'s spawn-derived calibration valid at every later instant (`cell_field_distribution_is_time_invariant`); animating amplitude/billow/weights would silently drift planetary coverage over a sim-day, a change no screenshot can catch. (b) A modulo wrap was tried and REMOVED: the field is hash-keyed lattice value noise and therefore aperiodic, so no wrap period returns it to itself — an "integer number of decorrelations" is not an integer lattice translation, and even that would not have worked. Un-wrapped raw sim time is safe because scenarios boot at time-of-day epochs (`evolution_phase_resolves_at_sim_epochs`). **Agent-verified:** same viewpoint at +30 min / +2 h sim time flips the cloud mask on 1.03% / 3.14% of frame (monotone, mechanism works). **Open:** the rate is conservative — real cumulus fields visibly turn over in 15–30 min and the visible cell scale here is the 2 h octave. Judging the right rate needs motion, so it is a **user gate**, not a still. | verify | S | — | INC-20260729T061228Z · cell_field.rs · atmosphere.wgsl |
| BL-20260724T003705Z-cloud-single-representation-reach | **One cloud representation across the visible range** (user verdict 2026-07-24 + Blackrack/MSFS reference study): the 67 km near→far handoff sits mid-view at cruise/aerial altitudes, so every residual symptom (ownership-arc curtain edge, gray crossfade band, detail-loss cliff) is the representation swap itself — the reference systems raymarch ONE representation to the horizon/orbit and LOD by *footprint* (sampling resolution + density bandwidth), swapping representation only where cells are sub-pixel. Direction: distance-banded march where the density field's bandwidth shrinks WITH the step size — drop the Cartesian shape octaves as footprint grows and converge density to the strata envelope (the `soften` seam is the first rung), THEN grow steps to match the band-limited field. This respects ADR-20260721T033055Z (conservative bounds): BL-33's moiré and INC-0011's isosurfaces came from stretched steps / heuristic skips over the UNFILTERED field; a field filtered to the step scale has no sub-step features to alias or skip. Target reach ~200–300 km (strata cells sub-pixel beyond), then the far estimator owns only true orbit; the derived `fill_lut` pairing remains the contract for that remaining boundary. Second track: cloud WHITENESS — the gray read is the energy/ambient budget (single flat ambient pair vs the references' multi-directional sky in-scatter; CLOUD-4's unfinished sky-LUT binding + prefactor stack 0.36/0.68). **Both tracks landed 2026-07-24** (cloud doc round-6): (1) the march contract lives in `thalos::atmosphere` (shared by marcher + composite — lockstep is structural): bands 600/1200/2400/4800 m at 42/90/180 km edges, reach 300 km, entry ownership 240–300 km, reach dissolve over the last 15%; the density field band-limits AHEAD of each step increase — erosion retires as the refine step outgrows the detail scale, the shape spectrum narrows to the tall mix, and past ~90 km density is the DERIVED homogenized field `E[shaped | env]` (a third LUT from the same spawn Monte-Carlo, so band 2+ is mean-preserving by construction and cheaper per probe than band 0 — no noise fetches); steep rays clamp steps to a 350 m radial rate (bounded: such rays have short in-shell segments); budgets 112/176/192/224 (baseline reaches the full 300 km). (2) Whiteness: cloud ambient now binds the F3/F4 `SkyAmbient` physical sky irradiance (`E_sky/π`, view-factor 0.45 undersides, analytic pair kept only as the space stand-in), with a τ-driven ambient self-occlusion proxy (0.30 + 0.70·exp(−τ·0.45)) so interiors keep shape — a directional sky march is the CLOUD-5 upgrade. Capture evidence: the mid-view ownership curtain arc is GONE (cruise: one continuous deck to the horizon), clouds read white; fixed en route: the morph fine octave needed its own alias gate (dot band at the new 240–300 km handoff), and pre-existing fringe jitter stipple is now more visible at the brighter contrast (temporal-quality item, noted below). Round 6b (2026-07-24, after the user's ascent screenshots): output-linear ownership partition (the far tier weighted accumulation INPUTS through the response LUT's nonlinear toe while the near tier faded linearly in output alpha — never summed to unity → the sharp ascent circle + over-count veil; ownership now applies to converged far opacity), shared `cloud_strata_warp` (measure-preserving tangential domain warp in the march contract kills the rounded-square texel lattice in BOTH tiers, no CPU-mirror change), shared `cloud_morph_noise` re-mottles the marcher's homogenized bands (kills the smooth-vs-crisp band split at 150 km). **User live verdict 2026-07-24: "continuity is quite acceptable now" — transition gate PASSED.** Residuals re-homed: fringe stipple → CLOUD-2 temporal ladder; far prefactor (0.68) re-match + GPU budget re-measure ride along on BL-20260724T022522Z-cloud-volumetric-morphology (the new active cloud front) | done | L | BL-20260723T214730Z evidence + protocol | cloud §3.3 · round-6/6b checkpoints · ADR-20260721T033055Z |
| BL-20260730T201500Z-far-tier-relief | **The far tier resolves cell outlines but has no volume — flat white stickers from 300 km out** (user 2026-07-30: clouds "read like synthetic shapes instead of purely organic shapes, especially at a distance"). Isolated by `just compare blobby-clouds cloud-tier`: near-only renders lobed, shaded, volumetric cloud; far-only renders the SAME sky as flat 2-D coverage with hard outlines, no relief and no self-shading — and the far tier owns everything beyond the 300 km handoff, i.e. most of the frame at orbital altitudes. **The premise that broke:** the handoff was placed where cells were assumed sub-pixel, but a 5.4 km cell subtends ~18 px at 300 km and only drops below 1 px near ~5,400 km. So the far tier owns a very large band in which cells are FULLY RESOLVED and drawn without volume — resolved silhouette, zero relief. It is doing what it was built to do (`fill_cell_edge`/`fill_cell_solid` resolve coverage correctly); what is missing is that a surface-parameterized estimator (ADR-20260722T141000Z) has no vertical structure to shade. **Direction (cheapest thing that could work):** give the far tier relief WITHOUT marching it — a cloud-top normal from the cell-field gradient, shaded with the same phase/BRDF the near tier uses, plus a thickness-driven self-shadow term; `orbital_cloud_normal_body` already does a version of this for the impostor. Far tier only: no step-ladder or budget changes, no calibration re-derivation. **Rejected for now:** pushing the near march's reach out to true sub-pixel range (reopens the round-6 cost model and budgets tuned to 300 km, against a passed user gate — multi-session, several M tokens). Do it only if relief alone fails, which this row's A/B will settle. **Verify with the instrument that found it:** re-run `just compare blobby-clouds cloud-tier`; near-only and far-only should converge in form, not just in coverage. | next | M | after BL-20260724T022522Z round-7 verification | ADR-20260722T141000Z · BL-20260724T003705Z (reach, done) · cloud_composite.wgsl |

Round 7's tower-vs-squat CONTRAST therefore runs at ~1/3 authored strength. Open question for whoever closes this row: restore contrast with a larger dome AND a wider threshold span, rather than either constant alone. **Round 8 (2026-07-30) — the dome was NOT the problem; measure before tuning.** Asked to restore round 7's tower contrast by raising the dome and widening the threshold span together, the new `cloud_weather_probe` HEIGHT table refuted the premise: **58.9% of cloudy columns already reached their own top** (the flat-slab end, not the over-carved end), so the dome was not cutting anything. Dome and threshold span left where the σ derivation put them. Capture: `r8_{cruise,above}.png` vs `r7_*` — foreground deck reads visibly heaped rather than plated.
