| BL-20260724T232256Z-cloud-radiance-multiscatter | **Cloud radiance: the missing multiple scattering** (user verdict 2026-07-24 post round-7, with MSFS references: "the clouds always look muddy, and not pure white"). NOT a morphology defect — the near march's "multi-scatter octaves" divided their weighted sum by `Σw`, making it a weighted AVERAGE of `1/4π`-normalised phase values, so three probability densities still carried ONE scattering event's energy. A lit cell returned `0.051·E` against the diffusion limit `A/π ≈ 0.255·E`, so the physically-bound F3/F4 sky ambient out-competed the sun and clouds took their chroma from it. Measured on the existing `cloud_cruise` capture: near-tier clouds 0.30 display luminance vs 0.49–0.73 for the sky BEHIND them, B/R 1.28. Fix splits the source term as the physics does — exact normalised phase for single scattering (glare + silver lining) plus an isotropic-equivalent reservoir at `CLOUD_MS_ALBEDO = 0.80`, wider octaves demoted to its depth response + residual anisotropy. Three compensating constants removed in the same change: far prefactor (hand-raised 0.55→0.68 to hide the dark near tier) now DERIVED from the same anchor at 0.302; Reinhard white point 2.2→10.0; both tiers' per-channel "phase headroom" tints deleted (they biased sunlit cloud ~12–15% blue on top of the authored albedo). `fill_lut` untouched — it governs coverage, this governs radiance per covered pixel. **Code landed; compile-check and captures BLOCKED** on unrelated in-progress fleet-kernel work in `physics_canonical/src/simulation.rs` (does not build). Verify: `just capture cloud-cruise cloud-interior cloud-sunset cloud-planet` — (a) sunlit tops white not blue-grey, (b) tops brighter than the sky beside them, (c) NO brightness step at the near↔far handoff (both tiers moved — the thing most at risk), (d) round-7 domes now read as volumes under restored lit/shadow contrast | verify | M | INC-20260724T232256Z · unblocks the BL-20260724T022522Z round-7 capture gate | cloud §3.4 round-8 · INC-20260724T232256Z |
| BL-20260725T042404Z-cloud-weather-distribution | **Orbital cloud distribution and weather pattern structure** (user 2026-07-25, orbital viewpoint + NASA references: "much too binary, either it's fully caked or no clouds in sight", wants "more accurate and interesting weather patterns" and even distribution). Diagnosed with a new agent-runnable probe (`cargo run -p thalos_runtime --example cloud_weather_probe` → equirect maps + per-stage histograms + a strata/coverage calibration ratio, in `artifacts/diagnostics/cloud_weather/`), which separates producer / strata / far-response as the stage that binarizes. **Measured before:** 43.3 % of the planet at *exactly* zero coverage and the far-tier opacity map literally two-valued (52 % at 0, the rest one flat plateau). Four causes, all in the producer: (1) the synoptic occupancy gate was a ±0.08 smoothstep on an fbm whose own σ is larger — a binary cut at PLANET scale; (2) `cloud_surface_density_cpu` asked "is the noise above threshold at this point" instead of "what fraction of this ~5 km texel is cloudy", so coverage moved area but never amount; (3) no optically thin population at all (no cirrus); (4) isotropic fbm blobs — nothing read as a front, band, or cyclone. **Landed 2026-07-25:** divergence-free curl warp + latitude-dependent zonal shear on the SYNOPTIC domain only (mesoscale partially advected, cell scale not at all — warping every scale renders the planet as a marbled fluid); widened+floored occupancy gate with a sparse fair-weather population; a high thin cirrus/cirrostratus veil expressed in the existing RGBA channels as a high thin sheet with reduced strata AMPLITUDE (never by scaling optical depth — that is the rejected grey-veil path in `weather_column_from_texel`); areal-fraction formation with the threshold line recalibrated so emitted column occupancy tracks the coverage channel; a cluster-scale shape octave (the producer's noise previously topped out at 211 cycles ≈ 95 km features on a cube with 4.9 km texels, so systems had no interior); and `COVERAGE_SCALE` collapsed from four hand-copied constants to one. **Measured after:** exactly-clear 43.3 %→~11 %, far-opacity mid-tones 44.9 %→~65 %, coverage mean tracks the authored 0.46. **Not fixed, and the reason further producer tuning will not help:** at planet-disc framing the far tier reads the cube through `strata_floor` (pinned to 0.75 mip whenever the pixel exceeds ~1600 m) with `cloud_morph_noise` fully gated OFF by the same footprint test — both tuned for near/cruise framings. Consequence, confirmed by capture: cube content below ~30 km is filtered away (halving the formation transition width produced ZERO render change), while content at 5–30 km reads as salt-and-pepper dither, so the only band that survives is 30–100 km smooth value-noise — which renders as rounded ovals, i.e. the remaining "looks like poured paint" verdict. Surface-space boundary erosion was tried at several strengths/gate widths and cannot fix it at 4.9 km texels (a gate narrow enough to stay a boundary effect is 1–2 texels wide, so it dithers); the note is in the code. Next step is the far tier's disc-range path, not the weather cube: give `cloud_morph_noise` a coarse octave that is safe at orbital footprints, and/or filter radiance/transmittance rather than occupancy (`E[opacity] ≠ response(E[occupancy])` for a nonlinear response) | verify | M | — | **user:** orbital view — organization/distribution should read as weather; the flat-paint texture is the filed follow-up, not a regression | cloud §3.1 · `solar_system_state::CloudWeatherField::from_climate` · cloud_composite.wgsl `sample_orbital_cloud` |
| BL-20260725T190000Z-cloud-solidity-vertical-development | **Cloud solidity and vertical development** (user 2026-07-25, cumulonimbus reference: "clouds are super ghost-ish… they should be realistic, solid appearing clouds", plus an explicit ask for vertical development). Reproduced before touching anything: from a level/below viewpoint (`cloud-runway`, `massif-ridge`) the deck rendered as flat grey-lavender pancakes *dimmer than the sky behind them* with the sky gradient showing through, while the same deck seen from above (`cloud-cruise`, `massif-aerial`) read acceptably solid — an optical-depth defect, not shading or coverage. **Cause 1: the near march scaled extinction by occupancy.** `env` is an AREAL FRACTION (share of a ~5 km texel that is cloudy), and the marcher spent it twice — once correctly as the input to the derived `formation_threshold(env)` (*where* cloud forms) and once as `shared_envelope = smoothstep(0.04, 0.42, env)` multiplying density, which thins every cell in proportion to how sparse its NEIGHBOURHOOD is. It also let the fill contract be satisfied the wrong way: `E[column alpha] = strata_mean` is indifferent between 40 % of columns at alpha 1.0 and 100 % at alpha 0.4, and the envelope multiply chose the latter. Occupancy now gates formation only (`formation_gate = smoothstep(0.02, 0.08, env)` — the toe matters: a gate ramping from zero turns a 3 %-occupancy lane into half-density fog and reintroduces the ghost by another door). Contract preserved by construction — the spawn Monte-Carlo re-fits against the new math; measured, threshold node 0 moved 0.503 → 0.667, i.e. the clear-air suppression migrated into the threshold. **Cause 2: convective cells were pancakes by construction** — one isotropic 8 km period over a 1.5–6 km deep layer is wider than the shell is tall, so round 7's dome threshold had nothing to carve a tower from. Convective columns now stretch the domain's RADIAL axis (`CONVECTIVE_STRETCH = 1.9`); sheets keep the isotropic period. **Rejected by capture in the same round:** narrowing the convective horizontal period to 0.42× (≈3.4 km cells) — solid near field, but the cruise deck became a regular LATTICE of identical puffs in rows (the 64³ volume's Cartesian repeat, ADR-20260722T141000Z's "rows" family from the tile side). Standing rule recorded: apparent cell size comes from the formation threshold picking peaks out of a WIDE period, never from shortening the period; narrower cells need the repeat broken first (second octave at a non-commensurate period). `FILL_LUT_VERSION` 1 → 5. **Agent-verified by capture:** `cloud-runway` white sunlit tops with cauliflower silhouettes and shaded undersides; `cloud-cruise` solid broken-cumulus streets, no lattice; `cloud-interior` dense, no regression. These captures also incidentally exercise the round-7 and round-8 gates that were blocked on a lost GPU / unbuildable tree. **Residual, NOT closed — the `massif-ridge` veil:** the massif still reads through a milky film. Narrowed by capture, not assumed — it IS cloud (`THALOS_SCREENSHOT_CLOUD_COVERAGE=0` renders the massif crisp, `artifacts/visual/runs/cloud-ghost/ridge_nocloud.png`), it IS the near tier (`near-only` ≡ production), and it is NOT march-budget exhaustion (forcing 900 steps gave a pixel-equivalent image). Next suspects in order: `edge_softness` widening 0.055 → 0.30 over the 30–42 km `c1` ramp is keyed to the BROAD band step although density is only ever integrated at the 0.2× refine cadence (~5× over-conservative), and the camera possibly sitting inside a tall column at that site (under-dense interior). | verify | M | — | **user:** live verdict on solidity + vertical development; the massif veil is the filed follow-up | cloud §3.4 round-9 · clouds_compute.wgsl · fill_lut.rs |
| BL-20260725T222409Z-cloud-cell-field | **Cell-scale morphology is an analytic surface field; LOD stops rendering the mean** (user verdict 2026-07-25 against Blackrack/MSFS references: "incoherent soupy blobby mess, with rough transitions between LOD"). Two measured causes, one fix — ADR-20260725T222409Z. (a) NOTHING could draw a 1–5 km cell at range: the weather cube is 4.89 km/texel (~10 km Nyquist) and its authored content bottoms out at 15–25 km (`fbm3(dir_raw·128,3)` / `·211,2`), so cell morphology existed only in the 8 km periodic Cartesian volume — retired past ~42 km, REPLACED past ~90 km. (b) That replacement rendered the field's MEAN (`E[shaped|env]`), which is mean-preserving but not appearance-preserving: shading is nonlinear, so a variance-destroying filter keeps a region's average brightness and deletes every cloud in it (the flat beige sheet from ~40 km to the horizon + the contrast step at its edge). Landed: `cloud_cell_field` in thalos::atmosphere — aperiodic hash-lattice COLUMN field in the body-fixed DIRECTION domain (no tile ⇒ no repeat for the shell to cut into rows; the general form of ADR-20260722T141000Z), shared verbatim by marcher + far projection + fill_lut's CPU mirror; the Cartesian volume demoted to a mean-preserving sub-cell sculptor (`SUBCELL_SHAPE_WEIGHT`) that retires with range; LOD is octave retirement inside ONE field keyed to `filter_m` (refine step or pixel footprint, whichever is coarser). Three capture rounds, each fixing what the last exposed: uniform popcorn carpet ⇒ raw octave mix has std **0.115** measured, so the threshold is a cliff (0.40→0.60 swings coverage 80%→20%) — `CLOUD_CELL_GAIN = 3.2`; flat-topped MESAS ⇒ that gain with a hard clamp saturated **9.6 %** of the sky to exactly 1.0 and a constant region has no isosurface, so the dome threshold cut every core at one altitude — soft saturation `x/(k+|x|)`, `CLOUD_CELL_KNEE = 0.45`; boxy right-angled silhouettes ⇒ `cloud_strata_warp` was gated to coarse bands on the (now-dead) premise that the sculptor hid the texel lattice near the camera — now unconditional in both tiers. Also: derived `shape_response` LUT DELETED (fill_lut + uniform + CloudsConfig + cache schema; FILL_LUT_VERSION 5→6); range-keyed edge softening 0.30→0.075 (it was keyed to the BROAD band step though density integrates only at the 0.2× refine cadence — ~5× over-conservative, and round 9's prime suspect for the massif-ridge veil). Agent-verified compile+clippy-clean: `cloud-cruise` cells resolved to the horizon with real openings (LOD sheet + ring gone), `cloud-runway` cumulus with rounded tops replacing the milky wash, `cloud-limb` thousands of individually resolved cells out to the limb replacing grey dither. **Open:** coverage reads too high at oblique framings (per-column fill contract IS satisfied per the fit log — this is authored occupancy + slant overlap, so it belongs to BL-20260725T042404Z, measure before touching either); whole-disc framings correctly unchanged (at ~14 km/px every cell octave is below the sampler — a producer question); faint horizontal banding on some distant cells (suspect radial march quantization, now unmasked because the cell field is constant down a column); GPU budget re-measure (≤3 hash octaves per sample added, offset by the retired noise fetches in the coarse bands) | verify | L | ADR-20260725T222409Z | **user:** live gates — cell read at cruise/ascent, and whether coverage needs the producer row | cloud round-10 · ADR-20260725T222409Z |
| BL-20260726T000929Z-cloud-footprint-lod | **Cloud LOD keyed to footprint, not distance; the impostor demoted to sub-pixel framings** (user ascent verdict 2026-07-25: mid-ascent horizontal comb, volumetrics fade out above ~300 km, and the impostor renders NO clouds over the space center where the near tier had just drawn them — plus "should we even have an impostor?"). One cause, measured: at 300 km a pixel covers **345 m** (0.00115 rad/px at Bevy's default 45° fov on a 1280×720 target) so a 5.4 km cell is ~12 px across, but the ladder read "300 km", switched to 4800 m steps (~10 samples through the deck = the comb) and handed the whole ray to the far estimator via `ray.start > CLOUD_MARCH_ENTRY_FADE_END_M` — which for a nadir view IS altitude. The far estimator renders `E[opacity|occupancy]`, so a 10%-occupancy region became a ~0.09 alpha wash while the near tier drew solid cells: round 9's occupancy-is-not-opacity from the far side. Impostor answer: NOT in the flight envelope — `get_ray` already skips clear air to the shell entry and a nadir orbital ray crosses the 10.5 km shell in ~10–15 km of path (grazing chords are the expensive case, a function of ray ANGLE not altitude); a projection is only correct where cells are genuinely sub-pixel (whole-disc, ~14 km/px). Landed: step law `cloud_march_step_m(t, pixel_angle, radial_rate)` — footprint is a FLOOR (600 m near-field min, runway cost unchanged), radial cap (350 m vertical) and cell cap (1800 m ≈ ⅓ coarsest cell period) are CAPS and win; `cloud_far_ownership(footprint)` 2.2→5.4 km ⇒ ~1900–4700 km altitude, whole flight envelope volumetric; `filter_m` = max(refine step, footprint) is the SOLE LOD input to `get_cloud_map_density` and the `coarse` ladder is deleted. **Rejected by capture:** a chord-length budget floor on the step (reach raised to 600 km, no frontier) — it makes the step a function of ray length, so a ground horizon ray (chord ~600 km) got ~9.7 km steps through a 1.15 km deck and slabbed the whole distant band; budget belongs in the REACH, which is retained and now integrates the footprint law in closed form (`cloud_march_stop_m`). Also fixed en route: `cloud_surface_shape` was piecewise-LINEAR so its slope broke at the four strata knots and an edge-on deck showed four shelves per cloud — now C1 Catmull-Rom in all three lockstep sites, FILL_LUT_VERSION 6→7 (cleaned the near field only, NOT the same defect). **Falsified, do not re-derive:** far tier (near-only pixel-identical), step count/sparse/temporal (`QUALITY=reference` identical — and note it no longer discriminates, since the step SIZE now comes from the footprint law), strata cube mip, weather cube mip. **Open:** oblique coverage still high (BL-20260725T042404Z); cloud undersides read flat grey from below = lighting budget, round-8 territory | verify | M | ADR-20260726T000929Z · builds on BL-20260725T222409Z | **user:** live ascent — continuity, comb, and whether the impostor still shows | cloud round-11 · ADR-20260726T000929Z |
| BL-20260726T042427Z-cloud-cell-style | **Cloud morphology varies with place** (user 2026-07-26, four orbital reference photos — roll streets, open-cell honeycomb, sparse fair-weather cumulus over open water, and a solid deck cut by clear lanes: "more interesting and realistic cloud patterns… different patterns at different places, with a bit of logic"). Reproduced first (`cloud-cruise`, `cloud-planet`): one texture over the whole planet — same-size popcorn cells, evenly packed, no directional organization, only slow blotches of *amount*. Cause was structural, not tuning: `cloud_cell_field` — the function that actually draws the cells in BOTH tiers — had **no spatial parameters at all** (one period, isotropic, one lobe character), so the weather cube could vary how much cloud a place had and how tall it was, but never what KIND it was. Fix: a per-place `cloud_cell_style` (`atmosphere.wgsl` + the one Rust mirror, now lifted out of `fill_lut` into `clouds::cell_field` so the weather producer shares it instead of adding a third copy) driving cell period, zonal elongation, roll tilt and lobe character from two inputs — the cube's own type channel (so morphology agrees with the producer's regime by construction) plus **one** low-frequency organization fetch. Elongation is the linear map `diag(1/a, 1, 1/a)`; a longitude-angle scale tears at the ±π meridian and a tangent-frame scale degenerates to the identity. **Calibration safety is the load-bearing constraint** — the formation threshold is one Monte-Carlo fit for the whole planet, so the field's *distribution* must not move: anisotropy/tilt/period are distribution-preserving by construction and the billow blend (σ 0.185→0.140→0.212) is renormalized by an analytic σ fit, guarded by two tests in `cell_field.rs` (every style lands at mean 0.499 / σ 0.2036, 0 % saturated). `FILL_LUT_VERSION` 7→8. Screenshot-verified: `cloud-cruise` now separated cells with real size variation over open water, `cloud-limb` strong wind-aligned rolls with clear leads (matched styled/isotropic pair in `artifacts/visual/runs/cloud-style/`). Two things this deliberately did NOT fix, both pre-existing and attributed by that pair: the full-disc view is still the smooth wash CLOUD-6 tracks, and the limb's horizontally-dashed strip is unchanged with anisotropy forced off. **Round 2 (user: "the clouds can get too distorted… the patterns themselves are relatively good, but the clouds themselves should stay believable, this is just strange artificial stripy patterns")** — two errors, both now fixed. (a) **Anisotropy was applied to every octave**, so each individual cloud stretched into a tapering ribbon; a street is a row of ROUND cells, so the aspect now grades across octaves (`CLOUD_CELL_ANISO_OCTAVE` 1.0/0.28/0.0, the finest octave — the one carrying the silhouette — deliberately isotropic), and the producer's shape fields take the same graded share. (b) **High aspect degenerates the field into latitude bands**: the diagonal map suppresses longitude by 1/a, so as a grows the field tends to a function of `dir.y` alone, whose contours on a sphere are circles — that was the fingerprint whorl visible in the orbital shot. Aspect capped (max ~3.8 → ~2.9 with the polar fade), gated to fade at the poles, and `tilt` given a latitude term so it can never vanish where the aspect is large (mixing longitude back into the lattice y is the direct antidote, and roll axes veering oppositely across the equator is the physically right shape). **Round 3 (user: "not much better") — the real defect, and it invalidates the round-1/2 diagnoses.** An A/B ladder at a 25 km downlook isolated it: full style → hatched; anisotropy+tilt neutral → still hatched; **styled PERIOD alone → hatched; everything neutral → clean.** The round-1 "pre-existing, survives with anisotropy off" claim was wrong because that control left the period and billow styled. Mechanism: the field is sampled at `dir·(radius/period)`, so a spatially varying period adds a second chain-rule term to the sampling gradient — the field runs at a higher LOCAL frequency than its period nominally sets, while the octave fade still band-limits against the nominal period, so the octave is under-filtered by exactly that factor. Measured: the shipped period ran **1.70×** finer than nominal, the current design 1.13× (and that residual is the roll variant's genuinely finer across-street spacing, which its own band-limit accounts for). A varying `zonal_aspect` shares the flaw (it scales the domain by √a). **Fix = a design change, not a retune:** cell size now comes from spatially varying the octave WEIGHTS (they multiply already-sampled values, so no domain change), the roll is a BLEND between two globally constant transforms (variance-renormalized for the cross-fade), and billow is untouched since it mixes two values at the same point. The producer's `styled_domain` carried the same defect and is deleted rather than tuned. Guarded by `live_style_field_stays_coherent_across_style_boundaries`, which measures local gradient against a constant-period baseline **and asserts the metric still flags a reconstruction of the defect**, so it cannot rot into a no-op. Screenshot-verified clean at 25 km downlook, cruise and limb (`artifacts/visual/runs/cloud-style/`, incl. the four-way isolation ladder). Regional variety is now subtler than the artifacted round 1 — the safe knobs (weight range, roll strength) can be pushed further on request. | verify | M | — | cloud §3.1 · ADR-20260725T222409Z |

### Biomes as the terrain authority (`bio`)

Design of record: [world/biomes.md](world/biomes.md); seam pinned by
ADR-20260725T004758Z. The inversion: the biome is computed **first** (climate +
geomorphology → a continuous `BiomeBlend`), and palette, material layers, scatter
profile, and generator conditioning become fields of an authored `BiomeDef`.
Absorbs TM-P2r and TM-P3b. Slices are strictly ordered — each one's gate is the
next one's precondition.

| ID | Item | Status | Est | Deps | Refs |
|----|------|--------|-----|------|------|
| BIO-1 | **One `Climate` authority:** lift the scalars currently inlined in `procedural.rs::macro_band_ts` (cold lift, warmth, moisture, continentality, orogeny) into a single struct evaluated once per sample, with one writer. **Output-identical** — the gate is `just map` producing the same biome coverage percentages and the same palette render as v13 (forest 29.6 / grassland 25.6 / steppe 19.5 / desert 4.6 / tundra 2.1 / upland 7.4 / rock 5.5 / snow 3.6 / beach 2.1 % of land). No `GENERATOR_VERSION` bump if it is truly identical; if it isn't, it wasn't a refactor. This is the swap point for `ntr §4.4`'s learned BIO1/4/12/15 channels — the whole reason it is a struct and not a tuple | later | S | — | bio §3 L1 · ADR-20260725T004758Z |

The macro palette is then **reimplemented on top** of the blend and `MacroBiome` becomes `weights[0]`'s id. ## Track 2c — Runtime rocket audio (future program · `audio`)

Goal (ADR-20260729T053329Z, 2026-07-29 user direction): rocket engine sound
**generated at runtime from sim state**, not sampled — so continuous throttle,
shipyard-composed engine clusters, sea-level-to-vacuum ambient, and any listener
range are all one model rather than a sample matrix. Thalos has **no audio in
tree at all** today, so this is green field.

Developed in the standalone probe **`korbindeman/thalos-audio-probe`** (private;
sibling checkout `~/Documents/thalos-audio-probe`), extracted here at A5 as a
pure-Rust no-Bevy `thalos_acoustics` crate. **The probe's README is the strategy
doc** — gates, calibration constants, validation against published rocket data,
and findings live there, not here. Behind the keystone renderer work until
explicitly pulled forward.

**A0–A2 landed in the probe 2026-07-29** (jet source model, Mach-wave
directivity, propagation with retarded-time delay + ISO 9613-1 absorption +
Doppler, turbulent intermittency, crackle, perceptual voicing). Model validates
against published Merlin/F-1 thrust and mass flow to ~8 %, Saturn V acoustic
power to the right order. User verdict on the first two rounds: "some elements
sound realistic, but generally synthetic and just like noise" → three defects
found and fixed; second round outstanding.

| ID | Item | Status | Est | Deps | Refs |
|----|------|--------|-----|------|------|
| AUD-A3 | **Spectral identity + crackle as propagation.** The rendered spectrum is still a featureless monotonic slope, which is the prime remaining suspect for "sounds synthetic" — real engines have *features*. Add shock-cell/screech tones keyed off the nozzle pressure ratio (`Exhaust::underexpansion` and `exit_temperature` already exist unread for exactly this), and replace the memoryless crackle shaper with steepening modelled as a propagation process. Regression bar: skewness/kurtosis/envelope-depth stay clear of Gaussian and the rendered-output spectrum stays within the source model's HF budget — the check that caught the last two crackle defects | later | M | — | probe README · ADR-20260729T053329Z |
| AUD-A4 | **Transients, machinery, and the internal path.** Turbopump blade-passage tones (RPM × blades), ignition/shutdown detail beyond the first-order chamber lag, and the structure-borne path a crew hears in vacuum — a different character entirely (low rumble + pump tones), and the only thing audible outside an atmosphere | later | M | AUD-A3 | probe README |
| AUD-A5 | **Extraction: `thalos_acoustics`.** Pure-Rust no-Bevy crate (parameter block in, samples out, allocation-free callback) plus a lock-free sim→audio bridge shaped like `CraftStateMirror`. Must carry a **bounded emission history**, not a current-position lookup — retarded time is not optional once vehicles go supersonic (ADR). Open at extraction time: output device layer (no `cpal`/`kira`/`bevy_audio` decision made), spatialisation (probe is mono by design), per-engine voice budget for clusters, and whether the band envelope runs sub-rate | later | M–L | AUD-A3, AUD-A4 | ADR-20260729T053329Z · architecture.md |
| AUD-CAL | **Calibration pass against reference recordings.** Two known soft spots: the cluster spectral peak sits about half an octave low (≤12.5 Hz vs ~20 Hz published for Saturn V) and the directivity lobe is a little wide (~80° vs 45–75°). Only `acoustic_efficiency`, `slice_strouhal`, `spread_rate` may move. Needs NASA public-domain launch audio and spectrogram comparison; deliberately *not* done by ear | later | S–M | — | probe README §Validation |
| AUD-ENV | **Environment coupling.** Ground-reflection comb filtering (audibly part of a real pad launch), terrain occlusion/reverberation, and wind-borne propagation. Out of probe scope by design — this is where the audio model meets Thalos's world rather than the probe's free field | later | M | AUD-A5 | probe README |

## Track 2e — Navigation, routes, and guidance (`nav`)

Spec: [gameplay/navigation.md](gameplay/navigation.md) · seam decision:
ADR-20260730T005746Z-one-navigation-authority-displays-are-projections.

Goal: one navigation authority (`thalos_navigation` + `crate::route`) that answers
"where am I going, what path gets me there, how far off am I", with every display
a projection of it. The runway-approach case is built. LAND now grows that
authority into the first destination-level route: select any same-body runway
from any normal airborne position and finish stopped on it
(ADR-20260730T232443Z).

| ID | Item | Status | Est | Deps | Refs |
|----|------|--------|-----|------|------|
| BL-20260730T005746Z-runway-approach-guidance | **Runway approach navigation, end to end** (user 2026-07-30: the ND's runway symbology "is pretty much useless… we should revamp the ND entirely"). Old ND drew strips at a fixed symbol size (a 5 km runway as a ~240 m stub, 20× wrong) and "extended centerline" dashes of fixed plot length off an arbitrary end — no route, no vertical guidance, no alignment anywhere. **Landed:** new pure crate `thalos_navigation` (waypoints with optional vertical constraints, gnomonic route frames, line/arc paths, bank-limited Dubins CSC planner, approach planner with join-late handling, distance-to-go VNAV, pure guidance fn — 63 tests); `crate::route` publishing one `RouteState` (selection via `RouteRequest`, re-plan policy, craft-derived approach speed from its own lift curve at threshold density); ND rewritten (true-scale strips + threshold bars, real route polyline with the final segment highlighted, waypoint symbols, bearing caret, click-to-arm + `< > FLIP CLR`); PFD gains LOC/GS deviation scales + a two-axis flight director + `APPR RWY nn`. Defects fixed on contact: `Drape` runways read as elevation 0 instead of inheriting their basin's ~700 m (`StructureRegistry::site_elevation_m` — harmless on a plot, fatal for a glideslope); a zero-length path leg reported a fabricated "east" course (`Leg::Point`); the final-segment highlight was derived by comparing chord lengths to an arc length and silently never drew (now an exact index). **Geometry/symbology verified** via `just nd-preview` (8 real plans, incl. the short-final case that exposed the join-late bug). **Round 2 (user feedback 2026-07-30, flown in-game):** "a bit small and zoomed out", "no hierarchy in the ui", "I want clear visual guides to both heading and glideslope, like a dot to keep level". Three fixes: (a) **the plot never zoomed in** because AUTO framed the *whole frozen plan* — established on final it still contained the 40 km intercept, so 0.4 nmi from the threshold it sat at R 27 nmi; AUTO now frames only what is still ahead (`path_along_m` restored to answer that), the ladder gained 500 m / 1 km rungs, and there are manual zoom controls (`− / + / AUTO` plus the scroll wheel over the plot, `NavZoom`); (b) **panel rebuilt with a reading order** — header (runway · phase chip · DTG) / plot / zoom row / guidance block / selector, plot 200→240 px; (c) **a centring dot** carrying both deviations in one glyph, which is also the fix for the fact that the PFD's LOC/GS scales are **HUD-mode only** and the user flies the navball. Found while implementing it: **the PFD glideslope index was inverted** — the two axes need opposite screen signs (high → slope is below → index *down*, positive screen y), and the old test's own comment described the correct behaviour while its assertion encoded the wrong one; both mappings are now pinned. **Round 3 (user feedback 2026-07-31):** "it should follow the path, not the runway… the ball should point to the way we should point our plane… if we're off course, we should calculate a navigable route back to the course, meeting with it in a smooth way." The dot was reading the **ILS beams** (runway-relative, meaningless off final); it now reads a **route-relative steering director**, and being off course produces a *planned* rejoin instead of a heuristic nudge. New `thalos_navigation::rejoin`: search forward along the route for the earliest point the craft can meet and join it with a Dubins path, where the comfort test is **excess turning** — turning demanded beyond the unavoidable heading change — because path length rates a hard 90°-out-and-back S as respectable and total turn blames a 180°-off craft for a turn it must make either way. Guidance gains `fpa_rad` / `fpa_command_rad` / `director_lateral()` / `director_vertical()`; the ND draws the rejoin dashed beneath the route past ~150 m off track; the PFD's ILS scales now hide until established on final and its pitch cue reads the published flight-path angles instead of re-deriving γ (the duplicated derivation ADR-20260730T005746Z exists to prevent). 78 navigation + 24 widget tests. **Remaining verify: in-game wiring** — resource plumbing, widget auto-selection, click handling, zoom controls, panel chrome, the rejoin drawing, and the PFD scales are not covered by the preview (which renders the plot material only, from planned routes with no live deviation) | verify | L | **user:** `just game runway-approach` → ND shows both strips at true size; arm one (click it, or `>`), confirm the drawn route is flyable and the centring dot moves the way you must fly; zoom with `− / +` / the wheel and check `AUTO` re-frames sensibly on final; `FLIP` lands the other way. Switch to `HUD` mode for the LOC/GS scales + flight director and confirm they agree with the ND | gameplay/navigation.md · ADR-20260730T005746Z · hud_widgets.md · tooling.md §nd-preview |

Retire the maneuver autopilot's direct throttle writes and the nosewheel's raw-input-only command path in the same change. **Landed:** throttle now has persistent `commanded`, arbitrated `selected`, and fuel-gated `effective` states; maneuver/LAND/ORBIT share the mode owner; plane flight-path, ground-steer, and brake demands all cross one arbiter; gear physics no longer reads raw yaw. | BL-20260730T225319Z-ground-stance-tipover | **The Meridian flipped over in ordinary ground turns; once tipped it slid/pirouetted forever with control surfaces slamming side to side** (user 2026-07-30, screenshots from `just game runway`). Three stacked causes, all fixed. **(1) Every CoM aggregation weighted each part's mass at its *origin* — the top/nose mating node — not its centroid** (INC-20260730T225319Z): the 35 m loft's ~10 t sat at its nose tip, dragging the modeled CoM to y −9.8 m when the mass-model centroid is −16.4 m, so the *nose* gear carried 69 % of the weight and the tricycle tip axis pinched to a 0.51 m effective half-track — roll-over at **0.12 g** lateral against 0.8 g of tire grip. Fixed with mirrored `part_centroid_offset` / `live_part_centroid_offset` (half the shared cylinder-model length down the local axis; wings/gear zero, documented) used by blueprint stats + both runtime aggregations; pitch/yaw MoI dropped ~40 % as the phantom nose mass vanished (expect snappier pitch response in flight). **(2) Track was capped at hull width** (`track_fraction × fuselage radius`, max 1.0): gear_mesh now anchors each strut on the true skin point and slants it outboard to the hub (physics frames unchanged — the suspension ray is the radial through the wheel), `gear_main_hd` goes to ±2.2·radius (7.26 m track) and the mains move station 0.55 → 0.51 (just aft of the corrected CoM). Verified by the new `meridian_balance` example (`cargo run -p thalos_shipyard --example meridian_balance`): **12 % nose / 88 % mains, roll-over threshold 0.78 g ≈ the 0.8 tire μ — it now skids before it tips**; 67/67 crate tests green. **(3) The tipped "zombie" slide was SAS-driven and frictionless**: with the wheels unloaded, `wheel_torque_ground_mask` handed SAS its full roll/yaw reaction-wheel authority back and it power-slid the craft trying to right it (the saturated commands also slammed the control surfaces as the error axis swept the body frame), while hull friction damped linear velocity only — zero angular. New `HullGroundContact` (sole writer `apply_surface_friction`) zeroes **all** wheel torque when the hull is at the ground with no wheel loaded (weight-on-wheels wins when both, so a rotation tail-graze can't drop pitch assist), and hull friction became a support-point impulse with angular coupling + a spin stick/slip term (`SPIN_PATCH_ARM_M`), so a tipped craft grinds to rest. Compile-clean + clippy-clean; **capture-blocked**: the gear-stance screenshot could not be taken — the capture lane fails on another session's in-flight `gpu_grass.rs:375` `maps[3]` out-of-bounds (length 3), outside this change | verify | M | capture lane green (gpu_grass.rs breakage is another session's) | **user:** `just game runway` — taxi a hard turn (should skid, not flip); if you do tip it (or belly-land), confirm it grinds to a stop with surfaces quiet, and T toggling SAS changes nothing while tipped. Then `just screenshot runway-atmosphere` for the wide-stance visual. Watch for: pitch feel in flight (MoI −40 %), rotation authority at takeoff (nose now carries 12 %) | INC-20260730T225319Z-mass-at-origin-com · gear_mesh.rs · local_physics/{gear,ground}.rs · ships/meridian.ron |
| BL-20260730T195349Z-freecam-level-lock-apparent-size | **Freecam's level lock released far too low and switched state on a threshold** (user 2026-07-30: "deactivates too low… I want to fly aligned to the planet for higher… base it on a fraction of the planet's size taking up our view (angle independent though)… transitions should be interpolated, not an instant snap"). The old envelope was an *altitude* ceiling — the authored Kármán line on atmospheric bodies, `min(5 % of radius, 100 km)` on airless ones — with 95 % re-engage hysteresis to stop it chattering. **Reworked 2026-07-30** onto the body's **apparent angular diameter** `2·asin(R/r)`, smoothstepped from rigid at 120° to fully 6-DOF at 45°. Apparent size, not altitude: independent of view direction and lens (panning or zooming must never move the flight model under the user's hands), and a pure function of `r/R`, so one pair of constants covers the 190 km Selva and the 3186 km Thalos. Full authority now reaches **~493 km over Thalos** (was its 80 km Kármán line) and **~134 km over Mira** (was 43 km). Nothing switches: authority is smoothstepped (zero slope at both ends, so the hysteresis state machine is *deleted* rather than retuned — a continuous law cannot chatter); the pose **eases** toward level by a per-frame slerp at `2 Hz · a/(1−a)`, which diverges at full authority (rigid, identical to the old hard constraint incl. its pitch clamp) and vanishes at zero; and mouse-look's yaw axis and R/F's climb axis interpolate between the camera's own up and the local vertical by the same authority, which also collapsed the two mouse-look branches into one (pre-multiplying about `R·Y` *is* intrinsic yaw). Q/E roll returns below half authority. One latent bug fixed en route: the constraint only ran on frames where input moved the pose, which was invisible under a one-shot snap but would freeze a half-eased horizon the instant the user let go — the "freecam owns this pose" decision is now latched on `FreeCam` instead of re-derived per frame. 16 unit tests pass, incl. scale-invariance across a 16× radius ratio, no-step monotonicity across the band, frame-rate independence of the ease against the continuous law, and idempotence at *every* weight. **Numbers are one constant each** (`LEVEL_LOCK_FULL_ANGLE_RAD` / `LEVEL_LOCK_RELEASE_ANGLE_RAD` / `LEVEL_LOCK_RATE_HZ`) — feel is only judgeable in a play session | verify | S | **user:** `just game` → F4 freecam, climb out over Thalos and Mira — is 493 km the right place for the lock to start loosening, and does the release read as a fade rather than a switch? | freecam/mod.rs `level_lock_authority` · gameplay/input.md §Freecam |
| BL-20260730T184738Z-godray-site-not-latched | **The `cloud-godray` site search re-runs every warmup frame, while every sibling preset latches its site once.** `ScreenshotDriver` caches `dry_site_dir` / `forest_site_dir` / `ocean_site_dir` / `coast_site_dir` — each documented as "same latching" — but `ScreenshotPreset::CloudGodray` calls `cloud_godray_site_context` straight from the per-frame pose path (screenshot.rs:3290) with no cache. Two consequences: (a) a full daylight-hemisphere search for broken cloud over land, every frame, for the whole 60-frame settle plus any stream/brake hold; (b) the site is free to *move* between warmup frames as the cloud field evolves, so the framing a shot lands on is not the one the search first chose — the determinism the other four latches exist to guarantee. Found via `just diag`: `screenshot·godray_site_selected` reached **8,356 records = 73 % of the whole lane** in a 2 h window, i.e. one emit per frame. Fix is the same shape as its siblings — a `godray_site: Option<...>` latch on the driver, cleared with the others on request arm (the epoch-change reset at the `driver.dry_site_dir = None` block). **Not folded into the 2026-07-30 receipt change because it moves what the preset frames**: it needs a before/after `cloud-godray` capture to confirm the latched site is the one we want, which is a verification cycle of its own | next | XS | — | screenshot.rs:3290 · `cloud_godray_site_context` · triage 2026-07-30 |

Reading the override map instead also keeps the change to two small edits in a file that was under a large concurrent edit all session. Verified: `THALOS_DEBUG_VIEW=1 THALOS_SCREENSHOT_HUD=1 just screenshot forest-stand` now renders the F3 view through the **resident** host, which queues behind a peer instead of failing outright the way the cold-host path does | done | S | — | tooling.md · perf/overlay.rs · screenshot.rs `CAPTURE_OVERRIDE_KEYS` |
| BL-20260730T180229Z-capture-receipt-tile-residency | **A capture receipt cannot tell you whether the terrain in the PNG was braked.** Headless capture deliberately runs a **2 GiB** machine-wide tile allowance (`DEFAULT_CAPTURE_TILE_BUDGET_MB`, capture.md §Automation defaults) — half the 4 GiB NTR-X13 chose precisely "so the brake cannot silently coarsen a capture-verified framing". Diag triage 2026-07-30 caught the collision: capture host session `23384-1785380595334` on `cloud-godray` transiently desired **7,911 tiles (~2.6 GiB)** against the 2,048 MiB share, the brake hit the **`MIN_SPLIT_SCALE` floor of 0.333** and retired **4,846** tiles in one gauge tick (03:03:57Z), recovering to 1.0 by 03:04:07Z and settling at 2,169 tiles / 735 MiB. **Two shots landed inside that 25 s window** (03:03:52 `restart_incompatible_scene`, 03:03:55 `reuse`) and neither `cloud_godray.png`'s `.capture.json` nor any other records residency at all — `graphics` carries only `clouds`/`grass`. So `just diag`'s own advice ("captures taken while braked may show coarser terrain than authored") is unactionable: an agent cannot map a finding onto a file. **Close it by** adding `split_scale` (at readback **and** the session min since boot), `tile_resident`, and `desired` to the receipt, and treating a readback-time brake as a validation failure the way a degraded target body already is (INC-20260724T182643Z) — a shot that renders coarser than authored is the canonical BL-20 non-crash failure. Then decide the second half: whether 2 GiB is right for a lane whose whole job is authored-fidelity evidence, or whether the preset's transient over-request (3.6× its settled working set) is itself the defect. **Landed 2026-07-30, and the fix is the cause not the symptom: a braked frame is no longer read back in the first place.** The readback now holds for the brake to release (`BRAKE_RECOVER_TIMEOUT_S = 45 s`, three of the ~15 s recovery actually measured), same shape as the existing massif stream hold — bounded wait, then warn and capture anyway rather than hang the host. Whatever the outcome, a `terrain` block rides in the receipt beside the PNG: `split_scale` at readback, `worst_split_scale` over the whole request, resident/desired tiles, resident MiB, the *share* and the `instances` it was divided across, and `brake_wait_s`. `CaptureTerrainResidency` travels on `CaptureResponse`; absent block = the legacy udlod path, which is "not applicable", not "fine". The client prints a loud stderr warning naming the numbers, the tool lane records `tile_split_scale`/`brake_wait_s`/`terrain_braked_count`, and a new `capture_terrain_braked` diag check names the *preset* — closing the actual gap, which was that no reader could map a braked session onto a file. **Verified end to end on real captures, both branches:** a normal `cloud-godray` shot writes `split_scale 1.0, brake_wait_s 0`, and the same preset forced to a 256 MiB budget writes `split_scale 0.333, brake_wait_s 45.0`, logs `terrain_braked_count=1`, and `just diag 2` reports it by name. **What the instrument immediately showed, and the reason the second half is still a real decision:** an unbraked `cloud-godray` reads **5,572 resident of 7,830 desired at 1,888 MiB against a 2,048 MiB share — 92 %**. It is not braking with room to spare; it is sitting on the line, which is why it was the preset that broke first. Two open questions the numbers now support: raise the capture allowance, and whether a 40 %-unmet desire at readback means non-massif presets should also gate on `settled()` (that would lengthen every shot, so it is a scope call, not a nudge) | verify | S | **user:** decide whether headless capture's 2 GiB allowance is right for a lane whose job is authored-fidelity evidence — the receipt now shows how close each preset runs to it | tiles/mod.rs `residency_budget_bytes` · screenshot.rs `BRAKE_RECOVER_TIMEOUT_S` · capture.md §6.1 · NTR-X13 · INC-20260725T012104Z |
| BL-20260730T180230Z-preset-catalog-compile-gate | **The two preset catalogs are held together by a runtime `debug_assert` in the capture host, so a divergence costs a host boot per shot to find.** `ScreenshotPreset::ALL`/`name()` (screenshot.rs) and `CAPTURE_PRESETS` (capture protocol) are hand-maintained twins; the only enforcement is `debug_assert_eq!` in `compatible_presets` (screenshot.rs:2353). Diag triage 2026-07-30: **6 of 37 shots in the window died on exactly that assert** ("capture protocol and runtime preset catalogs diverged", 00:53–01:20Z, presets `interstage`/`reentry`/`orbit-hull`), each one paying a full host boot before panicking on a compute task pool thread; the two `silent_sessions` in the same window are those hosts, dead before writing anything but `session_start`. Both lists agree at HEAD (34 entries, same order), so **this is not an open defect — it is a defect class**: `just check` and `just clippy` are both green while the capture lane is unusable. **Close it by** making the divergence a compile or `cargo test` failure — generate one list from the other (a macro over the enum, or `CAPTURE_PRESETS` derived from `ScreenshotPreset::ALL`), or failing that a `#[test]` in the runtime crate that asserts the equality `compatible_presets` currently checks at runtime. Deleting the twin outright is the better outcome if the protocol crate can depend on the name table. **Landed 2026-07-30: the twin is deleted, not asserted over.** `capture_preset_catalog!` in the protocol crate is now the one declaration — a callback macro handing `Variant => "wire-name"` pairs to a caller-supplied receiver. The protocol expands it into `CAPTURE_PRESETS`; the runtime expands the same list into `ScreenshotPreset::{ALL, name, from_canonical_name}`. Order and contents are identical by construction, so the `debug_assert_eq!` is gone. **Gate proven to fail on the real defect, both directions** (`cargo check -p thalos_runtime`): dropping `OrbitHull => "orbit-hull"` from the catalog → `E0004 non-exhaustive patterns: ScreenshotPreset::OrbitHull not covered`; adding an entry for a variant that does not exist → `E0599 no variant … named LagrangeStation`. Three backstop tests pin the properties the gate buys (catalogs are one list, names unique, every canonical name round-trips `try_parse` — the last one catches an alias arm shadowing a canonical name at the wrong variant, which the generated fallback would never see). `try_parse`'s hand-written table is now aliases-only in effect: an unmatched input falls through to the generated canonical parse, so a preset added without an alias arm still resolves under its own name. Clippy + check clean on both crates; 3/3 tests pass. **Known gap, not worth a row:** `just test` runs only `-p thalos_physics_canonical`, so those tests are a `cargo test -p thalos_runtime` backstop — the compile gate is what `just check` enforces, and it is the load-bearing half | done | S | — | screenshot.rs `screenshot_preset_catalog!` · crates/capture/protocol/src/lib.rs `capture_preset_catalog!` · capture.md §3.1 · triage 2026-07-30 |
| BL-20260730T180231Z-diag-slow-frames-idle-host | **`slow_frames` reads the capture host's idle poll as stutter, and will fire on every triage.** `frame_gauge` p95 is compared against `SLOW_FRAME_MS` with no notion of session role (checks.rs:709), but a persistent capture host between shots ticks at `IDLE_CAPTURE_POLL_INTERVAL = 100 ms` (screenshot.rs:2448) — i.e. ~10 fps and ~100 ms p95 **by design**. Triage 2026-07-30 flagged session `5048-1785377784417` as ATTENTION with "90 × 2 s windows below 30 fps, worst p95 119 ms"; the timeline is 58.8 fps / 19 ms p95 while shooting, then a flat 9.2 fps for 19 minutes of idling. 90 of its 93 gauges are the sleep. Since capture hosts are the most common runtime session in the tree, this check currently costs a dismissal every day and buries a real stutter when one appears — the § 4 "wrong threshold or dead check" case. **Close it by** giving the perf lane a role/active marker the check can read (`persistent_render_activity` already brackets the active spans, or `session_start.role`) and excluding idle host windows, then testing it fires on a real stutter session and stays silent on an idle host. **Landed 2026-07-30:** `frame_health` builds per-session `render_active` transitions from `persistent_render_activity` and judges a `frame_gauge`/`spike` only when the host was actually rendering at that timestamp. A session with no markers (an ordinary game session) is unaffected and judged on every frame; frames *before* a host's first request are excluded too, since that is the world boot the tool lane already measures as `phase_host_start_ms`. The finding now carries the excluded count as its denominator, so the exclusion is never silent. Four tests: idle host silent, same host stuttering *while rendering* still reported (the exclusion must not blind the check to capture latency), plain game session judged on every frame, boot frames excluded. **The fix revealed a real signal the noise was hiding** — the check reports only its worst session, so the 90-window idle host was outranking session `16392-1785368520987`, a genuine interactive session with 4,000 resident tiles swinging 8.6–78 fps and 71 windows under 30. That is now what `just diag` names | done | XS | — | tools/diag/src/checks.rs `render_activity_transitions` · screenshot.rs `IDLE_CAPTURE_POLL_INTERVAL` · triage 2026-07-30 |

| BL-20260729T040504Z-unified-diagnostics | **One diagnostics interface, and instrument the developer tools with it.** Observability is currently three unconnected shapes: the game's `thalos::diagnostic::*` → `runtime.jsonl` lane (good, but its contract/sink/rotation/session-id live *inside* `crates/runtime/game`), ~25 per-preset capture JSONLs plus the `.capture.json` receipts, and the offline tools (`tools/capture`, `capture_host`, `terrain_baker`, `texgen`, `world_map`) which have no lane at all — `eprintln!` prose and exit codes. Consequence: the surface agents depend on most, the headless capture lane, is the least observable thing in the repo, so its latency and flakiness are argued about from memory instead of measured. **Shape:** (1) extract a no-Bevy `thalos_diagnostics` crate owning the event contract (target namespace, schema, session id, unit-suffixed flat fields), the JSONL sink, and `artifact_paths`' resolution/rotation/prune, with a one-call install for *any* process (game via the Bevy layer, tools via plain `tracing`); (2) instrument the capture client + host on it — per-request phase timings (lock wait, host reuse vs restart, rebuild + its reason, scene settle, render, encode), outcome, source fingerprint, retries — so "is the capture lane fast and stable right now?" is a file, not a feeling; (3) give the offline tools (bake, texgen, map, preview) the same run record; (4) one reader shared by `perf-report` and any future report tool, plus an agent-facing query entry point (`just diag <session|latest> [--target …]`) so a session's events can be read without hand-rolling `jq`; (5) fold the per-preset capture JSONLs into the shared stream keyed by preset, keeping only sinks with a genuinely independent schema. **Acceptance:** a capture run's timing/outcome history is queryable across runs; a tool failure is diagnosable from its record alone; no new `println!` numerics anywhere. Deliberately *not* in scope: a remote-inspection channel, a metrics daemon, or anything that runs while the game isn't. **Steps 1–2 landed 2026-07-29:** `thalos_diagnostics` (`crates/foundation/diagnostics`) owns the contract/sink/session/paths/rotation; `crates/runtime/game/src/artifact_paths.rs` is deleted and `runtime_diagnostics.rs` is now only the Bevy adapter (layer + human console filter), so the game stream is byte-identical while the same layer installs in a plain `tracing` registry. `ToolRun` (`tool_run_start` / `tool_run`, phases and counters flattened to `phase_*_ms` / `*_count`) is wired through the capture client: per-shot records incl. batch scenes and compare variants, `host_action` classified by the *same* function `ensure_server` branches on (so the record cannot drift from the branch), phase timings for source-snapshot / host-start / shader-reload / render / validate, retry + rebuild-recovery counters, and a separate `capture_lock` event for machine-lock contention. 5 diagnostics tests + 23 capture tests pass. **Verified end-to-end on a real failing run**: `phase_host_start_ms=30594, host_action=restart_stale_source, outcome=error, error="capture launcher exited"` — i.e. the record already attributed 30.6 s of wasted agent time to a failed rebuild, which is the class of question that previously needed a log-reading session. **Step 4 landed 2026-07-29 (the reader):** `thalos_diagnostics::reader` (schema-gated, rotation-aware, session/role-grouped — roles exist so a silent `capture status` is not confused with a game that died at boot) + **`just diag [hours]`** (`tools/diag`), which reports only what crossed a threshold and prints nothing on a healthy window. 14 checks; thresholds are named constants with rationale in `finding.rs`, tested both ways (silent on a healthy window, firing on each defect). Paired with the **`diag-triage` skill** for the daily pass and CLAUDE.md rules making every new event owe a check. **First real run paid for itself**: `lane_noise` showed `vegetation·drive_gauge` was **60 % of every record in the lane** — a leftover investigation trace at ~1.5 s cadence that no check read, now behind `THALOS_VEG_DIAG=1`; and it surfaced three live runtime signals for the user (tile brake down to split scale **0.38**, **3.8 GiB** tile+slab growth in one session, 31 × 2 s windows below 30 fps). **Remaining:** host-internal render split (settle vs. warmup vs. readback — `phase_render_ms` is the client's outside view), run records for `bake`/`texgen`/`map`/`preview`, folding the ~25 per-preset capture JSONLs into the stream, and moving `perf-report` onto the shared reader | wip | M–L | — | CLAUDE.md §Observability · tooling.md §Runtime logging contract · runtime_diagnostics.rs · artifact_paths.rs · tools/capture · BL-20 (exit 0 under a fatal pipeline error) · INC-20260725T012104Z (a counter that couldn't falsify) |
| BL-20260729T070928Z-classify-launcher-exit | **`capture launcher exited` is a bucket, not a classifier — make the lane say *why* a host died at boot.** Triage 2026-07-29 (24 h): 16 of 58 shots failed (28 %), and **13 of them recorded exactly the string `capture launcher exited` with no further field** — `tools.jsonl` carries `phase_host_start_ms` / `host_action` / `host_start_count` but nothing that separates *this workspace does not compile* from *the host panicked at boot* from *it exited silently* (NTR-X9's `mira-disc` signature). `ToolRun::fail` records the first line by design ("the classifier belongs here", `crates/foundation/diagnostics/src/run.rs:104`) and the capture client passes it a bucket: `main.rs:1217` returns `format!("capture launcher exited\n{log}")`, so the whole diagnosis stays in the console the agent no longer has. The pattern to copy already exists two lines up — `resource_fault_kind(&log)` promotes an OOM/device-loss/timeout classification *into* the message, which is why the one OOM in this window was self-explaining. **Do:** classify the launcher log the same way (`rustc`/`cargo` build failure incl. the first error code, host panic + payload, `toolchain_corruption` — which is already a written classifier used only for self-heal — silent exit with byte count, missing asset/package), make that classification the first line, and record it as a typed `launcher_exit_kind` field beside the existing phase timings. Then a `capture_failures` finding can group by kind instead of by bucket. **Landed 2026-07-29:** `classify_launcher_exit` returns a stable `kind` + an identifying `detail`, ordered so a cause cannot be swallowed by a symptom that always follows it — `toolchain corruption` outranks the `could not compile` it produces (INC-20260724T182642Z), and a `capture host panic` outranks the `process didn't exit successfully (exit code: 3)` cargo always prints after it. Kinds: `workspace build failure` (crate + `error[E….]`), `capture host panic` (location + payload line), `capture host aborted`, `toolchain corruption`, `silent exit` (NTR-X9's signature — distinguished from), `unclassified launcher exit` (an explicit gap in the classifier). The kind is the failure reason's first line, so it is what `ToolRun::fail` records and what triage groups by; `launcher_exit_kind` / `launcher_exit_detail` ride alongside as typed fields. `tools/diag` prints one example payload under each cause. Every classifier case is a verbatim excerpt of the real `visual_capture_server.log`; capture 29/29 + diag 6/6 pass, clippy clean. **Remaining verify:** no launcher exit has occurred since the change, so the classification is proven against recorded log text, not yet against a live failure — the next real one should report a kind rather than a bucket, and any `unclassified launcher exit` in a future window is a signature to add | verify | S | — | CLAUDE.md §Observability · tooling.md §capture record · tools/capture/src/main.rs `classify_launcher_exit` · diagnostics/src/run.rs:104 · BL-20260729T040504Z-unified-diagnostics · NTR-X9 |
| BL-20260729T070929Z-memory-growth-check-boot-ramp | **`memory_growth` measures peak − *first sample*, so it fires on every boot ramp and cannot see the accumulation it exists for.** `tools/diag/src/checks.rs:442-467` takes the first `frame_gauge` in a session as the baseline; that sample is taken before terrain has streamed, so a healthy session that fills its working set and then holds flat reports its whole steady state as "growth". Measured on the session this check flagged today (`27824-1785299535941`, 86 gauges over 19 min): `tile+slab` = 797 MiB at the first sample, peaked at **4623 MiB 33 s in**, then settled to **2419 MiB (735 tile + 1684 slab) and stayed byte-identical for the remaining 18 minutes** — the reported "3827 MiB grew within one session" is a boot spike the tile brake reclaimed, i.e. a control loop working, reported as an ATTENTION. That is the wrong-threshold/dead-check case CLAUDE.md says to tune or delete, and worse than noise here: the check was built for INC-20260725T012104Z (tiles inside budget while the process climbs), and a metric that cannot distinguish a ramp from a leak would not fire on that defect any more loudly than on a healthy boot. **Do:** measure *sustained* growth — e.g. peak minus the median/quantile of the post-warmup tail, or a monotone-trend test over a trailing window — and keep reporting the `tile_mib` / `slab_mib` split, which is the actual diagnosis. Tier axis pinned it: near-only rendered clean terrain+haze, so the whole wash was the far cloud overlay — its fill-response LUT is an *expected-opacity* curve, so at footprints where cells are strongly resolved (150 m/px vs 1–5 km cells) every cell rendered ~0.5 translucent and every gap ~0.2 hazy: rendering the mean, the standing ADR-20260725T222409Z violation, plus foreshortening compressing cells into streaks. **Fixed 2026-07-29 by extending the derived fill calibration** (never hand-tuned, honoring BL-20260723T214730Z): `derive_fill_calibration` now also emits per-strata-mean `far_cell_edge` (the measured cell distribution's 1−q quantile, q = occupied column fraction) and `far_cell_solid` (E[alpha | occupied]); the composite renders `(cell > edge) · solid` per chord segment — equal to `fill_response` in expectation BY CONSTRUCTION — relaxing to the smooth response as cells go sub-pixel (`cloud_far_ownership`). Grazing chords additionally aggregate per segment (front-to-back over the 6 band-march samples) instead of one chord-mean → one LUT → one anchor. `FILL_LUT_VERSION` 8→9. Capture-verified at `tex_look` (broken masses, terrain in gaps to the horizon); regression-checked `cloud-limb` (resolved cells, no blanket) + `cloud-planet` (unchanged by construction, ownership=1). Bonus: cloud lane GPU at this 4K framing 27 ms → 6.7 ms mean (the lace fix's frontier break). ## Track 4 — Craft damage and destruction (`dmg`)

Goal ([damage.md](simulation/damage.md), ADR-20260730T003005Z): graded, survivable
BeamNG-flavoured damage where a part's **function** fails before its **structure**,
built on the *current* aggregate rigid body — node-beam (physics.md §7 Phase 3)
later becomes a better *producer* of the same canonical damage state, not a
prerequisite. Damage persists across flights. Slice IDs are owned by
[damage.md](simulation/damage.md) §9; `dmg §N` = that doc's sections.

| ID | Item | Status | Est | Deps | Refs |
|----|------|--------|-----|------|------|
| DMG-1 | **Canonical damage state:** `VesselDamage` in `VesselRecord` (`physics_canonical`, Bevy-free, serde) — per-part `integrity` + per-`PartFunction` `condition`/`failed`, per-joint `yielded`/`severed`; `PartKey` = `ShipBlueprint::parts` index, `JointKey` = the child part's key (one parent per part, so no second index) and **never re-indexed** across a cut; `is_destroyed()` re-derived from the state behind its existing accessor so `control_bus`/`fuel`/`bridge`/`scenario_menu` are untouched. No behaviour change beyond the derived verdict. Verify: pure tests (key stability across a graph cut, save/load round-trip exact, destroyed verdict matches the old tolerance on a whole-craft impact) | next | M | — | dmg §2 · ADR-20260730T003005Z |
| DMG-2 | **Per-panel aero** — the prerequisite for every asymmetric failure, and the riskiest slice, so it goes first among the consequence work. `evaluate_aero` becomes a panel sum over the already-per-panel `WingAeroPanel` geometry (`center_body_m`/`fore_dir`/`thick_dir`/`span_dir`, [stats.rs:577](../crates/domain/construction/src/stats.rs:577)): local flow `v + ω×r` per panel, own α from own basis, `F` and `r×F` accumulated; fuselage keeps a whole-body bluff residual. Asymmetric roll+yaw, roll damping, and roll authority all become **emergent geometry** instead of tuned coefficients. A Bevy-free panel array type is declared in `physics_canonical::aero` (glam) since `WingAeroPanel` lives in the Bevy-dependent `thalos_shipyard`. **Gate:** a parity test at the Meridian cruise point (CL/CD/Cm within a stated band, trim α within a stated angle) — the whole-body model survives only as that fixture, never as a runtime path. Expect a real retune of `cruise`/`runway`/`runway-approach` feel | next | L | — | dmg §4 · aerodynamics.md |
| DMG-3 | **Load channels + catalog limits** (the function-before-structure mechanism): each part spec declares a structural limit per channel and a *fraction* of it per declared function — contact impulse, sustained g-load (`a + ω×(ω×r)` at the part's station), aero load vs. panel limit, dynamic pressure. Failure is deterministic + a seeded `(craft_id, part_key, function)` offset, **never runtime RNG**. Load events stage per epoch and **commit once at interval close** so the fleet's replay-to-earliest-contact cannot double-apply plastic accumulation — the subtlest correctness requirement here, and it gets its own test. Verify: table-driven test over the catalog that every part declaring both limits fails its function first; idempotency-across-replay test | next | M–L | DMG-1 | dmg §3 |
| DMG-4 | **`rebuild_damaged_aggregates(craft_id)`** — the one consequence path every trigger (impact, sever, function failure, repair) routes through, recomputing derived craft from (blueprint ∪ damage): panel-sum aero with per-panel integrity + control-surface condition (a jammed surface = zero authority + fixed offset deflection), mass/CoM/inertia via the existing `recompute_ship_inertia`, `EngineThrust` gated by `Thrust` condition, `ResourceContainment` leak rate out of `PartResources`, control authority through the rebuilt config so `thalos_control` needs no damage-specific code. **No consumer reads `VesselDamage` to decide flight behaviour** — that is what makes node-beam a producer swap | next | M | DMG-2, DMG-3 | dmg §5 |
| DMG-5 | **Structural sever → debris:** a severed joint fires the landed separation transaction (`materialize_separated_vessels`, [staging.rs:438](../crates/runtime/game/src/staging.rs:438)) with a failure impulse in place of the decoupler's authored one; the detached assembly becomes a canonical vessel per ADR-20260724T230226Z (falls, collides, persists through warp, appears in map view). No debris-only path. Retires vessels.md §7's per-part-breakup deferral | next | S–M | DMG-1 | dmg §6 · vessels.md §3 |
| DMG-6 | **Damage read without art:** effects first (engine smoke, venting fuel, contact sparks/scrape — art-free and the most legible per token), then parametric deformation on the procedural meshes (a yielded joint kinks the child's generated geometry: wing dihedral/incidence offset + break-station taper, fuselage station-local radius perturbation — no rig, no skin weights), plus a HUD damage surface naming what failed. Verify: `just preview` / a damaged-craft screenshot preset | next | M | DMG-4 | dmg §7 |
| DMG-7 | **Repair scopes:** full repair at a base with the relevant facility, nothing ambient or in flight; `scenario_menu` already closes when `is_destroyed()` clears, so the hook exists. Damage persists across flights until repaired | later | S | DMG-4 | dmg §8 |

## Track 5 — Cinematics: director, replay, and scripted control (`cine`)

Goal (`cine §1`, ADR-20260730T212556Z): **one authored sequence document, sampled
at any granularity** — a still at time *t*, a high-resolution image per keyframe,
or every frame at `n/fps` muxed to video. Output kind and resolution are request
parameters, never document identity, so directing a trailer and screenshotting a
keyframe are the same act. Scoped with the user 2026-07-30: **agent-first**
(format before editor), **recorded state** not replayed input, FFmpeg assumed
installed, CI a later consumer that the tool adapts to rather than is shaped by.

This widens `CAP-4` from "render a camera track" to "author, replay, script";
`CAP-4` is superseded by `CINE-C` and the rows below.

| ID | Item | Status | Est | Deps | Refs |
|----|------|--------|-----|------|------|
| CINE-A | **Driven simulation clock — the foundation everything else waits on.** `sync_sim_clock` publishes a wall-clock delta and Avian's `Time<Physics>` follows `Time<Virtual>`, so frame *n* cannot be rendered at *n*/fps and *nothing* is reproducible frame-to-frame. Give `SimClock` a `Wall` / `Driven { dt }` mode and have its existing sole-writer system advance `Time<Physics>` by the same dt — no new authority, no second clock; this cashes in the single-writer invariant that resource already carries. Independently valuable: today's still warmup is wall-clock, so a busy machine settles a preset differently than an idle one. **Landed 2026-07-30, opt-in.** `SimClockDrive` (`Wall` / `Driven { dt_s }`) drives Bevy's own `TimeUpdateStrategy::ManualDuration`, so `Time<Real>` → `Time<Virtual>` → Avian's `Time<Physics>`/`Time<Fixed>` *and* every presentation animation become driven together — a seam that only fixed `SimClock` would have left local physics on wall time. Headless opt-in `THALOS_CAPTURE_CLOCK=wall\|driven\|driven:<fps>` is a boot property in `STARTUP_OVERRIDE_KEYS` (host restarts on change); the choice is recorded in every `.capture.json` as a `clock` block. **The riskiest half was the inverse:** three genuinely wall-clock measurements rode `Time<Real>` and would have silently reinterpreted their ceilings as frame counts (the 180 s massif stream hold → 10 800 frames, the 45 s brake wait → 2 700) — those plus the perf lane's `cpu_ms` moved onto `Instant`, which is more correct regardless. Verified on GPU: driven and wall captures of `spaceport-aerial` are visually identical with identical framing; the receipt distinguishes them; a squeezed-budget `cloud-godray` drove `brake_wait_s` to its 45 s ceiling inside 78 s total wall time, which frame-counting could not do. Default stays `wall` — flipping the capture lane's default changes 34 presets' warmup world-time and is its own decision, taken when `CINE-C` needs it | done | S–M | — | cine §3 · ADR-20260730T212556Z · capture.md §6.2 · sim_clock.rs |
| CINE-B | **Sequence document + director evaluator + keyframe stills — the agent's loop, and where the schema settles.** Versioned document in `thalos_capture_protocol` beside `Viewpoint` (scene / actors / ordered shots); camera as **three independently anchored tracks** — position (`BodyFixed`/`BodyInertial`/`ActorLocal`), aim (`Keyframed`/`LookAt`), optics — interpolated in the anchor's own frame in `f64`, with **anchor changes legal only at a cut**. One pure `t → (pose, optics)` evaluator shared by the in-game preview and the headless renderer. CLI: `--at <t>` and `--keyframes` produce settled stills at arbitrary extent through the existing still path. No UI, no encoder, no video. Verify: an agent-authored sequence renders its keys; a `viewpoints.json` entry promotes to a one-key shot and matches its own `just screenshot` | next | M–L | CINE-A | cine §4–§5 · ADR-20260730T212556Z · capture/protocol · capture.md §5 |
| CINE-C | **Deterministic frame sequence + per-frame hash + FFmpeg mux** (absorbs `CAP-4`). Every frame is a *settled still* that happens to sit at `n/fps` — same readiness gates, residency brake, and BL-20 pipeline validation as a `just screenshot`, never a real-time path that would record LOD pop as a real artifact. Readback ring + background lossless writer; resumable/validated sequences; external FFmpeg mux after rendering (never in the render loop — ADR-20260721T194629Z). **The per-frame hash ships with the writer**, because it is the instrument that finds the remaining non-determinism (tile-stream order, temporal cloud history, unseeded RNG) — no reproducibility is claimed before it reports. Plus the offline quality tier (supersample, raised tile budget, longer settle) as an extension of typed `CaptureGraphicsOverrides`, not a second settings path | later | L | CINE-B | cine §7 · capture.md §5–§7 · ADR-20260721T194629Z |
| CINE-D | **In-game recording + timeline UI.** Fly the take → sample a pose track → scrub, cut, set framing and optics per key → write the same document `CINE-B` defines. Deliberately after `CINE-B` so the editor targets a settled schema. Native Bevy UI via `thalos_ui` (no egui in player views); F9's quick-viewpoint flow is the model for "one keypress appends a key" | later | L | CINE-B | cine §4, §6, §8 · ui_flow.md · BL-20260726T211014Z-f9-quick-viewpoint |
| CINE-E0 | **Scripted-actor control prerequisite — shared, do not implement twice.** Satisfied by `BL-20260730T232443Z-autoflight-control-authority`: canonical throttle, explicit plane targets, ground control, and one autoflight owner are now the shared seam; cinematics adds no second throttle path | done | — | BL-20260730T232443Z-autoflight-control-authority (verify) | cine §6 · simulation/control.md · ADR-20260730T232443Z |
| CINE-E | **Scripted actors + a waypoint route kind.** `DemandSource::Script` feeding attitude *and* throttle from a program (waypoints, speed/altitude targets, timed events), driven through `thalos_navigation`'s pure guidance — the extension ADR-20260730T005746Z explicitly anticipates ("a new route kind extends `thalos_navigation` in one place"; "the same guidance drives a flight director, an autopilot, or nothing at all"). Routes through the existing nav/route/autopilot authorities; **does not fork them**. Verify: a scripted approach flies the same path the ND draws | later | M–L | CINE-E0, CINE-B | cine §6 · ADR-20260730T005746Z · navigation crate |
